# Researcher Response 2

## A raw term can realize at most one separator sign at each seam

Consider any of the unary seam-signal labels of item 32 with the hypotheses of round 6, Response 10. For a seam $i$ and a sign $s$, let $W_{i,s}$ be the set of literal erasures of **all** weak 3-terms over the corresponding one-colour strict label. Allow every choice of weak endpoints. Then
$$
                         W_{i,-}\cap W_{i,+}=\varnothing .
$$
For the usual identity erasures, the nerve of these $2k$ sets is exactly the boundary of the $k$-dimensional cross-polytope. This holds even when the seam graph is acyclic and the geometric fibre is contractible. For the six-seam ring, changing all the weak representatives of two opposite facets still cannot supply a common raw 3-term for their equator. Round 6, Response 15 established the obstruction for prescribed padded endpoints. [Seam and colour hypotheses](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-10)

**The two gaps.** The separation assertion has a stronger form. On either side adjoin any finite list of fresh unary separator variables $h:p\Rightarrow p$ on seam wires. Fix all old variables and substitute any old weak term of the indicated type for each separator. The two substitutions may use different terms, including terms with nonempty strict loop words. Suppose two 3-terms have literally equal substituted syntax. On a seam where their common old event list has one designated deletion $a$ and one insertion $b$, the following observation applies. In one projection a persistent separator cannot go from before the consumed occurrence to after the produced occurrence while a persistent separator in the other projection goes from after the consumed occurrence to before the produced occurrence. The two named separators can be different.

To prove this, decode the pair into one term in an extension of the old context by fresh **2-variables only**. A pair of old variables must be the same variable. Two coherence heads must have the same pasting arity and generic type, because substitution changes just their argument lists. Decode the paired arguments recursively. In every remaining case a fresh variable occurs on at least one side. Use a joint variable for that ordered pair, reusing it on every occurrence. Its type is the literal loop type on the same wire on both sides, since the maps fix every term below dimension two. This includes pairing a fresh variable with a compound term or with an old loop variable. Only finitely many joint variables occur for a given pair. Project each joint variable back to its defining pair.

For typing, one may first take all such joint variables and then restrict to the finite support of the decoded term. Decoding reverses the two projections on joint syntax by structural induction. Thus equality of both projections reflects equality of joint terms and, entry by entry, of arrow types. In a coherence substitution, induct over its declarations. The decoded argument's inferred type and its required type after the already decoded prefix have the same two projections, so they agree. This gives a well-typed term with exactly the two given projections. The old 3-generators retain their old attaching terms in the joint context.

Serialize the strict image of this one term, and follow the selected seam. Its joint 2-generators are unary. Each projects to an ordinary word on that wire, possibly empty. Indeed the outer 1-path of such a generator is the single edge $p$ and the lower generators act on individual lanes. In this serialization distinguish the occurrence $d$ actually consumed by $a$ and the occurrence $e$ actually created by $b$. Removing the former in the initial word and the latter in the final word leaves the *same ordered word* $P$. All other events in the specified history act off this lane or read it without changing it. Write $g_d,g_e$ for the gaps of $P$ at the two named ports. Substitution into either projection replaces each letter of $P$ by a word. It induces a nondecreasing map on gaps, given by the lengths of the expanded prefixes. The designated ports are preserved because $a,b$ and their faces are old. A persistent separator strictly between the two projected gaps forces the same strict ordering of the two original gaps. Two opposite strict orders are impossible. Serializing first is legitimate for every strict composite by interchange. One serialization suffices because each coloured label specifies which side of its persistent separator contains each named port.

The proof permits arbitrary inactive composites acting identically on this seam, arbitrary unchanged flanking words, and repeated letters identical to the changing letters. Powers of inactive loops may have any finite exponent. They lengthen $P$ or a projected block and preserve monotonicity. The hypothesis concerning $a,b$ concerns the actual changing occurrences on this seam. The result imposes no direction on a comparison whose other inputs themselves move a designated port.

For the nerve, any compatible nonempty sign assignment is realized. Take the single multicolour context for that assignment and a strict history respecting it, as in round 6, Responses 9–10. Strict surjectivity gives a weak term over this label. Erase all colours except each requested one in turn. The resulting terms have the specified one-colour labels and their final erasures are literally equal by composition of substitutions. Together with opposite-sign separation, this gives precisely the simplices that select at most one vertex from each of $k$ pairs. Even a higher term common to two opposite erasures over these labels would have a common erased 3-face, which the gap argument excludes.

**Reproduction.** The inverse-substitution solver enumerates every preimage of a given finite raw term. At each node it tries each variable with that image and, for a coherence, the same head with recursively lifted arguments. It checks each dependent argument type against the prefix already chosen. These are all the cases of the syntax, and recursion is on proper subterms. On the actual six-seam context the solver found four lifts of a padded $a_2$, four of its weak identity, and five of a two-letter loop under nested substitution choices. A separate search fired the old deletion and insertion rules in both orders, at every gap of each persistent word of length zero through three over six paired letters. Its 3,805 gap comparisons realized all seven possible weak sign pairs and neither conflicting pair. The unbounded assertion follows from the two monotone prefix maps. [Solver and certificate generator](../../../tools/relative_coherence_seam_intersections.py)

# Researcher Response 3

## The homotopy type of every intersection of signed seam facets

Let $K$ be the family of hidden sets allowed by the temporal order in item 32. In the actual chart complex for the strict label, intersect any collection of signed geometric hemispheres. Let $D$ be the seams requested with **both** signs, $A$ those requested with exactly one, and $U$ the unrequested seams. The intersection is empty precisely when $D\notin K$. Otherwise its homotopy type is
$$
                    Z_{K'},\qquad K'=\{T\subseteq U:D\cup T\in K\}.
$$
Here $Z_{K'}$ is the union of closed faces of $[-1,1]^U$ with free-coordinate set in $K'$. Retain a coordinate even when its singleton is forbidden. It then contributes two discrete endpoints. Thus the formula computes disconnected intersections as well.

In a chart in the intersection, every seam of $D$ is hidden, and any visible sign on $A$ agrees with its request. Map the chart to its visible-sign face on $U$. This lands in $Z_{K'}$ because the tier order hides $D$ together with every hidden coordinate of $U$. It reverses face order. For a target face with free set $T$, the inverse image of its principal lower ideal in the **opposite** face poset is the complex of ordered partitions strictly respecting $H_{D\cup T}$. Indeed the seams in $D\cup T$ must hide, and all other signs have one prescribed choice when visible. The unary lane reconstruction of round 6, Response 10 gives one chart per such ordered partition and commutes with splitting tiers. This complex is nonempty and contractible for each target face, by the order-ideal cubing argument there. If $D$ itself is infeasible no temporal order exists. The finite poset fibre theorem now gives the displayed homotopy equivalence. [Chart and order-ideal argument](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-10) · [Barmak, Theorem 1.1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1005.0538)

For a one-bridge ring $K$ consists of the proper subsets of the $k$ seams. An intersection with $A\ne\varnothing$ is contractible. With $A=\varnothing$ and $|D|<k$ it has type $S^{k-|D|-1}$. Opposing five seams of $\Gamma_6$ leaves two components. Moreover, successively opposing any $k-1$ distinct seams gives integral Mayer–Vietoris isomorphisms from $\widetilde H_{k-1}(E(c_k))$ down to the difference of the two final components in $\widetilde H_0$. At each step the two sign subcomplexes cover the current intersection, each is contractible by the $A\ne\varnothing$ case, and their intersection imposes the next opposed seam. This identifies the primitive class up to orientation.

**Implication for relative coherence.** The abstract nerve of the signed geometric hemispheres has a simplex for every family with $D\in K$. For $\Gamma_6$ it is the boundary of the 11-simplex, hence an $S^{10}$, while the fibre is an $S^5$. The literal nerve in Response 2 is another $S^5$. Its inclusion into the geometric nerve is nullhomotopic for every graph in this family. Indeed choose any seam $i$. The geometric nerve contains the join of the full edge on its two signs with the $k-1$ remaining pairs of points, since $\{i\}\in K$. This is a $k$-ball bounded by the literal nerve. Consequently a simplicial filling using just lists of nonempty intersections cannot decide RC for the ring. A geometric construction using this cover must retain the classes *inside* the opposed intersections. Response 2 shows why the two final components cannot be represented by literal common colour erasures. Transporting their difference through the displayed isomorphisms by CaTT comparisons still requires a proof.

**Reproduction.** For each of 160 signed requests the solver selected the subcomplex from actual typed histories, checked closure under all facets, and compared its mod-two Betti numbers, including emptiness, with a separate cubical boundary-matrix calculation on $K'$. These tests used all 96 histories of the two-seam ring, 8,418 of the three-seam ring, 14,198 of the overlapping-cycle example, and 97 of the acyclic two-seam chain. For six seams it separately sorted the actual signal order for all 4,096 signed requests. There are 728 nonempty simplices in the literal nerve and 4,094 in the geometric nerve. The six-seam test computes feasibility from the signal order; the proof supplies the intersection homotopy types and the integral isomorphisms. Run the following from the repository root with Agda 2.8.0 for the raw judgments and erasure equations of Response 2. [Solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_seam_intersections.py)

```sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_seam_intersections.py --agda "$dir/SeamLifts.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/SeamLifts.agda"
```

# Researcher Response 4

## Suspension reflects fillers, even with prescribed strict image

For **every** CaTT context $B$, parallel terms $u,v$ of $B$, and strict cell $b:\pi u\to\pi v$, there is a term $u\to v$ over $b$ if and only if there is a term $\Sigma u\to\Sigma v$ over $\Sigma b$ in $\Sigma B$. The same equivalence holds without specifying an image. The backward implication takes an arbitrary raw term as input. Consequently a failure of $\mathrm{RC}_{\mathrm{id}}(n)$ survives every iterated suspension, in the precisely shifted degree, with the original pair as witness. This includes counterexamples with loops, composite attaching terms and identity faces. Round 6, Response 10 obtained suspension-stable obstructions for seam presentations by repeating their geometric detection argument. The following operation on syntax gives reflection for all presentations. [Earlier seam result](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-10)

**Restriction to a cut.** More generally colour the objects of $G$ by $0,1$ so that every generating arrow is nondecreasing. A positive-dimensional cell crosses when its outer objects have colours $0,1$. The context $G|$ has one generator $x|$ of dimension $d-1$ for each crossing $d$-generator $x$. A crossing 1-generator gives an object. For a higher generator restrict its attaching type, taking $s\to_A t$ to $s|\to_{A|}t|$ and taking a crossing 1-type to $\star$. Contexts and types here can have arbitrary weak attaching terms.

For a labelled pasting arity $P$, list its root vertices in path order. Their colours are nondecreasing. If its head crosses, exactly one consecutive pair crosses, say in position $j$. All positive-dimensional variables between these two root vertices in the globular tree belong to $\Sigma P_j$, where $P_j$ is the child tree at that position. Restricting those variables, in declaration order, gives exactly $P_j$. An arbitrary crossing variable restricts to its new variable. For a crossing 1-dimensional coherence over a path take the restriction of its one crossing **argument**. For a higher head $\operatorname{coh}_{P,A}[\sigma]$ use
$$
                 \operatorname{coh}_{P_j,A|}[\sigma|].
$$
Here $A|$ uses the root cut of $P$ determined by the label, and $\sigma|$ consists of the restrictions of the arguments on the crossing branch. The restriction need not be the unbiased composite of that child. In particular a compound head can restrict to a unary coherence.

**Typing and substitution.** In a globular pasting tree a generic legal head spans its root path. Its strict value is the composite of its arity, possibly padded by identities, whose outer objects are the two extreme root vertices. The same facts applied recursively show that an argument with both outer objects on one side uses only variables on that side. Hence a crossing term's free variables after restriction are exactly its original crossing free variables, reindexed. For a variable this is immediate. For a coherence take the union over its substitution entries. Only the selected branch contributes. At dimension one the path has only one contributing edge and its object arguments contribute nothing. The same argument applies to the free variables of its type, with the two outer objects discarded.

The full-support coherence rule therefore restricts to the full-support rule. For the composition rule in a tree of height $d$, the signed $(d-1)$-boundary on the selected branch restricts to the corresponding boundary of $P_j$ if its height is $d-1$. If it is shorter, both restrictions are all of $P_j$. This follows by taking the signed boundary in each root branch up to that rank. For $d=1$ the branch is a point. A head of dimension one was handled separately, and for a higher head with both sides full the full-support test applies. These are the two CaTT side conditions in the restricted arity.

For a substitution, colour its source arity by its object images. Restricting a substituted crossing term gives the restriction of the generic term substituted by the restricted arguments. On variables this is the definition. At a coherence, composition of labels induces the very same cut on its root vertices. For a 1-head it selects the same single argument; for a higher head apply induction to each retained entry. The type equation follows by arrow recursion. In declaration order this equation identifies the inferred type of each restricted entry with its required substituted type, proving the typing assertion together with the construction. All recursive calls on heads use their generic boundary or proper arguments.

**Strict image.** There is a strict functor $F(G)\to\Sigma_{\mathrm{str}}F(G|)$ taking vertices to their colours, cells entirely on one side to units there, and each crossing generator $x$ to the suspension of $x|$. To check its construction on attachments and the image of a raw term, induct over declarations and term formation. The strict evaluation of a pasting tree is the horizontal composite of its root branches, with identities to the common height. All but the crossing factor evaluate to units at the outer cut. The crossing factor evaluates to the suspension of its child composite, with precisely the identity padding dictated by its height. For a crossing path this leaves the selected edge. This proves both the needed face equations on generators and that every crossing $t$ evaluates to $\Sigma_{\mathrm{str}}\pi(t|)$. Strict suspension is injective on the cells in its forward hom.

For $G=\Sigma B$, the cut between the poles recovers $B$ in declaration order and $(\Sigma t)|=t$ literally by induction. In the hom between the poles the operations are the suspended heads. The substitution equation therefore gives a CaTT retraction of the inclusion $\mathrm{Free}(B)\to\Omega\mathrm{Free}(\Sigma B)$. The hom can contain additional raw cells. Applying the retraction to a proposed filler proves reflection, and suspending a filler proves the forward implication. This uses the usual suspension–hom syntax. [Benjamin and Markakis, Sections 3–4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.01611)

**Directed test.** At an arbitrary cut, an upstairs reverse would restrict to a reverse between the restricted boundaries. For example take $f,g:x\to y$ and $a:f\Rightarrow g$, suspend, and add endoloops at either pole with generating 2-endoloops on them. Whisker $\Sigma a$ at cut zero with arbitrary compatible stationary-side inputs. The restricted strict 2-cell goes from $f$ to $g$. In the lower free category every generating 2-step replaces $f$ by $g$, so no strict 2-cell goes from $g$ to $f$. This excludes every upstairs reverse, whatever its syntax. The proof also permits arbitrary additional contexts confined to either side: none changes the cut context. Inactive inputs can have arbitrary composites and any finite loop powers, since their entire strict images under the cut functor are units. The same argument works for any strict or raw obstruction downstairs whenever the indicated restricted boundaries are the obstructed pair (for a strict obstruction it suffices to identify their strict boundaries).

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_cut.py --max-edges 7`. The solver checked all 625 planar rooted trees with one through seven edges, all 1,429 root cuts, and 2,858 projected composites and identities. It checked exact inferred types, crossing support, top counts, substitution by actual inactive identity towers, and the literal retraction equation. It also recovered a 4-term with a composite attaching map after suspension and tested powers one through seven on both sides of the one-way example. With `--agda "$dir/CutReflection.agda"` the solver emits the power-three upstairs and downstairs judgments; Agda 2.8.0 accepts them with `--safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir"`. The induction gives the dimension and exponent bounds claimed above. The statement reflects specified fillers; ascent of RC for arbitrary pairs of raw terms upstairs is a further question. [Cut solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_cut.py)

# Researcher Response 5

## The seam sphere is the strict limit of contractible lane projections

For the one-bridge rings $\Gamma_k$, $k\ge2$, retain any subset $J$ of the $2k+1$ lanes. Compress the retained lanes to a line in their original order. Restrict every event to its retained footprint, keeping its name when that footprint is nonempty. Write $c_J$ for the projected distinguished label of item 32. Then **every proper projection has contractible geometric fibre over $c_J$**, even when it retains every top generator. The poset of full histories over $c_k$ is isomorphic to the ordinary inverse limit of the history posets over all the proper projections. Their homotopy limit is contractible. Thus the comparison from the nerve of the ordinary limit to the homotopy limit has homotopy fibre $S^{k-1}$.

This projection keeps the dimension of each retained generator. It is defined strictly by sending omitted lanes to identities and using the lane restrictions of the two attaching diagrams on every nonempty footprint. A weak presentation of the projected context uses the same horizontal bracketing convention on its own lanes. The chart maps below are defined directly on marked diagrams, so they require no choice of weak terms comparing those bracketings.

**Geometric fibres.** On a projected presentation the old lower gates still have nonempty 1-input and 1-output, as does every added changing marker in the temporal presentation $B_m$ of round 6, Response 4. A retained bridge can read only identities and still has a nonempty spatial footprint. Hence the lower and marked 2-polygraphs are bubble-free, so the transition presentation computes the **actual** geometric fibre here. Restriction just drops omitted lane words, drops markers with empty footprint and drops tiers that have become constant. This commutes with the ordinal face maps on marked diagrams and with further lane restriction. A split tier maps to its corresponding refinement, allowing equality. The empty selection has one empty history. [Transition presentation](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-4)

The temporal edges on retained signals are $b_i<a_{i+1}$ on internal signals, $z<a_1$ on the left end and $b_k<z$ on the right end. Hiding a retained seam adds $a_i<b_i$. These $2k+1$ edges, one per lane, form one simple directed cycle. Any proper selection of lanes permits a temporal order hiding **all** its seams. With fixed signs, every ordered partition respecting the retained signal edges gives one chart. Each signal determines its gate; a bridge reading a retained interior lane lies before its prefix. After putting the bridge first where it reads, the only spatial comparisons of simultaneous short markers are the disjoint seam pairs; a bridge changing an endpoint signal cannot tie with its other changer. This gives a common serialization of each tier, including after compressing nonconsecutive lanes. Intersecting sign sheets adds precisely the corresponding strict hiding inequalities. All these intersections are contractible ordered-partition complexes and all are nonempty. Their nerve is a simplex. Every chart with the given endpoints and named counts is accounted for, and connected sheets and their intersections give one strict label by exchanges. This proves the proper-projection claim.

The same contractibility holds over the projected image of **any** strict 3-label of the original ring. Its signal states allow each top event at most once, also after projection since generators on nonempty footprints keep distinct names. Fix its named ports on the retained lanes. At a retained seam remove the named consumed occurrence from the starting word and the named inserted occurrence from the ending word whenever present. The persistent word retains its order. Distinct gaps give a fixed spatial order; equal gaps with both events give the two sheets; a single event has its specified port. An exchange preserves these port identifications by expanding its simultaneous diagram. Conversely these data and the temporal partition reconstruct the lane words exactly as in round 6, Response 8. The surviving temporal and hiding relations are still a subgraph of the broken cycle. Thus the same nerve proof gives one contractible component for the specified label. This accounts for arbitrary flanking words, powers of either loop and missing events. The assertion concerns labels in the image; the projected presentation can also admit histories repeating a bridge that the original signal would have allowed only once. [Port classification](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-8)

**Reconstructing a full history.** It suffices to take the codimension-one projections (omit lane $i$) and require equality after each double omission $(i,j)$. Restriction then defines every smaller projection independently of the larger one chosen. Each event of the distinguished label survives every single omission: a short event occupies two lanes and the bridge occupies the whole line. For a given pair of events, all the singly omitted histories must agree on their temporal comparison, including equality. Indeed a double omission can lose one of the pair only if the two omitted lanes are precisely its short footprint. On the complete graph of omissions, at most two edges therefore fail to compare this pair. The graph stays connected since there are at least five lanes. Hence all singly omitted histories carry the same ordered partition of all events.

Read the words on each individual lane using its singleton restriction and this common clock. A tier containing a marker on that lane has its specified word. At a tier with none, the lane stays in its current state, beginning with the given initial word. This recovers a unique candidate full history. Its signal orders and seam placements hold lane by lane; the bridge-first and disjoint-pair serialization above makes every tier legal. It has the strict label $c_k$ by the original sign-sheet calculation. Its projections give the supplied histories, since the schedule and the marked words determine a diagram. To see order reflection, if the local images are refinements then their common schedules specify one refinement of the full partition. Splitting the marked words by that refinement substitutes the before or after face of each absent marker in each lane. The resulting words agree with the refined local words and hence with the reconstructed full chart. This gives the poset isomorphism. Objectwise contractibility of the proper diagram gives the assertion about its homotopy limit, while item 32 identifies the nerve of the full chart poset with $S^{k-1}$.

For $\Gamma_6$, the integral degree-five class thus vanishes even in the homotopy limit of **all** proper lane observations. It survives exact matching of their histories on overlaps. A test that separately normalizes each projected history up to homotopy loses precisely the class that needs an algebraic witness. This identifies a gluing condition for an approach using projections. It does not establish that RC forces this comparison to be an equivalence.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_lane_limits.py`. The solver generates all 31 proper selections of the two-seam ring, including the empty selection. It joins independently generated local history tables on their double omissions and compares the result with the full complex, checking every ordered pair for refinement. It reconstructs 96 histories and 9,216 order tests for the distinguished label. With two equal initial deletion letters at one seam it reconstructs 145 histories and 21,025 order tests across two strict components, with mod-two Betti numbers $(1)$ and $(1,1)$. All proper components in both runs have Betti numbers $(1)$. At six seams it reads the precedence edges from the signal rewrites and the insertion and deletion rules and tests all 8,192 lane selections for the all-hidden order. Only the complete selection is infeasible. Contractibility and matching for all $k$ follow from the proofs above. [Lane projection and matching solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_lane_limits.py)

# Researcher Response 6

## Strict homs in strong-Steiner contexts from cuts one dimension lower

Let $K$ be a finite strong Steiner complex, with the CaTT attaching terms presenting its atoms. Every directed object cut of Response 4 produces another strong Steiner complex, one dimension lower. For prescribed outer poles, **strict cells are exactly the families of lower-dimensional cut tableaux whose chain coefficients agree on successive overlaps**. In particular there is an exact equality test and an existence test for assembling strict cells. They apply even to tableaux with stationary lower rows.

**The cut complex.** Colour the vertices by $0,1$ nondecreasingly along the generating edges. Call a positive-dimensional basis element crossing when its two outer vertices have colours $0,1$. In degree $j$ retain the crossing basis elements of degree $j+1$, with differential their old differential restricted to crossing faces, and give each retained 1-edge (now an object) augmentation one. All faces of a noncrossing element lie on its own side. Indeed a path whose ends have the same colour stays on that side; in a strict composition all constituents at cut zero lie on successive subpaths and the constituents at higher cuts have the same outer objects. Apply this to the signed faces of the atom. Consequently restriction commutes with the positive-degree differentials after shifting. At the bottom, the signed count of crossing edges of a 1-chain $p$ is the pairing of its boundary with the vertex colouring. Thus the augmentation vanishes on every new boundary.

Restriction is a coordinate projection of free abelian groups. It commutes with taking the positive or negative part, including after cancellation in the differential of a whole row. Each signed iterated boundary of a crossing atom therefore restricts to the corresponding iterated boundary in the cut. The original signed 1-rows are directed paths between the atom's outer vertices. Each uses exactly one crossing edge, so the cut basis is unital. The strong ordering restricts, since each signed incidence was already present. Steiner's presentation theorem identifies the strict category of the cut with the free category on those atoms. Its generator declarations agree strictly with the cut of the weak presentation: the strict functor of Response 4 restricts the rows of each attaching face, as can also be seen from the tableau composition rule (add above the cut of composition). Thus every object cut of each of the 148 three-dimensional contexts in item 37 is a homotopical presentation of dimension at most two. [Steiner, Theorem 6.1](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0403237)

**Assembling a tableau.** Totally order the vertices $v_0,\ldots,v_l$ extending the directed edge order. Use the successive cuts $i=1,\ldots,l$ just before $v_i$, and write $C_i$ for their crossing bases. A generator spanning $v_s$ to $v_t$ belongs exactly to $C_{s+1},\ldots,C_t$. This interval is nonempty for every positive-dimensional generator by unitality and acyclicity. Fix $n\ge1$ and outer poles $v_p,v_q$ with $p\le q$. At each $p<i\le q$ choose any strict $(n-1)$-tableau of the cut, including its two object entries. At every other position put the zero array of chains. Impose equality of the coefficients on $C_i\cap C_{i+1}$ in every signed row. Then there is exactly one strict $n$-cell with these poles and these arrays. The zero array outside the selected interval is bookkeeping for an inactive cut.

Indeed restriction of any actual cell gives these equations. Conversely the interval property assigns a single nonnegative coefficient to each basis element in each required row. The signed tableau equations with values in positive degrees follow coefficient by coefficient in a cut containing that basis element. For each reconstructed 1-row the crossing count at $i$ is one on $p<i\le q$ and zero elsewhere by the augmentation of the local object rows. Its derivative pairs with each suffix indicator as does $v_q-v_p$. Successive differences of those indicators, together with total augmentation zero of a boundary, determine every vertex coefficient. This is precisely the missing degree-zero equation. The two top rows coincide by the local top-row identities. For $n=1$ use the same selected object in both top rows. The resulting nonnegative tableau is an element of $\nu K$ and the construction determines every entry. This proves existence as well as injectivity. Reversed distinct poles in the chosen order admit no cell.

**Application to item 37.** Across the 148 contexts the number of strict 3-cells per context ranges from 22,955 to 54,997, including identities. There are 42 distinct totals, with sum 5,497,750 when counted separately in each context. These counts use lexicographically ordered cube vertices. The largest single cut table contained 2,131 strict 2-cells. They are finite lists of the labels over which RC(3) and RC(4) are being asked; at degree four in these contexts the strict cells are identities on the degree-three list. For a proposed comparison of two raw terms with fixed poles, the solver can check their required strict equality one dimension lower and assemble its label from the overlap equations. A lift of those equations to compatible **weak** comparisons would be additional information. Response 5 explains the relevance of keeping track of the mode of gluing.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_steiner_cuts.py`. For each cut the solver constructs signed atom tableaux by the restricted differential, checks both terminal unit rows and compares with restriction after taking the original atom, then closes the cut atoms under every defined composition. Dynamic programming counts matches on consecutive common bases with one contiguous nonzero interval. As an independent check it generates the global strict cells by atom closure in the full two-, three- and four-cubes in every positive rank up to their dimension. Every resulting cut tuple is legal and injective at its fixed poles, and the global count equals the independently assembled count. In the four-cube the four counts are 168, 454, 520 and 521. The 148 larger computations use the proved assembly theorem. [Steiner cut solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_steiner_cuts.py)

# Researcher Response 7

## A based strict four-loop without a weak representative

**Strictification is surjective on cells of every finite context in degrees zero through three. In each degree at least four there is a finite context on which surjectivity fails.** This corrects the unconditional surjectivity assertion in §1.2. There is a seven-generator finite context $D$ and a strict 4-cell $c:1_x^3\to1_x^3$ such that **no weak term has strict image $c$**, even with freely chosen weak boundary. Suspension gives an example in every degree at least four. The equivalence between all-dimensional identity-image RC and boundary lifting in §1.2 survives with a different proof.

**The two routes.** Write composites in execution order and use the unbiased binary CaTT heads. Start with $x$, put $i=1_x$, and declare $a:i\Rightarrow i$. Put $g=i*_0 i$, $b=a*_0 i$, $d=i*_0 a$. Both $b,d$ are literal endomorphisms of $g$. Declare a third generator $J:b\to d$. There is a structural exchange
$$
 X:b*_1 d\longrightarrow d*_1 b .
$$
To obtain its raw term, take the pasting context of two horizontally consecutive 2-discs labelled $u,v$. On one side perform $u$ at the source of $v$, then $v$ at the target of $u$. On the other perform $v$ at the source of $u$, then $u$ at the target of $v$. The two 2-terms are parallel and both use the whole pasting context. Take their coherence and substitute $a$ into both slots. Define parallel 3-terms
$$
 T=J*_1 b,\qquad T'=(b*_1 J)*_2 X
       \quad:\quad b*_1 b\longrightarrow d*_1 b .
$$
Their strict images agree. Indeed $\pi b=\pi d=a$, $\pi X$ is an identity, and the products at cuts zero and one on strict 3-cells with both 1-boundaries $1_x$ have a common unit and satisfy interchange. Eckmann–Hilton makes either product commutative, including on $J$ and the identity promotion of $a$.

Write $e=1_g$, a weak 2-term supported on $x$. Adjoin $R:e\to b*_1b$ and $S:d*_1b\to e$, and put
$$
        V=(R*_2 T)*_2 S,\qquad U=(R*_2 T')*_2 S .
$$
These are literal 3-endomorphisms of $e$, with $\pi U=\pi V$. Finish $D$ with two 4-generators $\alpha:1_e\to U$ and $\beta:V\to1_e$. The strict composite $c=\pi\alpha *_3\pi\beta$ therefore exists. Since $\pi e=1_x^2$, it has the claimed based boundary.

**A charge on four-cells.** Use the strict braided monoidal groupoid whose object group and every endomorphism group are $\mathbb F_2$, with no arrows between distinct objects. Composition and tensor on arrows are addition, and the braiding on $p,q$ has value $pq$. Bilinearity proves the hexagons. Its double delooping $G$ is a Gray category, with the CaTT interpretation from item 17. In it a 3-cell is an endomorphism of a 2-label with a scalar charge. The exchange head just specified is the Gray interchanger. In the generic Gray pasting shape its two boundaries perform the two slots in the two orders, and the unique 3-operation between them evaluates to the braiding or its inverse. These coincide over $\mathbb F_2$. Equivalently, reduce the braiding calculation of item 17 modulo two. Use the same morphism of operads for all Gray categories below, so strict Gray functors preserve the chosen operations. [Gray operad and contractibility, Examples 3.6 and 5.7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1209.2776)

Let $K$ retain $G$ through degree three and have exactly one 4-cell between each parallel pair of 3-cells, with identities above. This is a CaTT model. Heads through rank three evaluate in $G$. Above that rank the prescribed boundary has a unique filler. In a globular pasting context a head of output degree $r$ only uses inputs of degree at most $r$, by the support conditions. There is a CaTT map
$$
       \delta:K\longrightarrow B^4\mathbb F_2,\qquad
       \delta(H)=\operatorname{charge}(tH)-\operatorname{charge}(sH)
       \quad(\dim H=4).
$$
Here the strict target has one cell in each degree below four. The check that $\delta$ respects heads matters because an individual Gray interchange has a nonzero charge.

Let $D_0$ be the **strict** extension of the strict $B^3\mathbb F_2$ by one 4-cell between each parallel pair, again with identities above. Taking the difference of the two endpoints defines a strict functor $D_0\to B^4\mathbb F_2$. Differences add under the lower compositions and telescope at cut three. Give $G$ only identities above degree three. There is a CaTT map $G\times D_0\to K$. Through degree three it adds the second scalar to the first 3-charge and retains the first 2-label. This is a strict Gray functor there since the second factor has trivial interchanger. Above degree three the extension is unique by thinness.

Every labelling of a globular pasting arity in $K$ factors through this map. In the first factor retain the labels through degree two and set every 3-variable's charge to zero. Its 2-faces have equal labels since a 3-cell between them exists. In the second factor give each 3-variable its original charge. A 4-variable has *variables* as its two 3-faces. Their first projections are now the same zero-charge 3-cell, so use its identity there and the prescribed unique 4-cell in the second factor. Higher labels are forced. For a 4-head the evaluated first projection has equal 3-faces, so its contribution to the difference is zero. The evaluated second projection obeys the strict difference functor. This proves the displayed formula preserves every 4-head. Higher operations follow by uniqueness. The factorization is required only on globular arities, which is why this argument applies to arbitrary CaTT terms by substitution.

Interpret $D$ in $K$ with $a=1\in\mathbb F_2$ and $J,R,S$ of charge zero. The two 2-boundaries of $R,S$ have label zero since $1+1=0$. The whiskerings within the single hom have charge zero, while $X$ contributes $1\cdot1=1$. Thus $V$ has charge zero, $U$ has charge one, and $\delta(\alpha)=1,\ \delta(\beta)=0$. Since $\delta$ takes values in a strict model, its value on any weak 4-term is the multiplicity of $\alpha$ in the strict image, modulo two. In particular a lift of $c$ would have value one.

Both 3-faces of **any** candidate lift have strict image $1_x^3$. The support lemma of item 2 puts both faces in the point context on $x$. Its interpretation factors through the terminal strict subcategory of $G$, so both charges are zero. Their difference is zero. This contradicts the required value one and proves nonrepresentability.

The obstruction also occurs in the universal computad $P(\{x\})$ of round 6, Response 17. Send each positive-rank generator to the bracket indexed by its two substituted terms. The same-rank boundary pairs are distinct, so this identifies $D$ with a dependency-closed subcomputad. A representative of the indicated strict word there would already have support in $D$ by item 2. Exact lifting of based strict loops in this universal computad therefore fails as well. For an iterated suspension of $D$, cut any putative representative of the suspended strict word at its outer poles. The cut of Response 4 commutes with strict interpretation and sends that word back to $c$ on iteration. This proves the dimension claim.

**The valid lifting implication.** Under $\mathrm{RC}_{\mathrm{id}}(\infty)(\Gamma)$, lift a strict expression by induction. At a cut $k$, take weak lifts of the two operands and correct the shared boundaries in increasing dimension, whiskering by identity-image comparisons until the $k$-faces match literally. The operands can then be composed without changing the strict value. The same corrections prescribe the outer boundary. Conversely, apply boundary lifting to a strict identity to obtain $\mathrm{RC}_{\mathrm{id}}$. The induction for expressions of degree $m$ uses comparisons only through degree $m-1$. At degrees one and two the universal comparisons of item 1 can be chosen over identities: the path normalizations and the elementary exchanges there are structural comparisons with identity strict image. Degree zero consists of the object generators. This proves universal surjectivity through degree three and finishes the dimension classification. The comparison with a trivial fibration remains valid. The example refutes surjectivity without the RC hypothesis. Its 2-skeleton has a bubble, so it does not separate RC from homotopicality. [Boundary correction and initial lifts, §§4.3–4.4](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-2026-11-19.md#43-a-boundary-correction-lemma)

**Validation.** The certificate generator builds the generic two-disc exchange before specializing its slots, checks the two routes against the same literal type, checks all seven declarations, and emits the raw judgments for Agda 2.8.0. The parity proof excludes all terms, including terms with unprescribed weak boundaries. [Certificate generator](../../../tools/relative_coherence_spherical_lift.py)

```sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_spherical_lift.py --agda "$dir/SphericalLift.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/SphericalLift.agda"
```

## A seven-vertex integral obstruction for the original six seams

Let $R_8$ mean relative coherence with identity strict image through degree eight on $\Gamma_6$. Let $P(V)$ be the computad of Response 17 of round 6. Its vertices are $V$, and it has a generator $[u,v]$ for every ordered pair of parallel weak terms of the preceding rank. Every function $f:V\to W$ induces a map $P(f)$ by sending a bracket to the bracket of the two substituted terms. This commutes with composition literally. Write $X(V)=|L P(V)|$ for the groupoidified realization. There is a single integral class $b\in H_5 X(\{0,\ldots,6\})$, specified below without reference to the seam ring, with the following property. If $R_8$ holds, there is a homomorphism $D:H_5 X(\{0,\ldots,6\})\to\mathbb Z$ satisfying

$$
 D(b)=1,\qquad D\bigl(\operatorname{im}(H_5X(U)\to H_5X(\{0,\ldots,6\}))\bigr)=0\quad(U\subsetneq \{0,\ldots,6\}).
$$

In particular $b$ generates a split integral summand even after quotienting by the sum of all the displayed proper-subset images.

**The class.** Put $J=\{0,\ldots,6\}$. The cubical subdivision of the boundary of the ordered simplex on $J$ has one cube for each interval $[A,B]$ with $\varnothing\ne A\subseteq B\subsetneq J$. Its vertices are the subsets $C$ between $A$ and $B$. Indeed these are faces of the seven-cube, with the entries in $A$ fixed to one and those outside $B$ fixed to zero. Their staircase triangulation is exactly the order complex of the proper nonempty subsets of $J$, the barycentric subdivision of the simplex boundary. Every cube has rank at most five. Choose canonical terms through rank four once on the seven-cube, then adjoin the rank-five variables of this subcomplex between their two prescribed four-dimensional faces, as in round 6, Response 17. Restriction to each cube is a weak presentation of that Gray cube. Its groupoidification is contractible by the strong Steiner comparison, and the latching inclusions attach the characteristic globes. Thus the subdivision and the realized parameter have the same homotopy type, compatibly with restrictions to subcomplexes, by the homotopy colimit over faces. Orient it by the boundary orientation of the ordered simplex.

Send a vertex $C$ to $\min C$ and every positive-rank generator to the bracket of the images of its two literal attaching terms. The resulting map of the realized parameter into $X(J)$ sends its oriented fundamental class to $b$. In the entire simplex subface on $U\subsetneq J$ every cube has $B\subseteq U$; its map factors through $X(U)$. The same choice of syntax and minima is used on that rank subset in every copy of the ordered simplex.

**A detector under $R_8$.** Let $V=\{0,1\}^6$. Construct a map $p:|L P(V)_{\le6}|\to E(c_6)$ as follows. For each generator take the smallest cubical face containing the vertices in the support of its literal type, or its singleton vertex in rank zero. Give it a label in that face's coloured context $\Lambda_F$, with the three suspension poles of round 6, Response 9. The support of each boundary generator is contained in the support of the attaching terms, so its face is contained in $F$. Erasing all the needed labels into $\Lambda_F$ therefore gives a well-typed substitution on the support. The erasures compose literally and take the poles and strict labels to their counterparts. At rank zero lift $c_F$ at the poles: RC through degree two gives initial lifts of strict 3-expressions and then corrects their faces by the cut induction above. At rank $j=1,\ldots,6$, the two substituted boundaries are literally parallel $(j+2)$-terms, both over $1^{j-1}_{c_F}$. The generator-preserving colour map transfers the required lifting from $\Gamma_6$ to $\Lambda_F$. The largest degree used is eight. This defines the map after erasure to the base by suspension–hom. For any cubical face $F$, its restriction to the parameter on vertices of $F$ factors through the associated coloured fibre. The visible-sign map $\phi:E(c_6)\to\partial I^6$ consequently carries the restriction into the union $S_F$ of the closed facets meeting $F$ when $F$ is proper. The $S_F$ are nested and contractible by the facet-nerve argument in round 6, Response 9. Write $d:H_5 X(V)\to\mathbb Z$ for the homomorphism of $\phi p$, using the fact that adding cells of dimension at least seven preserves $H_5$.

Triangulate $I^6$ by its saturated flags of vertex subsets. For a permutation $\sigma$ of the axes let $f_\sigma:J\to V$ take $j$ to the vertex with the first $j$ axes of $\sigma$ equal to one. Orient the ordered simplex on each flag with coefficient $\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)$, for the increasing-axis orientation of the cube. Set

$$
 D=\sum_{\sigma\in S_6}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\,d\, X(f_\sigma)_* .
$$

The oriented sum of the transported subdivided simplex *boundaries* maps into $X(V)$. At an interior facet, the two flags differ by transposing two consecutive axes. The same rank is omitted from both; the restricted vertex maps on the remaining rank set agree. All the cubical subface maps agree there too, by their construction from one parameter on the ordered rank set, so their oriented fundamental chains cancel. Equivalently, take a single fundamental chain for each oriented face, recursively compatible with its boundary. This is possible on the cubical parameter since each cell realization is contractible and the latching diagram is cellular. Thus $\sum_\sigma\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma) X(f_\sigma)_*b$ is the class of the remaining subdivided outer boundary. On a cube facet $F$ all its parameter vertices lie in $F$; composing its map with $\phi p$ lands in $S_F$. This composite and the ordinary subdivision map are homotopic by the contractible carriers, using their common face diagram. Its degree is one. This proves the first identity, with the sign of the identification of $H_5(\partial I^6)$ chosen by the cube orientation.

If $j\notin U$ for some $0<j<6$, pair the permutations by interchanging entries $j,j+1$ (positions counted from one). The restrictions of their $f_\sigma$ to $U$ agree, while their coefficients are opposite. If $0\notin U$, all vertices of $f_\sigma(U)$ have the first axis of $\sigma$ fixed to one. If $6\notin U$, all have its last axis fixed to zero. In these endpoint cases each summand factors through a single proper-facet carrier after $\phi p$, so vanishes on $H_5$. This proves the second identity. In particular any nonzero multiple of $b$ supported in homology on proper subsets disproves $R_8$. Establishing such a relation, or proving $R_8$, requires an additional argument.

**Incidence check.** The checker independently builds the oriented boundary from the 720 maximal flags and from the twelve exterior facets. The two integer chains agree on all 8,640 exterior five-cubes. It also checks pair cancellation or proper-facet containment for all 126 nonempty proper subsets of ranks. Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_simplex_carrier.py`. [Integral carrier checker](../../../tools/relative_coherence_simplex_carrier.py)

The same calculation gives a uniform sufficient homology test for the one-bridge rings. For any $k\ge6$, suppose
$$
       H_{j-1}\bigl(X(\{0,\ldots,j\});\mathbb Q\bigr)=0
                     \qquad (6\le j\le k).
$$
Then $\Gamma_k$ fails identity-image RC by degree $k+2$. To prove this, make the coloured labelling on the $k$-skeleton of $P(\{0,1\}^k)$ under the contrary assumption, exactly as above. Use ranks $0,\ldots,k$ for the staircase triangulation. On its simplices through dimension five, use the cubical subdivision and the single degree-four choice on the $(k+1)$-cube to obtain compatible fundamental chains. Choose these recursively on the cubical pieces, prescribing their boundaries by the signed facet sums. Each such sum bounds in its contractible face realization. For every rank subset $U$ of size $j+1$, $6\le j\le k$, the oriented sum of the chains on its proper facets is then a cycle in $X(U)$. The displayed vanishing supplies a rational filling chain. Choose it once for $U$ and use its images on all flags with that rank set. The chain can be chosen in the $j$-skeleton because the relative homology of the whole space modulo that skeleton vanishes in degree $j$. This gives a filling of the outer cycle in the full parameter. On each proper face the construction on its boundary factors through the vertices of that face. After applying the detector this carried boundary has degree one, since corresponding vertices have the same augmentation and the usual oriented face chains and the constructed chains are chain homotopic on the boundary by induction inside the contractible carriers $S_F$. A rational boundary cannot have that degree. This proves the test. The vanishing hypothesis, already its $j=6$ instance, remains open here. The based loop above shows why exact surjectivity on $P(V)$ cannot establish that hypothesis.

# Researcher Response 8

## A rational matrix invariant for every power of the based four-loop

In the seven-generator context $D$ of Response 7, let $U$ be the route containing the exchange and $V$ the direct route. There is a literal CaTT comparison $V\to U$, but **no comparison $U\to V$ of any strict image**. This persists for every positive power and for arbitrary inactive parallel inputs as specified below. In fact the only representable strict 4-endomorphism of $1_x^3$ in $D$ is its identity. Thus the distinct strict loops $c^n$, $n>0$, all lack representatives even with freely chosen weak boundaries.

**Evaluation of the routes.** Use the strict symmetric monoidal category $M$ of positive integer dimensions and nonnegative rational matrices with a nonzero entry in every row and column. Composition is matrix multiplication, tensor is the Kronecker product in lexicographic order, and symmetry swaps the two tensor indices. These operations preserve the row and column condition, including for rectangular matrices. In particular every morphism is nonzero. Double delooping gives a Gray category $G$ with matrices as 3-cells. Use the Gray interpretation of CaTT of item 17. Interpret $a$ by dimension two, $J$ by its identity matrix, $R$ by the column $r=(1,1,2,1)^t$ and $S$ by $r^t/7$. Their sources and targets are respectively $1\to4$ and $4\to1$ for $R,S$. Both $b,d$ evaluate to dimension two. The direct route between the cup and cap evaluates to the identity matrix on four coordinates. The other route evaluates to the transposition of the two tensor indices: the generic two-disc exchange is the Gray interchanger, and either choice of its orientation gives the same swap here. Therefore $V=r^tr/7=1$ and $U=r^t\operatorname{swap}(r)/7=6/7$. The weak 3-identity on $e$ evaluates to $1$. Write $q=6/7$. [Gray-category operad, Examples 3.6 and 5.7](https://arxiv.org/abs/1209.2776)

**Why every CaTT term obeys the invariant.** Extend $G$ by a unique 4-cell for each parallel pair and identities above, obtaining $K$. Inside $K\times B^4\mathbb Z$ take every cell through degree three and in degree four take precisely the triples $(M,N,w)$ with $N=q^w M$. Above take their identities. This defines a CaTT submodel $H$. To verify closure, let $E$ be the strict extension of $B^3\mathbb Z$ by unique 4-cells between parallel 3-cells, and identities above. There is a CaTT map $G\times E\to K\times B^4\mathbb Z$. The first component is the identity on the first factor below rank three and at rank three sends $(M,h)$ to $q^h M$. This is a strict Gray functor through rank three: matrix composition and tensor are bilinear, and each structural interchanger in the product has second coordinate zero. The extension above rank three is forced by thinness of $K$. The second component takes the difference of the two integer endpoints of an $E$-arrow in rank four. This is strict since differences add and telescope. Every image 4-cell satisfies the equation defining $H$.

Every globular pasting arity of height at most four labelled in $H$ lifts along this map. Keep the labels through dimension two in $G$. In the arity, the directed graph on 3-variables with an edge for each 4-variable is a disjoint union of paths and isolated vertices: at each depth-three node of the planar tree the children are consecutive 4-discs with distinct successive 3-faces. Along each path use the initial matrix $M_0$ for every first-factor 3-label and use the successive partial sums of the edge weights for the $E$-labels, starting at zero. The original labels are exactly $q^h M_0$, and every 4-label lifts using an identity in $G$. Use the given matrix and potential zero for an isolated vertex. Hence every legal 4-head evaluated on this arity lands in $H$, regardless of its generic attaching terms. An output of degree at most four has arity of height at most four by the CaTT support rules. In higher ranks the product has only identities, and closure follows from closure on faces. This proves the claim for arbitrary raw operations by substitution.

Interpret $D$ in $H$ by giving $\alpha:1_e\to U$ weight one and $\beta:V\to1_e$ weight zero. Projection to the strict $B^4\mathbb Z$ then says that **the weight of every weak 4-term is exactly the multiplicity of $\alpha$ in its strict image**. It is a nonnegative integer. The weight between given weak 3-boundaries is unique whenever it exists, since their source matrix is nonzero and $q$ has infinite multiplicative order.

**Directed comparisons.** The forward derivation is $F=\beta *_3\alpha:V\to U$. Both endpoints are 3-endomorphisms of the same literal 2-term $e$ and have equal strict image. Form the left-associated powers at cut two. For any $n\ge1$ and arbitrary 3-endomorphisms $L,R'$ of $e$, whiskering $F^{*_2 n}$ gives
$$
 (L *_2 V^{*_2 n}) *_2 R' \longrightarrow (L *_2 U^{*_2 n}) *_2 R'.
$$
The two evaluated endpoint matrices have ratio $q^n$ because composition is bilinear. The inactive matrices can be any definable values, including composites of any loop powers; the common product is nonzero by the row and column condition. Every forward comparison uses exactly $n$ copies of $\alpha$, and a reverse would have weight $-n$. More generally the same proof works in any fixed expression of matrix compositions, tensor products and whiskerings with $n$ distinguished occurrences of the route and shared, typed inactive inputs. All the other factors evaluate in $M$ and preserve the nonzero condition. This proves a failure of RC(3) even when the comparison can have any strict image. In the forward direction the pair also lacks an identity-image comparison. Response 4 transfers the failure to its iterated suspensions.

**Strict loops.** The two faces of a putative representative of a strict 4-endomorphism of $1_x^3$ use only $x$, by the strict support lemma of item 2. They both evaluate to the scalar $1$ because the point interpretation factors through the terminal strict subcategory. Hence the representative uses zero copies of $\alpha$. For any strict 4-cell, target minus source multiplicity of the 3-generator $R$ equals the multiplicity of $\alpha$ minus that of $\beta$. This holds on both generators from their displayed boundaries, on identities, and under compositions by addition or telescoping. Thus a based loop also has zero copies of $\beta$. An expression with no 4-generator is an identity by the strict unit and composition laws. Conversely the identity is represented by an iterated weak identity on $x$. The powers of $c=\pi\alpha *_3\pi\beta$ have distinct $\alpha$-counts, proving the assertion for all of them. For each prescribed word $c^n$ the support argument in Response 7 also applies in the universal bracket computad on one vertex. This does not determine the homology class used in the six-seam test.

**Reproduction.** The solver evaluates all 16 columns with entries in $\{1,2\}$ and caps normalized by the squared norm; eight distinguish the exchange. It verifies path potentials on all 2,341 rank-four planar tree arities with at most nine edges, for each of their 8,664 binary edge labellings in total. It type-checks the endpoints and generator counts for powers one through four, both bare and with $U,V$ as flanks. The emitted Agda judgments check the first two bare powers and the once-flanked comparison at power one. The argument in $H$ supplies the unbounded result. # Researcher Response 9

## A single five-bracket gives the seven-vertex homology test

Use the degree-four choice of cubical attaching terms and the minimum vertex labelling of Response 7. In the ordered simplex on $J=\{0,1,\ldots,6\}$ take the five-cube with lower subset $\{6\}$ and upper subset $I=\{1,\ldots,6\}$. Let $g$ denote its image 5-generator in $P(I)$, a bracket of its two labelled 4-faces. For $S\subseteq I$ write $g_S$ for its relabelling sending all ranks in $S$ to zero. The following cellular chain has **64 distinct generators**, and its class gives a test involving only one six-vertex homology group:
$$
 z=\sum_{S\subseteq I}(-1)^{|S|}g_S .
$$

Write $p_i$ for the map collapsing rank $i$ to zero and $E=\prod_{i\in I}(1-p_i)$ on integral cellular chains or homology. The factors commute and are idempotent. All vertex maps here send generators to generators, and act on the cellular chains by that relabelling. A cell whose support misses $i$ is fixed by $p_i$. For the cubical maps of Response 7 the support of each bracket contains exactly the vertex names occurring on its cube. Indeed the two strict boundary pastings together use all immediate facets, by the signed cube differential; the support lemma gives their occurrence in the weak attaching terms. The statement follows down to vertices by induction. Relabelling commutes with brackets, so in particular the support of $g_S$ distinguishes every $S$.

**Computing the projection.** The cellular representative of $b$ is the oriented sum of the 42 five-cubes, six in each of the seven boundary simplices. Every cube on a simplex missing a rank $i>0$ is killed by $E$. On the simplex missing zero, the cube anchored at $j$ only uses names at most $j$, by the minimum labelling. Thus exactly the cube anchored at six survives, and $Eb=\pm[z]$, with sign $-$ in the ordered incidence convention of the checker. One can also see $\partial z=0$ facet by facet. A zero-side facet of this five-cube misses its free rank $i$. A one-side facet has minimum at most $i<6$ and misses rank six. Each of the ten facet brackets therefore cancels separately after applying $E$.

Let $A=H_5X(J)$ and $B$ be the sum of all proper-subset images in $A$. Every class $a$ satisfies $a-Ea\in B$ by expansion. Moreover $EB=E\,\mathrm{im}(H_5X(I)\to A)$: every other proper subset either misses a positive rank, or is contained in $I$. Since $E$ is an idempotent, these identities give
$$
 A/B\ \cong\ EA\big/E\,\mathrm{im}(H_5X(I)\to A).
$$
Consequently the sufficient obstruction of Response 7 can be tested exactly as follows. Find a nonzero integer $m$, a cellular 5-cycle $y$ on the six positive ranks, and a finite cellular 6-chain $w$ on the seven ranks with $mz-Ey=\partial w$. Signs can be absorbed into $m$. Such a certificate disproves identity-image relative coherence through degree eight for $\Gamma_6$. Conversely any nonzero-multiple relation in the proper-subset quotient of Response 7 yields a certificate of this form. Each possible 6-generator of $w$ is indexed by two literal parallel 5-terms; its boundary is their difference of top-generator counts. Brackets between two variables alone always give zero here, since in $P(J)$ a 5-variable is uniquely determined by its literal attaching pair.

**Where a certificate must use seven ranks.** Let $Y=\bigcup_{U\subsetneq J} X(U)$ inside $X(J)$. It admits a continuous map to the boundary of the ordinary simplex on $J$, sending each vertex to its name and every generator into the face spanned by its support. Construct it inductively on cells by extending their attaching maps in those convex faces. On the subdivided simplex boundary defining $b$ this map has degree one: on every original simplex face both it and the subdivision map land in that face, so cellular induction gives a homotopy between them. Every cycle in a single proper $X(U)$ has degree zero. Thus $[z]$ has degree $\pm1$ already in $Y$, and $Ey$ has degree zero there. Any $w$ in the test must contain a 6-bracket with all seven ranks in its support. This argument identifies a primitive class before the full-support attachments. Whether those attachments give the required boundary remains open.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_polarized_cube.py`. For three through seven ranks it expands the projection on every top cube, records the sole survivor and its orientation, checks its labelled-vertex summands and cancels each facet independently. At seven ranks it checks all 42 cubes, the 64 different labellings, all ten facet cancellations, and annihilation of the chain by each of the six individual collapse maps. The calculation uses vertex relabellings and cubical incidence. The cellular proof makes it independent of the allowed common choice of weak attaching syntax.
