# Researcher Response 10

## A single vertex identification tests the groupoidal realization

Let $P(V)$ be the bracket computad of Response 7 and put $A(V)=\mathrm{Free}(P(V))$, $X(V)=|LP(V)|$. For nonempty $V$, the map $A(V)\to 1$ has globular boundary lifting. A vertex solves the dimension-zero problem and the generator $[u,v]$ solves the problem over each prescribed parallel pair. Every positive-dimensional cell has a reverse and both cancellation witnesses by the same bracket construction, iterated on the witnesses. Thus $A(V)$ is groupoidal and computad-cofibrant. By two-out-of-three, **every** function between nonempty vertex sets induces an FHM weak equivalence of these models. Here both maps to $1$ are globular trivial fibrations.

Here is what degree-eight relative coherence on $\Gamma_6$ would force on one such equivalence. Fix a pointed set $V=\{*\}\amalg M$ with $M$ finite or countably infinite, choose $a\in M$, and let $q$ identify $a$ with $*$, fixing the other vertices. Then
$$
  \ker\!\left(H_5(X(q);\mathbb Z)\right)
  \quad\text{contains a direct summand}\quad
  \bigoplus_{\substack{S\subseteq M,\ |S|=6\\a\in S}}\mathbb Z .
$$
Here and below $H_5 X(q)$ denotes the induced map on integral homology. In particular its kernel has a split free subgroup of rank $\binom{|M|-1}{5}$ in the finite case and of countably infinite rank in the infinite case.

**Proof.** Use $J=\{0,\ldots,6\}$ with base point $0$ and the class $b$ and functional $D$ of Response 7. For $i>0$, let $e_i$ send $i$ to $0$ and fix the other ranks. The induced endomorphisms of homology are commuting idempotents. Write $f_*$ for the action of a vertex function and set
$$
             h=\prod_{i=1}^6 (1-e_{i*})b .
$$
Every nonidentity term in the expansion factors through a proper subset. Consequently $D(h)=1$, while $e_{i*}h=0$ for every $i$.

Order $M$ once. For each six-element subset $S$, let $j_S:J\hookrightarrow V$ list its elements in order after $*$ and $r_S:V\to J$ be its left inverse taking the complement to $0$. If $S\ne T$ then $r_T j_S$ misses at least one positive rank. Therefore $D r_{T*} j_{S*} h$ is the Kronecker delta on the six-element subsets. The map with coordinates $D r_{T*}$ takes values in their **direct sum**, even for infinite $M$. Indeed a homology class has a cellular cycle of finite support. Each of its finitely many generating cells and its attaching terms contain only finitely many vertex names, by finite syntax and descent in dimension. For a coordinate to be nonzero all six vertices of $T$ must occur among those names. Thus these coordinates give a retraction onto the free subgroup spanned by the $j_{S*}h$. If $a\in S$ has rank $i$ there, then $qj_S=qj_Se_i$, so $q_*j_{S*}h=0$. Keeping only the coordinates for $S$ containing $a$ gives the stated retraction on the kernel.

**Consequence for all one-bridge rings.** Suppose groupoidified realization takes FHM weak equivalences between groupoidal computad-cofibrant CaTT models to weak equivalences of spaces. In fact it suffices to test just the inclusions of the free point $\mathbb D^0\to A(\{0,\ldots,j\})$, $j\ge6$, on rational homology in degree $j-1$. The free point is groupoidal and algebraically contractible by pasting-context coherence, and its groupoidification is a point. These inclusions are FHM weak equivalences by the same two-out-of-three argument. The homological condition therefore gives exactly the vanishings in the last paragraph of Response 7. For **every** $k\ge6$ it follows that $\Gamma_k$ fails $\mathrm{RC}_{\mathrm{id}}(n)$ for some $n\le k+2$. For $k=6$, injectivity on integral fifth homology for the single countable vertex identification $q$ above would also suffice.

This supplies a specific test for Lanari's all-dimensional path-object proposal. Apply his Theorem 4.4 to the CaTT categorical coherator with independent left and right inverse data. A path object on its models, with endpoint map a fibration and both endpoint projections trivial fibrations as in that theorem, gives the groupoid coherator and its canonical semi-model structure. Source-globe inclusions are then trivial cofibrations, so their pushouts at cellular models are weak equivalences. The groupoidal comparison of the unit-condition problem, Known (VIII)(A) and the proof of (K), identifies the specified groupoidified CaTT realization on groupoidal cofibrant models and shows that it preserves their FHM weak equivalences. Consequently a positive answer to Lanari's proposal for this theory decides relative coherence negatively for the whole one-bridge family. Conversely, degree-eight relative coherence on $\Gamma_6$ would already preclude that path object, with the single map $A(q)$ exhibiting the failure on fifth homology. [Lanari, Theorems 4.4 and 4.10 and Remark 4.11](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1809.07923) · [Groupoidal realization comparison, §1.5](../answered/catt-models-unit-condition-r2-2026-11-19.md#15-identifying-the-specified-realization)

The published three-dimensional path object does not reach the required degree. The elementary-expansion theorem for Grothendieck coherators assumes that its input theory is already a groupoid coherator. Its proof of Proposition 3.4 explicitly invokes contractibility of that theory for the pair lifted to a globular sum. Together with Known (VIII)(A), the theorem thus gives the following implication. If the inverse-data extension of CaTT is a Grothendieck coherator in the Ara–Henry convention, all the one-bridge rings above fail relative coherence by the stated degrees. If degree-eight relative coherence holds on the six-seam ring, that extension fails the recognition condition. The recognition condition, fifth-homology injectivity for $q$, and the six-seam decision remain unproved here. [Elementary expansions, Theorem 1.1 and Proposition 3.4](https://github.com/openai/math/blob/main/preprints/The-Grothendieck-homotopy-hypothesis-via-elementary-expansions-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)

For a finite check of the splitting, take $M=\{1,\ldots,9\}$. Expand each $j_{S*}h$ as the signed sum of its 64 vertex functions, then compose with every $r_T$. The coefficient of the identity on $J$ gives the $84\times84$ identity matrix. Directly collapsing each vertex of each $S$ gives zero in all 504 signed sums. Exactly 56 of the 84 subsets contain a fixed vertex. This checks the integer calculation independently of the homological premise $R_8$.

# Researcher Response 11

## Relative simplex degree detects one-bridge seam rings

Let $k\ge2$. Colour the objects of any finite, well-typed CaTT context $Q$ by the vertices $J=\{0,\ldots,k\}$ of an ordered simplex. A generator carries the colours of the objects in its dependency closure. Write $Y_Q$ for the union of the subcomputads missing at least one colour, and $|Q|$ for the groupoidified cellular realization. There is a map of pairs
$$
  (|Q|,|Y_Q|)\longrightarrow (\Delta^k,\partial\Delta^k)
$$
sending vertices to their colours and each generator into the simplex on its colours. Extend over the cells in their convex supporting faces; the same facewise construction gives uniqueness up to carried homotopy. **If this map has nonzero image on $H_k(-;\mathbb Q)$, the one-bridge ring $\Gamma_k$ fails identity-image RC in some degree at most $k+2$.** The boundary of a certificate may be any cycle on the union of proper colour subcontexts. Response 9 gives a particular degree-one boundary of interest. The present criterion does not require the boundary to have that class modulo cycles on individual subcontexts.

**Proof.** Work on the $k$-skeleton, which contains every cellular $k$-chain. Suppose RC holds through degree $k+2$. The coloured labelling of Response 7 gives a map from $X(\{0,1\}^k)_{\le k}$ to $\partial I^k$. In detail, assign each bracket generator the smallest cube face containing its support. In degree zero lift the distinguished strict 3-label at the specified poles using boundary correction through degree two. In rank $j>0$ erase all the lower labels into the face's coloured presentation, then use the generator-preserving transfer and RC in degree $j+2$ to fill the resulting parallel pair. These choices give the map after erasure to $\Gamma_k$ and application of the visible-sign map. On any proper face $F$ its cubically supported subcontext maps to the contractible union $S_F$ of the closed facets meeting $F$, with $S_{F'}\subseteq S_F$ for $F'\subseteq F$. This construction needs only globular brackets in the parameter. [Coloured labelling and carriers](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-9)

For each permutation $\sigma$ of the $k$ axes take a copy of $|Q|_{\le k}$. Across two flags differing by interchanging consecutive positions $j,j+1$, identify their identical subcomplexes missing rank $j$ ($0<j<k$). Call the resulting cellular space $T$ and let $T_\partial$ be the union of its copies of the subcomplexes missing rank zero or $k$. Identifications are the identity on each named cell. The two constructions below thus give continuous maps on $T$.

- The first, $t:T\to I^k$, uses the map to $\Delta^k$ above and then the affine identification with the staircase simplex of the flag. These affine maps agree on the shared rank face. On $T_\partial$ the image lies in $\partial I^k$.
- The second, $p:T\to\partial I^k$, sends each vertex colour to the corresponding flag vertex in the cube, each positive-rank generator to the bracket of the images of its two literal attaching terms, and then uses the coloured labelling. The flag vertex functions agree on the identified cells and therefore so do the bracket substitutions.

On $T_\partial$, the flag vertices on each cell lie in a smallest **proper** cube face $F$. It is the same face across every identification of that cell. The two maps land respectively in $F$ and $S_F$. A homotopy between them extends over cells in $S_F$ by contractibility and nesting. Thus $p|_{T_\partial}$ and $t|_{T_\partial}$ induce the same boundary degree.

Given a relative cycle $a$ of simplex degree $d$, put $A=\sum_\sigma \operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)a_\sigma$ in $T$. Every generator in $\partial a$ misses some rank. If it misses an interior $j$, fix one such $j$ for that generator: the two copies at each transposition cancel. All remaining boundary cells lie in $T_\partial$. The class of $tA$ in $H_k(I^k,\partial I^k)$ has degree $d$. Indeed modulo the union of all staircase simplex boundaries, its component in each oriented simplex is precisely $d$ times that simplex's coefficient in the oriented cube; the fundamental class is determined by these components. Hence $t(\partial A)$ has degree $d$ on the cube boundary. But $p(\partial A)$ bounds there, because $p$ is defined on all of $T$. Their degrees are equal, so $d=0$, as asserted.

**An exact finite test.** In the cellular chain complex of $Q$, the boundary of a generator is target minus source top-generator counts. To see this in the specified realization, induct on dimension and term formation. A variable contributes its own oriented globe. In a generic pasting arity of height $r$, the cellular top differential is injective: the realized arity is contractible and has dimension $r$. The boundary of the characteristic chain of an $r$-operation is the difference of its already computed face chains. In the strict free arity the sum of its $r$-slot occurrences has exactly this boundary by addition and telescoping under strict composition. It is therefore the unique top chain. Arity of smaller height contributes zero. Substitution pushes these chains forward and gives the count formula in every context.

Compute a simplex chain map $h$ by sending each object to its colour and taking $h(g)=v*h(\partial g)$ in higher ranks, with $v$ the least colour of $g$. Here $v*$ is the ordinary oriented simplex cone, with repeated vertices giving zero. The cone identity makes this a chain map, using augmentation zero for edge boundaries. It computes the map of pairs above by the acyclic-carrier chain homotopy in the spanning faces. Let $M$ consist of the cellular differential from rank $k$, retaining only rank-$(k-1)$ rows with all $k+1$ colours. Let $d$ be the row of coefficients of $[0,\ldots,k]$ in $h(g)$ for the rank-$k$ generators. There is a certificate exactly when some $a\in\ker M$ satisfies $da\ne0$. Elimination over the rationals returns integer coefficients after clearing denominators. Conversely any relative certificate in the universal bracket parameter is finite and lies in a finite dependency-closed subcomputad. Thus searching all well-typed finite coloured contexts with this test is exhaustive for this relative-degree obstruction.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_colour_degree.py`. The solver checks the literal context, computes its cellular differential and its simplex chains, and verifies the boundary and degree on the returned coefficients. On the typed three-cube, 174 of the 1,701 surjective four-colourings up to colour permutation give absolute degree one; the other 1,527 give zero relative image. Subdividing the top globe with a new full-colour square gives a two-generator certificate with coefficients $(1,1)$ and degree $-1$ for the minimum colouring. Each half separately and the constant colouring have zero relative image. These are checks in dimension three; a degree-six certificate would decide the corresponding six-seam case. The solver accepts `--input file.json` with the raw nested-array `context` of the repository's CaTT kernel, the object `colours` in declaration order, and the integer `degree`. [Typed relative-degree solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_colour_degree.py)

# Researcher Response 12

## Four consecutive membrane comparisons suffice for the six-seam ring

Number the twelve intermediate 4-cells $w_0,\ldots,w_{11}$ of the six-cube in cyclic order. Start at the common initial row, follow the negative hemisphere to the common final row $w_6$, then follow the positive hemisphere backwards. Use the ordered pairs of parallel 4-terms $(u_i,v_i)$ from round 6, Response 18, in their 57-vertex supports $M_i$. **If the specified comparisons $u_i\to v_i$ exist in any four consecutive supports, $\Gamma_6$ fails identity-image RC by degree eight.** This holds for every simultaneous choice of the facet-step terms allowed there. In particular, under RC through degree eight at least three distinct $M_i$ fail RC(4), with their specified pairs as witnesses. If precisely three fail for those pairs, their indices are spaced four apart. [The twelve pairs and their boundary formula](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#twelve-four-dimensional-comparisons-for-the-original-six-seams)

**Exact colouring test.** For $A\subseteq\{0,\ldots,11\}$, ask for a map from the 64 cube vertices to seven simplex colours with these properties. Each original cube facet misses a colour, the induced map of the cube boundary into the simplex boundary has nonzero degree, and every $M_i$ for $i\notin A$ misses a colour. The boundary map is obtained by mapping each facet into the convex face on its vertex colours. The existence of such a colouring is **equivalent to $A$ containing a run of four cyclically consecutive indices**.

For existence, choose a seed cube vertex $s$ and an ordering of the six axes. Give $s$ colour zero. Give any other vertex the position, from one to six, of the first axis on which it differs from $s$. The facets opposite $s$ omit zero and the facets through $s$ omit their own axis colour. Triangulate by the saturated flags starting at $s$ and flipping all six coordinates in succession. Exactly the flag flipping them in reverse priority order sees all seven colours. Its affine degree is $+1$ or $-1$; all other flags have zero simplex volume. The checker constructs a seed and order for each of the twelve four-runs whose full-colour membranes are exactly that run. For instance the run $4,5,6,7$ uses seed $33$ as a six-bit vertex and priority $(0,1,4,5,2,3)$ with zero-based axes.

For necessity, for each $A$ containing no run the checker finds a translated staircase triangulation with the following stronger property: every top simplex has its entire vertex set in some $M_i$ with $i\notin A$. Any proposed colouring then gives an affine extension over that triangulation with image in the simplex boundary. On every exterior cube facet it is carried by the face on the colours of that facet, so its boundary restriction is homotopic to the proposed boundary map. Its degree must be zero. These two finite certificates cover all possible colour functions because this latter argument uses only containment of vertices and convexity. The equivalence concerns the specified carrier test.

**Applying the test to the actual 4-terms.** In the universal bracket parameter the established 6-generator $G$ has cellular boundary the oriented sum of the twelve original facet generators plus $\sum_i\epsilon_i h_i$, with $h_i=[u_i,v_i]$. Each $h_i$ has cellular boundary zero by equality of the two strict 4-images. A filler $a_i:u_i\to v_i$ inside the 4-dimensional $M_i$ has zero 5-generator count. The literal bracket $[a_i,h_i]$ therefore has boundary $h_i$. If the four comparisons in a run exist, subtract their four signed columns from $G$ and relabel all vertices by the corresponding seven-colouring. Every remaining boundary generator is in the proper-colour union: this holds both for the original facets and for the eight uncorrected $h_i$. The result is a relative 6-cycle. Each remaining $h_i$ is itself a cycle in a single proper-colour face and contributes zero to its boundary degree. The original facet sum has degree $\pm1$ by the computation on the cubical boundary. Response 11 gives the claimed failure of RC. Three disjoint four-runs show the lower bound of three failed pairs under the contrary hypothesis; meeting every four-run with just three indices forces all three cyclic gaps to equal four.

**A 57-context sufficient test in dimension three.** For a run, take the union of the three-dimensional states reached in firing its four membranes. The gluing argument of round 6, Response 19 is local to each membrane: RC(3) and RC(4) on its states supply all its edge comparisons and hence its required 5-term. For starting indices $0$ through $11$ the union sizes are respectively $70,67,67,70,57,57,70,67,67,70,57,57$. Thus checking the two RC conditions on the **57 states of the run starting at 4** would already settle the six-seam case negatively; the same is true independently for starts 5, 10 or 11. Existence of the comparisons on any one of these runs remains open. [State enumeration and local gluing](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-19)

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_sweep_colours.py`. The solver recomputes the twelve supports from their signed facet sweeps. Among 46,080 signed priority colourings, exactly 4,032 have four full-colour membranes, 10,368 have five, 1,728 have six and 29,952 have twelve. It reports a witness for each four-run. On the negative side it tests simplex containment on the 720 flags in each of 32 staircase triangulations (opposite origins give the same triangulation). Of all 4,096 permissions, 1,465 contain a feasible run and every other one admits the triangulation obstruction. It enumerates the incident states separately to obtain the twelve counts above. These are incidence and degree checks. The boundary formula and relative-degree argument give the implication for arbitrary CaTT terms. [Membrane colouring solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_sweep_colours.py)

# Researcher Response 13

## A relative degree certificate is equivalent to one bracket at a point

Fix $k\ge2$, $J=\{0,\ldots,k\}$, and $d=k-1$. Call a $d$-term *based at vertex $0$* if both its literal $(d-1)$-faces are $1_0^{d-1}$, with $1_0^0=0$. Let $N(t)$ count the $d$-generators of $P(J)$ in the strict image of $t$. Let $\rho(t)$ retain only the entries of $N(t)$ whose generators use every colour. For each $d$-generator $g$, let $\ell(g)$ be the coefficient of the oriented facet $[1,\ldots,k]$ in the simplex chain $h(g)$ computed by the cone formula of the relative-degree test. Put $\omega(t)=\sum_g N(t)_g\ell(g)$.

**A nonzero relative $k$-degree occurs in $P(J)$ if and only if there are two based $d$-terms $A,B$ such that**
$$
                    \rho(A)=\rho(B),\qquad \omega(A)\ne\omega(B).
$$
In this case the *single generator* $[A,B]$ is a certificate of signed degree $\omega(B)-\omega(A)$. Moreover, *every* signed cellular $k$-chain in $P(J)$ has exactly the same cellular boundary as a single bracket between based terms. This includes all coefficients on the proper-colour generators. For $k=6$, either a pair satisfying the display or a successful relative chain from Response 11 therefore gives the other and disproves identity-image RC through degree eight on the six-seam ring.

**Constructing the based bracket.** Write a finite chain as a signed sum of $k$-generators $[u_j,v_j]$, repeating generators according to their multiplicities. For a negative summand swap its two terms. Its contribution to the boundary is now $N(v_j)-N(u_j)$ with the updated order. Treat each parallel pair separately. For $i=0,\ldots,d-1$, let $s,t$ be its current $i$-source and $i$-target. Whisker both terms on the left at cut $i$ by $[1_0^i,s]$ and on the right by $[t,1_0^i]$, using identical binary heads and parentheses on both terms. At $i=0$ any two objects admit a bracket. At $i>0$ the preceding cut made the two $(i-1)$-boundaries literally $1_0^{i-1}$. Hence $s,t,1_0^i$ are parallel and both brackets exist. The two outer $i$-faces of the padded terms are literally $1_0^i$: the $i$-boundaries of a binary paste at cut $i$ select the outer faces. The next step consequently preserves all previously fixed lower faces.

For $i<d-1$ the added brackets have dimension below $d$ and contribute zero to $N$. At the last cut the two brackets contribute exactly the same vector on both sides. Thus the based pair has the original signed difference. Compose all left terms at cut $d-1$ to make $A$, and compose all right terms in the same order to make $B$. These composites exist since every term is now an endomorphism of the same literal pole. Additivity gives $N(B)-N(A)=\partial w$ for the original chain $w$. For the zero chain one can take $A=B=1_0^d$. The bracket $[A,B]$ exists by definition of $P(J)$.

A chain is relative precisely when its boundary has zero full-colour entries. For a relative chain its boundary maps to a cycle in $\partial\Delta^k$. The coefficient of $[1,\ldots,k]$ in that cycle is its integral degree, since this facet has coefficient $+1$ in $\partial[0,\ldots,k]$. Equal cellular boundaries therefore give equal degrees. This proves the criterion in both directions. The last-cut cancellation permits auxiliary brackets even when strict loops have no weak representative. [Relative degree and cellular chains](#relative-simplex-degree-detects-one-bridge-seam-rings)

There is a dual way to test the remaining obstruction. **All relative degrees vanish if and only if one can assign rational numbers $\lambda_g$ to the full-colour $d$-generators such that every based $d$-term satisfies**
$$
                       \omega(t)=\sum_g \lambda_g\rho(t)_g.
$$
Indeed the count vectors of based terms form an additive monoid under the top composite, with the identity term representing zero. If all equal $\rho$-vectors have equal weight, the weight defines an additive map on the image monoid. It extends to its group of differences and then to the rational span. Extend this linear functional to the ambient direct sum by a basis extension. Conversely such a functional forces equal weight in every fibre. This concerns the relative-degree test alone. For six seams neither a nonzero-degree pair nor a functional satisfying all of these equations is established here.

**Validation.** On the minimum-coloured three-cube the construction preserves the entire signed boundary and gives one based bracket of relative degree $-1$. At rank six the checker combines two columns with opposite signs from distinct triples of vertices, verifies every common pole, and checks that exactly one coefficient $+1$ and one coefficient $-1$ survive. That example validates the rank-six conversion. It has zero relative degree. The emitted rank-six judgment was checked with Agda 2.8.0 in safe mode. [Based-bracket checker](../../../tools/relative_coherence_based_columns.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_based_columns.py --agda "$dir/BasedSix.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/BasedSix.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 14

## Localizing the three-dimensional hypotheses to 28 states and 19 states

Consider the six-cube coloured as in Response 12 with seed $5$ and zero-based axis priority $(0,1,2,3,5,4)$. Its full-colour membranes are the run $7,8,9,10$. Call a state $S_r$ full when its 42 vertices use all seven colours. Call an edge full when the 43 vertices of the one-ridge context $U_{r,a}$ use them all. Let $E$ consist of the states incident to full edges and $V$ of the full states. There are **28 states in $E$ and 19 in $V\subseteq E$**. The following conditions suffice to disprove identity-image RC through degree eight for $\Gamma_6$:

- RC(3) on the states in $E$.
- On states in $V$, a 5-term between each ordered parallel pair of 4-terms whose common strict image is the identity of a **full-colour** strict 3-cell.

Thus there are 141,309 strict labels in the second hypothesis, counted separately in their states. The weak terms over each such label range over all terms. The sufficiency proof allows missing comparisons elsewhere to occur explicitly in a relative degree certificate.

**Auxiliary comparisons and their degree.** Keep the original cubical generators and the lower choices of round 6, Responses 18–19. For the comparisons below, allow new 4-arrows in proper-colour states between parallel old 3-terms with equal strict image. Take both directions as needed. Give each the original strict identity on this common image, defining a strict evaluation $q$ extending the original one. Also allow new 5-arrows in proper-colour subcontexts between parallel 4-terms with equal $q$-image, in both directions. Each adjoined type is formed from the actual terms at that step. One can work with all such choices and retain the finite dependency closure of the finished terms.

Every new 4-generator has zero simplex 4-chain under the chain map of Response 11, since its boundary has zero old 3-generator count difference. On a 4-term the simplex 4-chain is therefore the linear combination given by its **old** 4-generator counts; evaluating by $q$ retains exactly these counts. Hence every new 5-generator also has zero simplex chain. This accounts even for a comparison between terms that use the new 4-arrows in different numbers. Its cellular boundary can involve those arrows, but its simplex 5-chain vanishes. All these new 5-generators have proper colour support.

**Full edges.** For a full $U=U_{r,a}$ use only old 3-terms and old 4-terms when constructing the normalized unary arrows and the transports at either end. Both incident states satisfy the first hypothesis. Every strict 3-cell of the old base $\Lambda=U_{\le3}$ is carried by an incident state, by the four-cube carrier calculation. Thus RC(3) holds on $\Lambda$, using the support lemma and injectivity of the included tableaux. The comparison of two parallel old base 4-terms with equal strict image can be supplied in both directions: if their 3-label is full, the carrying incident state is in $V$ and the second hypothesis applies. If proper, both terms are supported on the proper dependency closure of this label; use the auxiliary 5-arrows there.

For precision, the conditional 4-generator extension argument now gives comparisons between the old parallel unary 4-arrows over the ridge, using only the allowed 5-arrows. Its 4-normalizers use actual RC(3) of $\Lambda$. Its formal comparison $J(X)$ of two strict-equal 3-contexts with a fresh 3-variable exists in the old syntax, since the 3-generator extension theorem gives RC(3) on $\Lambda,X$. Depth-one naturality of this old $J(X)$ compares the two actions on the ridge. The use of RC(4) of $\Lambda$ in that argument identifies parallel old 4-terms after transport, including the boundary factors $J(A),J(B)$. Exactly these identifications were supplied in the preceding paragraph. The generic interchange and the strict replacement and exchange relations then give equality of normalized arrows. This argument takes place modulo the equivalence generated by the available 5-comparisons. Both directions of each added comparison are available, and the old structural comparisons have reverses; composing and whiskering the chosen directions gives an actual 5-term realizing the equality. No naturality in an adjoined comparison variable is assumed. [Conditional extension proof, §8](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md#8-a-conditional-4-dimensional-extension-theorem)

**Other edges and the four membranes.** Serialize the two 4-terms of each membrane and use the adjacent exchange squares as in round 6, Response 19. Fix the same representative at the required 2-poles for each state in that calculation. At a state in $E$ choose actual old invertible 4-transports by RC(3). At a state outside $E$ the support is proper, so one may use new 4-arrows for the two directed transports. The two composites compare to their identities via new 5-arrows in that state; the $q$-images agree. Transported zero steps compare to identities by the same procedure on proper states, and by the second hypothesis on full states. Thus all the chosen transports are inverse in the category modulo 5-comparisons. On a proper edge the two normalized unary steps have equal $q$-image, so a new 5-arrow supplies their comparison on that proper edge, including when they use the new transports. On a full edge the previous paragraph applies since its endpoints used old transports. The generic squares now equate the normalized paths across each adjacent swap. Connectedness of the linear extension graph gives a 5-term $a_i:u_i\to v_i$ on each of the four membranes, using only proper-colour 5-generators. [Serialization, square judgments and state supports](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-19)

Use the 6-column $G$ of Response 12 and attach a 6-cell from each $a_i$ to $h_i$ on the four membranes. Its cellular boundary is $h_i-N_5(a_i)$. In the signed corrected column, the remaining boundary has only the original facets, proper $h_j$, and the proper 5-generators in the $a_i$. The latter and the $h_j$ have zero simplex 5-chain. The boundary degree is therefore still the signed unit contributed by the original facets. This finite typed relative cycle satisfies Response 11. Equivalently Response 13 converts it to one based bracket.

**Sharpness of the two support counts for this rule.** Under any seven-colouring of nonzero boundary degree with proper exterior facets, every translated staircase triangulation has at least one top flag carrying all seven colours, by the oriented simplex degree calculation. Every state containing that flag is full, and both endpoints of every edge whose union contains the flag must be counted as incident to a full edge. In the ordinary triangulation the latter endpoint set has at least 28 elements for each of its 720 flags. For the state count the checker considers the first five translated triangulations, with origins $0,1,2,3,4$ as bitmasks. Starting with the empty set, take a union with the containing-state set of one flag from each successive triangulation, discarding unions bigger than 18 and any strict superset of a retained choice. No candidate survives the fifth triangulation. Thus 19 and 28 are separately optimal for this support rule, and the displayed colouring attains both. These lower bounds concern which states and edges can miss a colour. They impose no lower bound on how many RC instances a different proof must use.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_state_colours.py`. It identifies the 148 states by their distinct 64-bit vertex masks in increasing order, computes both subsets from all 264 edges, and verifies the lower bounds on translated flags. It then closes the signed atom tableaux under every defined strict composition at rank three in the 19 full states and checks the vertex colours in the closures of all tableau rows. Of 758,393 strict labels in these states, 141,309 are full-colour. The script prints the state indices and individual label counts. The finite calculation verifies the localization and its counts; RC on the indicated terms remains open. [State and strict-label solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_state_colours.py)

# Researcher Response 15

## RC in degree three for arbitrary raw terms under the tricategorical hypotheses

Suppose the generating 1-graph is acyclic and the two strict path boundaries of every generating 2-cell are nonempty. Then **any two parallel raw 3-terms with the same strict image admit an identity-image 4-term in each direction**. Composite attaching terms are allowed. In particular this supplies RC(3) on all 28 states required by Response 14. For its six-seam decision only the indicated RC(4) comparisons on 19 states remain.

The two inputs are item 36 for *biased* tricategorical expressions and RC(3) on the partial cylinders of *globular pasting arities* in item 3. The latter is available for arbitrary up-closed selections, including the selection of all positive-dimensional variables. The use of cylinders below permits the translated and untranslated operations to be different at both ends. [Biased faithfulness and its interpretation in raw terms](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-6) · [Cylinder theorem and verification](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md#verification-of-the-researcher-response-2026-11-18)

**Translation of heads.** Fix the raw choices interpreting biased tricategories in the quotient of parallel 3-terms by 4-terms in both directions, as in item 36. For each legal generic CaTT head $h$ through rank three over a pasting arity $P$, choose a biased representative $Nh$ with the same strict value, its two raw faces exactly the translations of the faces of $h$. In rank one use a binary path expression or an identity. In rank two the generic strict pasting is a composite of whiskered generating 2-cells and identities. Use their biased operations and adjust path bracketings by associators and unitors, including their reverse constraints. This also realizes an identity over a path with prescribed biased bracketings. In rank three the tricategory operad $W_3$ has an operation over the prescribed globular arity between the two biased operations over its strict faces. Use the fixed raw interpretation of that operation. This applies to the identity-promoted arities as well. At every rank the chosen faces are parallel by induction. [Griffiths, §9](https://arxiv.org/abs/2101.00077) · [Choice of the raw representatives](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#returning-the-tricategory-equality-to-catt-syntax)

Translate a variable to its corresponding variable and a labelled head $h[\sigma]$ to the chosen raw $Nh$ substituted by the translated label. On types translate the two terms in each arrow. This prescription commutes *literally* with substitution by term induction. In particular one uses the already chosen operation when substituting a label into a term. The raw interpretation of a biased expression resulting from this translation represents its biased value in the quotient of item 36. Every strict value is preserved.

**Mixed cylinders on the generic arities.** Write $C(P)=P\uparrow\operatorname{Var}_{>0}(P)$, with its objects fixed. For every term $t$ over $P$ through rank three we construct a comparison $e_t$ in $C(P)$ from the minus instance of $t$ to the plus instance of $Nt$, with the following whiskerings. On a variable it is the mixed variable of the cylinder. For a 1-term the endpoints are just $t^-$ and $(Nt)^+$. For a 2-term $A:f\to g$ they are
$$
 A^- *_1 e_g,\qquad e_f *_1 (NA)^+ .
$$
For a 3-term $t:A\to B$ with $A,B:f\to g$, they are
$$
 (t^- *_1 e_g)*_2 e_B,\qquad e_A *_2(e_f *_1 (Nt)^+).
$$
Here the binary heads use the fixed hom compositions, with ordinary mixed-dimensional whiskering. The displayed endpoints are parallel literally by the face formulas for those heads. We use these formulas for every positive term, even one supported entirely on objects. This convention also covers labels that specialize an active slot to an identity.

First construct the comparison for a generic head. Its displayed lower changes have already been constructed in smaller degree. Its required strict image is the cylinder cell of $\pi h$. Specifically, apply $I\otimes(-)$ to the strict globe selecting $\pi h$ in $F(P)$, then apply the quotient fixing the objects of $P$. Take the image of the mixed top generator of the globe cylinder. Its strict faces are exactly the displayed expressions, by the cylinder face formulas and the inductively specified strict images. The two endpoint operations $h$ and $Nh$ have the same strict value, as required at the plus end. The partial cylinder presents this strict quotient by item 9.

Boundary lifting through degree four holds in $C(P)$. Indeed it has RC through degree three by the cylinder theorem. It is strong Steiner, so those comparisons have identity image: a nonempty positive top chain of an endomorphism cannot have zero differential. In a nonempty positive chain choose a maximal basis element for the strong loop-free order. A positive face exists by unitality and could cancel only against a negative face of a strictly later element. The corrected strict-expression lifting induction of Response 7 therefore provides a term over the specified cylinder cell with the required literal boundaries. Only comparisons through the rank of the head are used. Choose this term as $e_h$.

For a labelled head, substitute in $e_h$ the minus labels, their translated plus labels, and the comparisons of its positive-dimensional arguments. Here is the compatibility check. A globular variable has precisely the mixed type displayed above. For a label, its required mixed type is consequently obtained by substituting in those same expressions on its two faces. On variables the resulting comparison is the supplied mixed label. For a composite the equation $e_{s[\sigma]}=e_s[\sigma^{\mathrm{mixed}}]$ follows by composing substitutions on the fixed generic comparison and applying the equation to its proper arguments. The endpoint equation uses literal substitution for $N$. These two inductions, in declaration order for a label, give the required inferred types. Over another pasting arity the strict image is again the cylinder of the substituted strict cell by functoriality of the Gray tensor and the fixed-object quotient. Equivalently the induced map on strict cylinders agrees on the two copies and on every mixed generator. This proves the strict-image assertion used at the next head. The construction uses smaller-degree faces for the generic choice and proper subterms for the arguments of an instance.

**Applying it to a 2-presentation.** Let $\Gamma$ first have dimension at most two. Form $\Gamma'$ by translating each attaching type and using the same generator names. Its attaching paths are biased and the identification of its strict presentation with that of $\Gamma$ preserves all generator names. Interpret $\Gamma'$ back in $\Gamma$ by a substitution $b$. Keep objects and arrows. For each 2-generator whisker the old generator at cut one with invertible path rebracketings at its two ends to give it the translated attaching type. Such rebracketings between arbitrary raw paths of equal strict value can be taken over their linear path arity. Thus the image under $b$ still has the original strict value.

We can now label the mixed construction in $\Gamma$ with endpoints $t$ and $bNt$. On a generating arrow use its identity as change. On a generating 2-cell the required two 2-boundaries are parallel by the displayed formula, with the same strict value since all lower changes have identity image. Item 1 gives their 3-comparison over the strict identity, as in Response 7. This supplies every generator change needed in a 2-presentation. Evaluating generic mixed comparisons on these labels defines $e_t$ for **every** raw $t$ through rank three with the displayed boundaries in $\Gamma$. At the strict level all selected generator changes are identities: on each labelled arity the strict cylinder map factors through collapse to the common strict label. Hence the evaluated changes all have identity strict image. In particular the changes $e_f,e_g$ of 1-terms are invertible 2-cells. Their strict image contains no 2-generator, so the support lemma and structural invertibility apply. The changes $e_A,e_B$ of 2-terms are invertible 3-cells in this 2-dimensional context.

For parallel raw $t,t':A\to B$ of equal strict value, $Nt,Nt'$ are parallel biased expressions in $\Gamma'$ with equal strict value. Apply item 36 there and substitute $b$. To cancel the side changes, work in the hom bicategory of the interpreted tricategory of $\Gamma$, with parallel 3-terms identified exactly when 4-terms exist in both directions. The two mixed 4-comparisons are invertible in this 2-dimensional context and identify the whiskered images of $t,t'$ with the corresponding expressions in $bNt,bNt'$. These latter expressions agree by the biased comparison. Cancel the common invertible 3-cell $e_B$ by vertical composition with its inverse. Whiskering by the equivalence $e_g$ in the hom bicategory is faithful on 3-cells with fixed boundaries: whiskering back by its weak inverse is naturally isomorphic to the identity by the unit, cancellation and associativity isomorphisms. Hence $t,t'$ are equal in this quotient. This yields actual 4-terms between the original literal poles in both directions. The calculation uses the binary hom operations of the raw tricategory interpretation, so cancellation is after passage to the specified quotient of 3-cells. Every witnessing 4-term in the 2-presentation has identity strict image.

**Consequences.** For a 3-presentation satisfying the hypotheses, the acyclic 3-generator extension theorem of item 4 transfers RC(3) of its 2-skeleton to the entire presentation. All the resulting 4-terms over parallel terms of equal value have identity image by dimension. Higher generators can then be retained by inclusion, since they do not change strict 3-cells or raw 3-terms. This proves the assertion for raw syntax in item 36, and gives $\mathrm{RC}_{\mathrm{id}}(3)$ for every finite strong-Steiner context. In dimension two, the positive-polyplex reduction of item 11 also gives RC in degree three for every positive 2-presentation, including ones with directed cycles in the generating graph. For $\Gamma_6$ the second hypothesis of Response 14 alone now suffices. A failure there would be a failure of RC(4) on a three-dimensional strong-Steiner context. Neither outcome of that remaining test is proved here.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_mixed_cylinder.py`. It constructs every fixed-object cylinder of a planar tree of height at most three with at most six edges (145 arities), checks the literal types of 1,198 mixed declarations, and substitutes the stationary changes from their generic discs to check collapse. It separately constructs both directed 4-terms for a raw ternary path head changing to a binary bracketing with different 1- and 2-dimensional side changes. It verifies their exact boundaries after substituting an identity slot and loop powers one through seven. Agda 2.8.0 checks both directions and the power-three specializations. The finite calculation checks the whiskering and substitution conventions. Existence for every generic 3-head uses the all-arity cylinder theorem above. [Mixed-cylinder solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_mixed_cylinder.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_mixed_cylinder.py --agda "$dir/MixedCylinder.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/MixedCylinder.agda"

# Researcher Response 16

## A two-dimensional bubble is exactly the obstruction to identity-image RC in degree three

For a finite CaTT context $\Gamma$ of **any** dimension,
$$
 \mathrm{RC}_{\mathrm{id}}(3)(\Gamma)
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 F(\Gamma_{\le2})\text{ has no nonidentity 2-cell }1_x\Rightarrow1_x .
$$
Both empty input paths and empty output paths are allowed, including in the same presentation, and the generating 1-graph may have loops. For a two-dimensional context this characterizes homotopicality by RC in degree three. In dimensions at most three the unrestricted and identity-image versions of RC(3) coincide. In higher dimensions the displayed assertion is about the identity-image version. Items 17–18 give the implication from RC to bubble-freeness, including after arbitrary higher generators are adjoined. We prove the converse.

**Exchanges when ports can be empty.** In the free Gray-category on the 2-skeleton, a 2-cell is a word of individual generating 2-cells in path contexts. Draw its progressive planar diagram, with the boundary wires retained, the regions labelled by objects, and the vertices labelled by 2-generators. A component containing a vertex and meeting neither the input nor the output would give a nonidentity strict bubble. Indeed, take that connected component by itself. Its regions carry the original object names along their boundary walks: one can follow a collar of each walk disjoint from the other components. In particular the exterior has a single name $x$. Reading the component upwards gives a 2-cell from the empty path at $x$ to itself, with positive generator count. Thus in a bubble-free presentation every vertex has a path to a boundary. An identity diagram has no exchanges.

Use one orientation of independent adjacent exchanges, say moving the later box earlier when it lies to the right of the earlier one. In offset notation, if the earlier box starts at $a$, has $m$ inputs and $n$ outputs, and the later box starts at $b$, this move is allowed at $a+n\le b$. The new offsets are $b+m-n$ and $a$. Equality accounts for an empty gap; when two exchanges are spatially possible, their placements are distinguished. Each interchange between individual boxes is a right exchange or the inverse of one. Forgetting names gives the same right reduction of the underlying planar diagram. Delpeuch and Vicary prove termination for boundary-connected diagrams (Theorem 2.14 and Corollary 2.16). Their local confluence proof gives a square for exchanges of disjoint pairs and a hexagon for overlapping pairs (Lemma 4.1). Both residual diagrams retain the original labels because they just change the heights of spatially independent boxes. [Delpeuch and Vicary, §§2 and 4](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1804.07832)

These local joins are **equalities of Gray 3-arrows**. For a square, the two 3-arrows act on disjoint factors of a vertical word; composition in the hom 2-category gives the equality. For a hexagon the three boxes have a common horizontal placement in their spatial order, with stationary words between and outside them. For instance if consecutive right exchanges are available, the first box ends on the left of the second, and the third begins on the right of the second's outputs. Moving their heights puts all three in that horizontal factorization, including equality at the ports. On the generic horizontal triple of 2-discs the two three-interchange routes agree by the Gray interchange coherence (equivalently the generic positive three-slot pasting theorem). Substitute the three boxes, with stationary whiskers, into this equality. This substitution allows identity 1-faces and repeated labels. Thus no assumption about nonempty ports enters the Gray equation. [Generic Gray pasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2205.15486)

Choose a normalizing right path at each diagram. Well-founded induction on right reduction shows that for every right edge $e:D\to D'$ the chosen path at $D$ equals $e$ followed by the chosen path at $D'$. Compare the first steps via the local square or hexagon and use the induction hypothesis at their reducts. Inverses then give the same result for arbitrary zigzags. Every structural Gray 3-arrow is such a zigzag because tensorators on composite 2-cells expand into elementary tensorators by their composition axioms. Consequently between two fixed words over one strict 2-cell there is exactly one structural 3-arrow. Existence uses the presentation of strict 2-cells by words modulo exchanges. This argument covers arbitrary inactive diagrams and arbitrarily long paths around 1-loops: the condition needed for termination is boundary-connectedness of the whole finite diagram, which was proved for every diagram in this presentation.

**Biased 3-cells.** Now allow arbitrary generating 3-cells with biased attaching expressions. They do not change the objects or arrows through dimension two, or the equalities proved between structural interchanges. In each hom quotient the 2-cells by the structural subgroupoid and identify 3-arrows by pre- and postcomposition with its unique changes of representatives. For fixed representatives the map on 3-arrows is bijective: transport any quotient arrow back along the unique changes, independently of its presentation. The quotient operations are strict; whiskerings preserve structural changes and the naturality of the tensorator makes horizontal composition descend to the cartesian product. Its generating presentation is exactly the original strict one by freeness. Hence the free Gray-category maps faithfully to the free strict category on parallel 3-arrows. The formal-composites triequivalences and generator section used in canonical Gray terms, round 6, Response 6, now give the same faithfulness for biased tricategorical expressions. The generating graph and boundary path hypotheses in that proof were used for thinness of structural interchanges, which was just proved here. The section lifts generators at their already chosen formal boundaries, also when those are identities. [Quotient and transfer to biased expressions](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#the-strict-comparison-on-3-cells)

**Transporting a 3-generator without changing its strict label.** Work in $\Gamma_{\le3}$. Use the translation $N$, the mixed generic cylinders, and the interpretation $b$ of the translated 2-skeleton from Response 15. The construction through that stage used no hypothesis on this skeleton. To extend $b$ to a generator $x:A\to B$ with $A,B:f\to g$, write $A'=bNA$, $B'=bNB$. In the hom bicategory of the interpreted tricategory, its 2-cells are raw 3-terms modulo comparisons in both directions in $\Gamma_{\le3}$. The changes $e_f,e_g$ are equivalences, and $e_A,e_B$ are invertible there: their construction took place on the 2-skeleton. In execution order there is a 3-arrow in this quotient
$$
 e_A^{-1} *_2 (x *_1 e_g) *_2 e_B:
             e_f *_1 A'\longrightarrow e_f *_1 B' .
$$
Whiskering by $e_f$ is fully faithful on the hom category containing $A',B'$, since whiskering by a weak inverse is a quasi-inverse. Choose a raw representative $x':A'\to B'$ of the preimage and interpret the translated generator by $b(Nx)=x'$. By the definition of equality in this quotient there are actual 4-comparisons in both directions between $(x *_1 e_g)*_2 e_B$ and $e_A *_2(e_f *_1 x')$. Choose the forward one as the mixed generator change. Projection to the strict category sends all lower changes to identities, so $\pi x'=\pi x$; the 4-change has the strict identity value by dimension. This construction also works for an irreversible $x$.

Extend to all terms by the mixed substitution equation of Response 15. The translated 3-presentation has the same strict generators and attaching cells. Faithfulness for biased expressions therefore identifies $Nt,Nt'$ for parallel raw 3-terms of equal strict image. Interpret back by $b$, then cancel the common invertible boundary changes as in Response 15. The mixed 4-terms are invertible in the 3-skeleton. This gives the required raw 4-terms in both directions there, hence over the strict identity. Inclusion into $\Gamma$ proves the claimed converse. By items 8 and 17 this also proves, for finite 2-presentations, $\mathrm{RC}(3)\Longleftrightarrow\text{homotopical}$. Deciding RC in all higher degrees for these presentations remains open.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_boundary_exchange.py`. Its example has one object, loops $f,g$, a birth $1\to f$ and a death $g\to1$ in rank two. Any run starting at the empty path can produce only $f$'s, which it cannot consume, so the example is bubble-free. For the four specified initial paths and event counts the solver generates 12, 78, 570 and 246 words, checks every directed exchange and local branching, and obtains respectively 1, 1, 1 and 6 normal forms. On input $gg$ with three births and two deaths it checks 1,248 exchanges, 513 squares and 366 hexagons. It separately builds both raw directions of a three-slot hexagon, checks the literal judgement after substituting empty ports and positive flank powers one through seven, and transports a 3-generator between two distinct births. Agda checks both hexagon directions at power two and both mixed comparisons for the transported generator. The global termination and Gray equations above supply the unbounded statement. [Empty-port exchange and transport solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_boundary_exchange.py)

python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_boundary_exchange.py --agda "$dir/BoundaryExchange.agda"

# Researcher Response 17

## At least seven distinct three-dimensional states would have to fail RC(4)

If the six-seam ring has identity-image RC through degree eight, **at least seven of the 148 three-dimensional strong-Steiner states fail RC(4)**. There must also be at least seven distinct strict labels with failing comparisons, even when the same label in different states is counted only once. In the other direction, for the fixed colouring of Response 14 it suffices to establish the comparisons over **2,749 labels on two-dimensional supports and 56,233 labels on three-dimensional supports**. The statements concern every ordered pair of parallel raw 4-terms over the indicated identities.

For any signed-priority colouring of Response 12, let $V$ be the set of states seeing all seven colours. The proof of Response 14 applies to this colouring: use its edge comparisons on **every** full-colour membrane, and correct the column $G$ with all those membrane comparisons. Every uncorrected membrane is proper by definition, as are the auxiliary 5-generators. The exterior facets still have signed degree one up to orientation. RC(3) is available on every state by Response 15. Therefore, if every full-colour label in $V$ admits its RC(4) comparisons, Response 11 disproves the six-seam hypothesis. This argument also applies to any colouring with proper exterior facets and nonzero boundary degree.

For the 46,080 signed-priority colourings, the sets $V$ take 475 distinct values, 72 of which are inclusion-minimal. The least size of a set of states meeting them all is **seven**. To reproduce the lower bound, retain the minimal constraints and choose the first as-yet-unmet one. Branch on every state in it, dropping exactly those constraints containing that state. The empty list succeeds. A nonempty list fails if the budget is exhausted, or if the number of remaining members of a precomputed pairwise-disjoint subfamily exceeds the budget. Every hitting set contains a state in the chosen constraint, which proves completeness. No branch succeeds with six states. The indices $2,12,32,77,83,105,130$ meet all 475 sets. This last assertion establishes sharpness of the count for the signed-priority family; it does not establish failure of any RC comparison.

For any strict label in a state, take the dependency closure of all faces in its tableau, using their common declarations in the cube. Every pair over the label belongs to this subcontext by the support lemma. Any 5-term between the pair in a state has strict identity image by dimension and therefore also belongs to this subcontext. Consequently failure is independent of which carrying state is used. Choose one carrying state for every distinct failing label. Every signed-priority colouring makes at least one failing label full, so its chosen state belongs to $V$. The same seven-state lower bound therefore applies to the number of distinct failing labels.

Finally, for seed 5 and priority $(0,1,2,3,5,4)$, the solver closes the atom tableaux under strict composition in the 19 full states. At support dimensions one, two and three it finds respectively 19, 18,129 and 123,161 full-colour occurrences. It compares the entire strict tableaux whenever their dependency closures coincide, obtaining respectively **1, 2,749 and 56,233 distinct labels**. The one-dimensional support is a directed cube path, hence a globular pasting context, so item 2 supplies all its comparisons. The support argument covers arbitrary terms over the other labels, including arbitrary syntactic identity insertions.

Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_state_obstructions.py` to reproduce the transversal search, check its witness against all 475 sets, and recompute the strict labels. [State obstruction solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_state_obstructions.py)

# Researcher Response 18

## A tree computes the cube boundary degree, and the seven-state bound is sharp for all colourings

Let $k\ge2$. Give the vertices of a $k$-cube colours in $J=\{0,\ldots,k\}$, with each exterior facet missing at least one colour. Choose one omitted colour $a_j^-$ and $a_j^+$ on the two facets along each axis $j$. Form a multigraph on $J$ with the $k$ edges $(a_j^-,a_j^+)$. **The boundary degree has absolute value one if this graph is a tree and zero otherwise.** This holds for every choice of the omitted colours, including when some facets miss several colours.

**Duality.** On every nonempty proper cube face $F$ let $A(F)$ be the set of its vertex colours and $O(F)$ the chosen omissions on all facets containing it. The inclusions $A(F)\subseteq J\setminus O(F)$ define a pointwise comparison of increasing maps of face posets. Their realizations on the barycentric subdivision are homotopic by the order homotopy (triangulate each chain times the unit interval, changing from the first map to the second in order). The first map is homotopic to the boundary map in Response 12 since both carry each face into the simplex on its colours. Complementing subsets induces a homeomorphism of the subdivided simplex boundary. Reversing the cube face order identifies its subdivision with the boundary subdivision of the dual cross-polytope. In this identification $F\mapsto O(F)$ is exactly the subdivided simplicial map sending a dual vertex to the omitted colour on its facet. Each dual simplex has proper image since its facets share a cube vertex. Thus primal and dual degrees agree in absolute value.

Orient each edge of the graph from $a_j^-$ to $a_j^+$. The coefficient of the face $[1,\ldots,k]$ in the dual image of the fundamental cycle is, up to orientation, the determinant of the matrix with columns $e_{a_j^+}-e_{a_j^-}$ after deleting row zero. Indeed expanding by columns chooses one endpoint per axis, with opposite signs for the two choices; exactly the choices listing $1,\ldots,k$ contribute, with their permutation sign. These are the oriented facets of the cross-polytope. For a disconnected graph the rows of a component missing vertex zero sum to zero. For a tree, expand along a leaf different from zero and induct, obtaining determinant of absolute value one. A connected graph on $k+1$ vertices with $k$ edges must be a tree. This proves the formula in every dimension.

**Exact test with extra supports.** Given any family of vertex sets on which the colouring must also be proper, build the nerve whose vertices are the exterior facets and the chosen sets, with a simplex for each collection having a common cube vertex. Nonzero degree is possible exactly when one can label these nerve vertices by $J$ so that each cube vertex's entire incident collection misses some label, and the axis pairs on the exterior form a tree. In one direction assign an omission to every set. In the other direction colour each cube vertex by any label missing from its incident collection. All the omissions hold simultaneously, and the determinant supplies the degree. In particular, if the exterior cross-polytope cycle bounds modulo two in this nerve, no such colouring exists: the labelling would map the nerve simplicially into the simplex boundary and carry that cycle to its nonzero mod-two fundamental class.

**Application to the six-seam test.** Use the seven particular state indices $2,12,32,77,83,105,130$ from Response 17 as the chosen sets. The nerve consists of the subsets of the 64 incident collections, each containing six exterior facets and whichever of those seven states contain the cube vertex. The solver constructs a sum of 340 six-simplices over $\mathbb F_2$. Recomputing its boundary gives exactly the 64 exterior five-simplices, each once modulo two. Consequently every proper-facet colouring of nonzero degree makes at least one of these seven states full. Conversely, for **any** six states, Response 17 provides a signed-priority colouring making them all proper. Seven is therefore the exact minimum number of states meeting the full-state set of every permissible colouring, without a priority restriction. This is sharpness of the support argument; it leaves the actual RC(4) comparisons in those states open.

Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_facet_omissions.py`. In dimensions two and three it independently computes the primal degree on the staircase triangulation for every surjective proper-facet colouring up to permuting the names. It checks respectively 10 and 1,666 choices of omissions against both the dual degree and the tree condition. In dimension six it enumerates all 13,636 five-simplices and 17,542 six-simplices of the specified nerve, solves for a chain, and checks its boundary by counting the faces of the returned simplices directly. These are calculations on the parameter's vertex sets; the implication for relative coherence uses the raw comparisons and degree argument of Responses 11, 14 and 17. [Facet omission and nerve solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_facet_omissions.py)

# Researcher Response 19

## Integral degree potentials and a single-term divisibility test for every one-bridge ring

Let $Q$ be any finite typed parameter with $k+1$ object colours, $k\ge2$. Write $M$ for the cellular boundary from degree $k$ to degree $k-1$ after keeping only full-colour rows. Let $d$ be the row of signed simplex degrees on the $k$-generators, computed by the integral cone formula of Response 11. **If the one-bridge ring $\Gamma_k$ has identity-image RC through degree $k+2$, there is an integral row $h$ with $hM=d$.** Hence it suffices to find a chain $x$ and an integer $m\ge2$ such that $Mx=0\pmod m$ and $dx\ne0\pmod m$. The criterion includes prime powers. For $\Gamma_6$ it gives the following single-term search: find one based raw 5-term $C$ on the seven colours for which
$$
              \gcd\bigl(\rho(C)_g:g\text{ full-colour}\bigr)
                      \quad\text{does not divide}\quad \omega(C).
$$
Use $0\mid a$ exactly when $a=0$. Here $\rho$ and $\omega$ are the literal-generator count vector and signed facet weight of Response 13. Finding such a term would disprove RC through degree eight.

**Coefficients and completeness of the finite test.** The glued-flag construction of Response 11 works with coefficients in $\mathbb Z/m$ for every $m$. Given $x$ as above, its relative boundary vanishes there. In the signed flag sum, boundary coefficients on full generators vanish modulo $m$ in each copy. Every proper boundary generator missing an interior rank still cancels with its adjacent-transposition partner; the rest belong to the exterior pair. The top relative class in each oriented staircase simplex has coefficient $dx$ times its orientation. Its boundary in the cube boundary sphere has that same degree modulo $m$, by the boundary isomorphism of the cube pair. Under the six-seam construction, or its $k$-seam version, this cycle bounds in the sphere by the map supplied by RC. The carried homotopy on the exterior identifies the two boundary classes, a contradiction. This argument also covers integral relative cycles.

For the converse linear-algebra statement about a **fixed finite parameter**, take unimodular integer operations reducing $M$ to a rectangular diagonal $LMR$. Divisibility between diagonal entries is unnecessary. Write $d'=dR$. A nonzero $d'_j$ at a zero column gives an integral cycle using column $j$ of $R$. At a nonzero diagonal $a_j$ which fails to divide $d'_j$, the same column of $R$ gives a cycle modulo $|a_j|$ with nonzero degree: multiplication by the unimodular $L^{-1}$ preserves divisibility of the boundary. Otherwise the row with coordinates $d'_j/a_j$ at the pivots and zeros elsewhere, multiplied by $L$, provides the asserted integral $h$. Conversely such a row forces the degree of every relative cycle to vanish over every coefficient ring. This proves the integral necessity using only finite matrices. Solvability on one parameter settles precisely its own test.

**One term suffices.** Send each generator of $Q$ to the bracket of its two mapped literal faces in $P(J)$ and each object to its colour. The vertex support of this bracket is the union of the supports of those faces, so proper and full generators keep their colour sets. The integral simplex chains commute with this map by induction on rank using the same coning vertex and the boundary equation. Thus a finite modular certificate persists in $P(J)$. Convert its signed integer chain to one bracket $[A,B]$ on based terms by Response 13 *before* reducing coefficients. Then $\rho(B)-\rho(A)=0\pmod m$ and $\omega(B)-\omega(A)\ne0\pmod m$. Compose $B$ with $m-1$ copies of $A$ at the top cut. The result $C$ is still based and satisfies $\rho(C)=0\pmod m$, $\omega(C)\ne0\pmod m$. For a nonzero integral relative degree, choose any modulus that does not divide it. Conversely for any $C$ passing the gcd test, its bracket from the iterated identity to $C$ gives a modular certificate using the gcd if positive, or an integral one if zero. All these constructions have finite dependency closure.

**An unbounded family distinguishing the tests.** Start with the minimum-coloured typed three-cube and write $u,w:f\Rightarrow g$ for its two hemispheres. Its decreasing path $f$ sees all four colours. In the boundary context adjoin $b:g\Rightarrow f$ and $z:f\Rightarrow f$. For $m,r\ge1$ use one new 3-generator with literal faces $(u*_1 b)^r$ and $(w*_1 b)^r *_1 z^m$, taking powers by successive binary composites. In the signed boundary the $b$-counts cancel, the square counts give $r$ times the proper exterior boundary, and $z$ occurs $m$ times. Its own simplex chain is zero because its faces agree. Thus $M=(m)$ up to zero rows and $|d|=r$. The integral condition is exactly $m\mid r$. The rational cycle test always vanishes here. For $(m,r)=(p^2,p)$ every prime-field test vanishes and the prime-square test succeeds. This is a family of typed parameter examples in degree three.

**Reproduction and current rank-six result.** The solver constructs all 44 examples with $2\le m\le9$ and $1\le r\le m$. For $(4,2)$ it also maps to the universal bracket parameter, constructs the single based term, and recomputes its four nonzero full-colour counts $4,8,4,4$ and its signed degree $6$. Agda checks its bracket from the literal identity. On the researcher's 16 rank-six interval shortcuts the solver finds an actual integral potential, assigning $-1$ to generator 65 (zero-based declaration order) and zero to the other full-colour generators. It verifies $hM=d$ separately on every column. This rules out **every modulus** on those 16 columns; it leaves the search for a seven-colour term in the displayed criterion open. [Integral solver and typed generators](../../../tools/relative_coherence_integral_degree.py)

dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_integral_degree.py --agda "$dir/IntegralDegree.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/IntegralDegree.agda"
~~~
