# Researcher Response 20

## The integral degree test reduces to head equations with labels two ranks below the terms

Fix $k\ge3$, put $r=k-2$, and use the universal bracket parameter $P(0,\ldots,k)$. The finite integral obstructions of Response 19 can be found by solving integer affine equations whose free input labels have dimensions **at most $r-1$**. In particular, for seven colours the search can generate arbitrary pasting heads labelled through **degree three**. A finite inconsistent set of these equations suffices to disprove identity-image RC through degree eight for $\Gamma_6$. Conversely, every finite obstruction from Response 19 forces a finite inconsistent set. The arities and the number of equations remain unbounded.

**From generator weights to values on terms.** Write $H(t)$ for the integral simplex chain of a term, obtained by summing the chains of its top generators with their strict multiplicities. For a $(k-1)$-term $U$, $\omega(U)$ is its coefficient on $[1,\ldots,k]$. An integral row $h$ on full-colour $(k-1)$-generators defines $L(U)=\omega(U)-h\rho(U)$. The equation for $[U,V]$ is precisely $L(U)=L(V)$ for parallel $U,V$, by the boundary coefficient of the oriented $k$-simplex. For an $r$-term $t$ let $a(t)=[\sigma t,\tau t]$ be the unique bracket variable of its literal type. Put $p(t)=L([a(t),t])$. Comparing $[a(t),t]*_r U$ with $[a(t),t']$ gives
$$
                    L(U)=p(t')-p(t)\qquad(U:t\to t').
$$
In particular $p(a(t))=0$ by comparison with the identity. On a proper-colour $t$ this forces $p(t)=b(t):=\omega([a(t),t])$.

**Unknowns and recursion.** For an arbitrary $r$-head $D$ with a fixed substitution on slots below rank $r$, replace each rank-$r$ argument by the bracket variable of its required type. This gives a term $t_0$ determined by the head and the lower substitution. For each such $t_0$ of full colour introduce one integer $\beta(t_0)$. On a proper-colour $t_0$ use the fixed integer $b(t_0)$ instead. Set $p$ to zero on $r$-variables. On $t=D[\gamma]$ the recursion is
$$
               p(t)=\beta(t_0)+\sum_{i:\,\dim i=r}p(\gamma_i),
$$
with the stated substitution of $b$ in the proper case. To see necessity, vary all rank-$r$ slots from their anchors to the actual terms by the primitive brackets $[a(\gamma_i),\gamma_i]$. These slots are maximal. The cylinder at the selected maximal variables is a pasting arity by item 14. Its comparison for $D$ uses each varying top generator once, since its strict composite is the composite of that cylinder. All lower labels are stationary. Applying the formula for $L$ yields the recursion. With no rank-$r$ slots the term already is $t_0$.

The recursion gives $p(t)=b(t)$ on every proper term, independently of the free integers. Indeed $t,t_0$ are parallel, share an anchor, and all the terms in the following calculation use only colours of $t$. The chain difference $H(t)-H(t_0)$ is the sum of $H(\gamma_i)-H(a(\gamma_i))$ over the top slots. Thus the $(r+1)$-chains of $[a(t),t]$ and of $[a(t),t_0]$ plus the slot brackets have identical boundary. Boundary is injective in that degree on a simplex of dimension at most $r+1$. Taking the facet coefficient proves the assertion by recursion. The same argument on the support of any proper generator $[s,t]$ gives $\omega([s,t])=p(t)-p(s)$.

**Which equations to impose.** For every $(r+1)$-head and a substitution $\gamma$, write $U$ for its value. Require
$$
 p(\tau U)-p(\sigma U)=\sum_{z:\,\dim z=r+1}
                 \bigl(p(\tau\gamma_z)-p(\sigma\gamma_z)\bigr).                 \tag{*}
$$
It suffices to label the arity only through rank $r-1$, then put the unique bracket over the required faces in each slot of rank $r$ and $r+1$. Here is why arbitrary $r$-labels cancel. With their lower labels fixed, changing them from anchors to $\gamma_i$ changes $p(w[\gamma])$ for any generic $r$-term $w$ by $\sum_i N_i(w)p(\gamma_i)$. This holds on variables. At each nested head its lower substitution is unchanged, so its constant is fixed and the equality follows by summing over top slots. On the two sides of $(*)$ the coefficient of each $p(\gamma_i)$ agrees: the boundary count of the strict composite of the arity is the sum of the boundary counts of its $(r+1)$-variables. Slots of rank $r+1$ enter only by their faces. This proves the reduction for arbitrary inactive labels and arbitrary powers occurring in them.

Conversely, given values solving the equations, assign $h_g=\omega(g)-p(\tau g)+p(\sigma g)$ on full generators. Proper generators already obey the required equality. Induction on raw $(r+1)$-terms now gives $L(U)=p(\tau U)-p(\sigma U)$: the induction step sums the contributions of the top slots and uses $(*)$. It follows that every bracket between parallel terms obeys $hM=d$. Both implications have finite witnesses where needed. For finitely many head equations the necessity argument uses finitely many anchors, maximal-slot comparisons, and brackets comparing parallel terms; include their finite dependency closures. For a finite inconsistent system of original degree columns, relabel in $P(J)$ by Response 19, retain the subheads of the finitely many attaching terms, and use their anchored equations in the converse induction. This finite argument avoids any compactness assumption for integer solutions.

**Reproduction.** The solver builds the affine expressions, checks the actual types of the anchored labels, and compares each supplied equation before and after anchoring. On the minimum-coloured three-cube it extracts 22 distinct 2-heads from the two hemispheres. One anchored equation already reduces to $0=1$ up to sign. With `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_shortcut_potential.py --input data.json`, use the typed context, colours and degree of Response 19 and an additional `terms` array of raw coherence-headed $(k-1)$-terms in that context. The output is an integral assignment on the encountered unknowns or a verified combination of the input equations obstructing it. [Affine-equation solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_shortcut_potential.py)

# Researcher Response 21

## All composite shortcuts admit an integral potential, but structural flattening forces a unit change

In the notation of Response 20, set every free $\beta$ equal to zero and keep the prescribed values on proper terms. Write $p_0$ for the resulting value and put $h_g=\omega(g)-p_0(\tau g)+p_0(\sigma g)$ on full $(r+1)$-generators. **No pair of parallel $(r+1)$-terms made from bracket variables by identities, binary globular compositions at any cut, and arbitrary stationary lower terms gives a degree obstruction.** This applies to the $k$-columns comparing any two such terms, including their signed combinations and every modulus. It also permits successive or simultaneous changes at rank-$r$ slots of a common head as in Response 20.

Indeed $\omega-h\rho$ equals the endpoint difference of $p_0$ on each primitive bracket. A composite at cut $r$ telescopes. At a cut below $r$, its two $r$-faces have the same binary head on their active faces, with the same lower substitution. The recursion of Response 20 cancels the common constant, leaving the sum of changes of the active factors. Any stationary factor of rank $r$ contributes the same value twice; those of lower rank are included in the common substitution. An identity has zero change. The same subtraction works for any common head with changes in several maximal $r$-slots, using their actual comparison counts as in item 14. Induction proves the assertion even for an arbitrary number of composites, repeated labels, and stationary inputs containing unbounded loop powers. It includes all the interval shortcut columns of Response 19. The restriction concerns operations at dimension $r+1$; the lower terms may use unrestricted raw syntax.

**An explicit equation outside this family at every $k\ge3$.** Start with the edge $[k-1,k]$. Prefix the edge $[j,j+1]$ for $j=k-2,\ldots,2$ in this order. At each stage replace the whiskered term $w$ by the one-higher bracket $[a(w),w]$. The resulting $r$-variable $g$ goes between outer objects $2,k$ and its integral simplex chain has coefficient $+1$ on $[2,\ldots,k]$. Inductively the old generator is the only top contribution on the old simplex. For a prefix of two edges the new edge contributes zero on the old edge. The shortcut $a(w)$ has the new least colour in its support, and its chain is coned there, so it has zero coefficient on the old simplex. Coning the difference adds the new least vertex with positive sign.

Put $e=[1,2]$, $f=[k,0]$, and $w=e*_0 g$. Then $w$ is proper and $p_0(w)=+1$ by the same coefficient calculation for $[a(w),w]$ on $[1,\ldots,k]$. Form the nested $r$-term $L=w*_0 f$. In the generic pasting context $P=D^1\vee_0 D^r\vee_0 D^1$ also form the **single** $r$-head on $P$ with exactly the generic type of $L$, then substitute $(e,g,f)$ to obtain $R$. That head is legal: the two generic faces of the nested binary composite cover the corresponding signed boundaries of $P$. In $P$ the generic nested and single-head terms are parallel and each uses every variable, so raw coherences give $E:L\to R$ **and** $E':R\to L$ after substitution. Both have zero $(r+1)$-generator counts. The anchored outer expression on either side contains colour zero and hence has zero basic weight, whether proper or full. The individual slots $e,g,f$ are variables. Thus $p_0(L)=1$, $p_0(R)=0$. Their structural comparisons have opposite unit defects for the proposed zero assignment.

Concretely at seven colours the two full anchored expressions give an equation $\beta_R-\beta_L=1$: the inner $w$ on the nested side contributed the known proper value. Under six-seam RC the two integer values must differ by one. The reverse comparison imposes the same equation with reversed sign. Finding an incompatible second route between the same unknowns would obstruct six-seam coherence by Response 20. This pair alone gives a consistent equation.

**Checks.** Run the following from the repository root. At $k=3,4,5,6$ the solver checks the exact types of both comparison directions, their zero top counts, and their two opposite affine expressions, before and after anchoring the slots. For seven colours Agda checks both rank-five judgements with literal boundaries. The solver also tests all interval shortcut columns at these ranks and composites of their based sides at every cut, with a loop factor repeated 1, 2, 4 and 8 times. It checks 36, 56, 80 and 112 distinct typed sides and composites respectively. The unrestricted family follows from the recursion above. [Solver and judgement generator](../../../tools/relative_coherence_shortcut_potential.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_shortcut_potential.py --agda "$dir/Flatten.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/Flatten.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 22

## Flattenings on arities of height four suffice for the six-seam degree test

For $k\ge3$ put $r=k-2$. In Response 20 it suffices to impose the equations for the following **reversible structural comparisons**. Take a pasting context $D$ of height at most $r$, a full-support $r$-term $t$ on $D$, and the single head $f=\operatorname{coh}_{D,\operatorname{type}(t)}[\mathrm{id}]$. Compare $t$ with $f$ after labelling $D$ arbitrarily through rank $r-1$ and using anchors in rank $r$. The resulting equation is $p(t[\gamma])=p(f[\gamma])$. For seven colours this requires only trees of height at most four with arbitrary labels through dimension three. Any finite inconsistent system from Response 20 gives a finite inconsistent set of these flattenings. Each flattening itself is an allowed head equation.

**Folding the slots.** Anchor a given $(r+1)$-head on $P$ by Response 20. If $P$ has height $r+1$, keep its negative $r$-boundary $D$. On each top string of the tree, identify the two $r$-faces of every $(r+1)$-variable, retaining the initial $r$-variable of that string. This gives a substitution labelling $P$ in $D$: every lower variable is unchanged, each $r$-variable maps to the retained representative, and each $(r+1)$-variable to a weak identity on that representative. This is typed because the two original $r$-faces are parallel variables and have the same literal lower faces. The top strings are just the lists of children at the last tree level. Both signed boundaries map bijectively to $D$ in every rank. If $P$ is shorter, take the identity substitution.

The anchored labels of the two faces of a top variable coincide literally, since the bracket over a specified pair of faces is unique. Hence each top slot contributes zero on the right of $(*)$. Its label may itself be a bracket loop. The two generic $r$-terms on the left use only variables through rank $r$, on which folding followed by the restricted labelling agrees literally with the anchored substitution. This does not require the bracket loop to agree with a weak identity.

The resulting generic terms $s,t$ in $D$ are parallel and full. For a full-support head this follows by taking the image of its support, and for a composition head it follows by the boundary bijections. In a shorter arity a composition side condition with distinct proper boundaries cannot occur for such parallel terms: they lie above those boundaries' generator dimensions and hence have the support of their common face. The flat head on their common type is legal. Indeed a full $r$-cell of a globular tree of height $r$ has the total composite as strict image. At the root it traverses all branches, and its strict factor on each must use that entire branch; induction on the child trees gives the total composite there, with identity padding on shorter branches. Thus the two faces have the signed boundary supports by item 2. In a tree of smaller height both faces have full support by the same item. The two comparisons $s\to f$ and $t\to f$ are full-support coherences over $D$, as are their reverses. Subtracting their affine equations gives exactly the original one, including every coefficient and the integer constant. This proof covers arbitrary inactive substitutions and unbounded powers in them by literal substitution and Response 20.

**Checks.** With `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_folded_frames.py --edges 7`, the solver folds all trees with at most seven edges of heights two through five, respectively 120, 250, 173 and 62 trees. Each test uses bracket labels, a nonstandard negative face with an inserted weak unit, and the original and folded dependent types. It verifies the full affine row equality. On the 22-head three-cube input of Response 20 the folded system detects an integral contradiction using two flattenings of opposite signs. The solver also accepts the same `--input` format as Response 20. This is an exact reduction of the degree search; no contradiction in degree six follows from folding alone. [Folding and comparison solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_folded_frames.py)

# Researcher Response 23

## A facet assignment integrates every route retaining a common active colour

There is a second route in the unit comparison of Response 21: insert a weak unit at the top composition cut of its nested side, then flatten to the single head. The two affine equations sum to the original equation. More generally **all** flattening routes in which the proper frame contributions miss the same simplex facet have a simultaneous integral solution. This includes every reflattening of one marked $r$-generator at $r\ge2$ in arbitrary lower-dimensional whiskering contexts as specified below.

**The chain calculation.** In the recursion of Response 20 retain the coefficient of every anchored frame $F$, including proper ones, before evaluating any constants. Assign it the integral $(r+1)$-chain $B_F=H([a(F),F])$. For any $r$-term $t$ the boundaries of these chains, counted recursively with multiplicity, sum to $H(t)-H(a(t))$. On a variable both sides vanish. At a head, each changed top slot contributes the difference between its chain and that of its anchor; the outer anchored term has exactly the same literal type as $t$. This proves the identity by induction. Consequently in any head equation the signed total $\sum_F n_F B_F$ is a cycle: the top slot boundary differences telescope against the output boundary, and the output's two $r$-faces have the same anchor, as do each input's two faces. The cycle is a multiple of $\partial[0,\ldots,k]$, since this generates the group of $(k-1)$-cycles of the simplex.

Fix $i$ and let $c_i(B)$ be $(-1)^i$ times the coefficient of the facet omitting $i$. For a full frame assign
$$
                         \beta_i(F)=c_0(B_F)-c_i(B_F).
$$
For a proper frame continue to use $c_0(B_F)$ as required. The residual of any head equation with this assignment is exactly $\sum_{F\ \mathrm{proper}}n_F c_i(B_F)$: apply $c_0=c_i$ on the total cycle and cancel the full contributions. Thus a common zero in coordinate $i$ solves an entire family over the integers, even when proper frames contribute nontrivially on other facets. One can choose $i$ independently on groups of equations with disjoint sets of full unknowns. In particular a certificate with nonzero degree modulo $m$ needs a nonzero signed proper contribution on **each** of the $k+1$ facets after taking its row combination modulo $m$. This tests the actual simplex chains of the frames and allows cancellations within a facet.

**Persistent occurrence.** For $r\ge2$ start with an $r$-variable carrying colour $i$. Form $r$-expressions by binary whiskering at any defined cut with arbitrary terms of rank strictly less than $r$, and by flattening nested subexpressions on their generic arities with their literal boundary types. Consider comparisons of parallel such expressions over the same single active slot, or collections of these comparisons whose marked slots share $i$. Both $(r-1)$-faces together retain the original variable's colour: this holds by the bracket declaration for the variable; a whisker cut is below $r-1$, so both original faces occur in the corresponding composite faces; flattening preserves their types. Every anchored frame along the active path therefore contains $i$, since the anchor uses both faces. The stationary lower arguments introduce no separate $r$-frames in the recursion. A proper frame containing $i$ cannot contribute to the facet omitting $i$. The common assignment above integrates every such comparison, with arbitrary parentheses, inactive terms and loop powers. It also applies to arbitrary other rows whenever their proper chain on that facet vanishes. A conflicting route for Response 21 must escape this family.

**Typed checks.** For $k=3,4,5,6$ the solver builds the three parallel generic terms $L$, $1_{\sigma L}*_{r-1} L$ and $R$ from Response 21 and checks all six oriented comparisons after substitution. It verifies literal types, both routes' affine sums, and the residual on every facet directly against the chain formula. At seven colours the direct residual vector is $(-1,0,0,0,0,0,0)$; the six comparisons all satisfy the assignment omitting colour one. Thus the extra route does not contradict the unit equation. Agda checks all six rank-five judgments, including each reverse. [Facet and route solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_folded_frames.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_folded_frames.py --edges 7 --agda "$dir/FoldedRoutes.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/FoldedRoutes.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 24

## A degree certificate must use an operation whose root vertices see every colour

For $k\ge3$ there is an explicitly computable integer on each generic $(k-1)$-head with its **object slots alone** coloured. Under substitution this number is independent of the positive-dimensional arguments. For a $(k-1)$-term $U$, let $d(U)$ be the sum of these integers over its occurrences of top heads: count its head, recurse into its arguments of the same dimension, and give a variable zero. There is an explicit integer row $h$ on full-colour $(k-1)$-generators such that for every **based** $U$ in Response 19,
$$
                         \omega(U)-h\rho(U)=d(U).
$$
In particular a seven-colour modular certificate must contain a rank-five head whose own root path sees all seven colours, in at least six intervals. Even an input containing every colour cannot make a head with fewer root colours contribute. This excludes the reflattenings of Response 21 and all other families made from narrower top heads simultaneously, with arbitrarily many active slots. The assertion concerns the integral degree test for the seam ring.

**Computing the number.** Use ordered integral simplex chains; $C_a$ cones at vertex $a$, and $[a,b]$ is the signed edge (zero for $a=b$). For an $n$-head $D$ on $P$ with generic faces $s,t$ and object colouring $c$, compute a cycle $Z_n(D,c)$ and set $W_n(D,c)=C_oZ_n(D,c)$, where $o$ is its coloured outer source. For $n=1$, $Z_1$ is the sum of the consecutive coloured edges of $P$ minus the edge between the two endpoint colours. This includes the point arity. For a term $w$ in a coloured globular arity define $Q_n(w)$ by summing $W_n$ over its top-head occurrences and giving variables zero, passing the induced object colouring at each nested head. Inductively put
$$
                            Z_n(D,c)=Q_{n-1}(s)-Q_{n-1}(t),\qquad n\ge2.
$$
The structural integer at $n=k-1$ is the coefficient of $[1,\ldots,k]$ in $Z_{k-1}$. All these chains are carried by the root vertex colours of the indicated arity: the objects of every nested generic substitution are labelled by object variables of its parent. A $(k-1)$-cycle on at most $k$ vertices is zero, since boundary is injective on top chains of the proper simplex. We verify the cycle assertion and the formula next.

**Comparison with the actual generator counts.** Let $H(t)$ be the carried simplex chain from Response 11 on an arbitrary term, given by its top-generator counts. Define a carried $(n+1)$-chain $K_n(t)$ for every positive-dimensional term. In dimension one set $K_1(x)=0$ on generators. In higher dimension put
$$
 K_n(x)= C_{o(x)}\bigl(H(x)+K_{n-1}(\sigma x)-K_{n-1}(\tau x)\bigr)
$$
on a generator $x$. Evaluate a composite head by adding $W_n$ to the sum of the $K_n$ of its rank-$n$ arguments. Then
$$
 \begin{aligned}
 \partial K_1(t)&=H(t)-[o(t),o'(t)],\\
 \partial K_n(t)&=H(t)+K_{n-1}(\sigma t)-K_{n-1}(\tau t)\quad(n\ge2),
 \end{aligned}
$$
where $o'$ is the outer target. In rank one the consecutive edges telescope and $H$ of a generating edge is its chord. At a higher generator the expression coned is a cycle by the preceding rank's identity and parallelism of its two faces.

For the head step, on any generic $j$-term $w$ the substitution law is
$$
 K_j(w[\gamma])=Q_j(w)+\sum_{i:\dim i=j} N_i(w)K_j(\gamma_i).
$$
Indeed on a variable only its own summand occurs. At a head the contribution $W_j$ sees exactly the same object names after composing labels; summing the induction statement over its top arguments gives the formula. Apply this in degree $n-1$ to the two generic faces of $D$. Their target-minus-source count difference is the cellular boundary of the strict total composite of $P$, hence the sum of the boundaries of its rank-$n$ variables. Because those variables have variable faces, every lower-argument term in the substitution formula cancels against the corresponding input boundary contribution. The remaining correction at the head is exactly $Z_n(D,c)$. It is a cycle: the desired boundary formula's right side is closed by the preceding rank, and the input contributions have boundaries already. This reasoning can be carried out on $P$ itself with the chosen object colours and then after arbitrary substitution. Coning therefore fills that cycle and proves the asserted induction. In particular the proof ranges over arbitrary inactive inputs, including paths visiting every colour and arbitrarily large finite powers of a loop.

In rank $n=k-1$ write $V(x)=H(x)+K_{n-1}(\sigma x)-K_{n-1}(\tau x)$. This is a carried $(k-1)$-cycle. It vanishes for every proper generator and is $h_x\partial[0,\ldots,k]$ on a full generator. Before coning, the head-step calculation gives, for **any** $U$,
$$
 H(U)+K_{n-1}(\sigma U)-K_{n-1}(\tau U)
       - \sum_x N_x(U)V(x)=d(U)\,\partial[0,\ldots,k].
$$
On a based term the two endpoint chains cancel. Taking the distinguished facet coefficient gives the claimed equality. Thus when full counts vanish modulo $m$, the signed sum of the individual root-colour contributions must give the observed degree modulo $m$. The same formula applies to differences between terms with identical literal boundaries.

**Checks.** `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_head_degree.py` separately evaluates the generator-count side and the generic-head side as entire signed simplex chains. On the based four-turn example of Response 19, the count weight and structural sum both equal $6$, nonzero modulo four. On the 22 two-heads of the three-cube input it finds five terms with structural sum $+1$ and the rest zero. The solver accepts the head-equation JSON of Response 20 with `--input`. [Root-colour degree solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_head_degree.py)

# Researcher Response 25

## The root-arity bound is sharp for both directions of a typed comparison in every dimension

For every $k\ge3$, on the path of $k$ generating arrows with successive vertex colours $0,\ldots,k$, there are parallel $(k-2)$-terms $L,R$ with a $(k-1)$-comparison in either direction. **Every comparison $L\to R$ on that path has structural degree $-1$, and every reverse has degree $+1$.** Consequently each uses a top head with at least $k$ root intervals. The bounds are attained by a single top head in each direction. The two terms have the same strict image, the full path padded by identities as needed.

Start on three edges $x,y,z$ with $L=(xy)z$ and $R=x(yz)$, using binary composition. Both generic full path terms have the same literal type. The head $L\to R$ is legal and $Q_1(L)-Q_1(R)$ is minus the oriented boundary of the tetrahedron: the two bracketings give its two signed triangulations of the quadrilateral. Reversing the two sides negates the cycle.

Inductively shift a length-$k$ comparison $g$ to the last $k$ edges of a path of length $k+1$, and prefix its remaining edge $e$ from colour zero to one. The new nested side is $w=e*_0g$. The other side $f$ is the single head on the entire path with the same generic type as $w$. This head is legal: $w$ lies above dimension one, and both its faces use the entire path by the support lemma. The generic structural head $w\to f$ is legal by full support, as is its reverse. To calculate its degree, the shifted $g$ contributes $-[1,\ldots,k+1]$ to $Q_{k-1}(w)$, by coning its structural cycle within that simplex. Its binary whisker contributes zero, since its root has only three vertices and its $W_{k-1}$ has dimension at least three. The single head $f$ has only path inputs and its own $W_{k-1}$ is coned at zero. It therefore has zero coefficient on $[1,\ldots,k+1]$. This proves the signed unit at the next rank.

On the path there are no generators in the comparison dimension. The general identity of Response 24 makes the structural sum of a comparison depend only on its endpoints, giving the assertion for **every** forward or reverse term. The same conclusion holds after substituting arbitrary paths with those same endpoint colours, for comparisons with the original strict image: its top-generator counts are all zero. In particular inactive excursions can have unbounded length. If the root colours themselves coincide, the structural degree of the displayed head instead vanishes, even when the substituted paths together visit every colour.

**Checks.** At lengths three through seven the solver constructs both literal judgements, computes the entire signed boundary cycle, and tests the identification of two colours. For the six-arrow example it separately substitutes powers one through seven of a path visiting all seven colours, both with the original root colours and with all root objects identified. The outcomes are respectively a signed unit and zero at every tested power. Agda checks both rank-five terms on six arrows. These are parallel comparisons with different endpoints in the two directions. A based modular certificate for the six-seam ring would also have to satisfy the full-colour count condition of Response 19.

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_head_degree.py --agda "$dir/HeadDegree.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/HeadDegree.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 26

## A relative degree extends by one colour exactly when its opposite-face chain bounds after relabelling

Fix coefficients $R=\mathbb Z$ or $R=\mathbb Z/m$, $m\ge2$. Write $C(J)$ for the cellular chains of $X(J)$ from Response 9 and $D(J)$ for the quotient by chains supported on the union of the proper colour subcontexts. Order $J=(0,1,\ldots,k)$, put $I=J-\{0\}$, and let $i$ include $I$ into $J$. For $j\in I$ let $c_j$ replace $j$ by zero everywhere in a literal bracket, and put $E=\prod_{j\in I}(1-c_j)$. For $k\ge2$ and any $d\in R$, the following are equivalent.

1. A relative $k$-cycle on $J$ has oriented simplex degree $d$.
2. Some relative $(k-1)$-cycle $y$ on $I$ has degree $d$, and the absolute cycle $Eiy$ bounds in $C(J)$.

Here $y$ can be given by any lift to cellular chains. In particular the degree images form a descending sequence of ideals in each fixed coefficient ring. A finite certificate of nonzero degree at **any** rank $k\ge6$ therefore disproves identity-image RC through degree eight on $\Gamma_6$, by repeated descent and Response 19. This transfers a homological obstruction between different seam rings.

**Chain proof.** There is a short exact sequence
$$
 0\longrightarrow D(I)\xrightarrow{Ei} EC(J)\xrightarrow{q}D(J)\longrightarrow0 .
$$
The collapses are commuting idempotent chain maps. The kernel of $E$ is precisely the span of generators missing some positive colour. Each such generator is fixed by its missing-colour collapse, and $x-Ex$ belongs to that span by expanding the product. Consequently $Ex$ lifts the class of every $x$ under $q$. On chains from $I$, all nonidentity summands of $Ei$ miss a positive colour. Restricting to generators with support exactly $I$ recovers every full coefficient of the input. This proves injectivity on $D(I)$. If $q(w)=0$ for $w=Ew$, decompose $w$ into its proper generators and apply $E$ once more. Only generators supported on exactly $I$ can survive, proving exactness in the middle.

In (1) pick an absolute lift $x$ and take $y$ to be the restriction of $\partial x$ to generators of support exactly $I$. Exactness gives $\partial Ex=Eiy$ and $\partial y=0$ in $D(I)$. For the degree, apply the carried simplex chain map of Response 11 to $\partial x$. Only its $I$-supported summands can hit $[1,\ldots,k]$, whose coefficient in $\partial[0,\ldots,k]$ is $+1$. Conversely, if $\partial w=Eiy$, every boundary generator is proper, and on the opposite facet only the uncollapsed $y$ can contribute. Thus $w$ gives (1). Omitting the vertex in position $j$ of any increasing list multiplies the induced degree by $(-1)^j$. These proofs use finite cellular chains with arbitrary integer multiplicities. Collapses act on the entire typed substitution, including every inactive argument, and commute with the cellular boundary for every literal term.

At six seams there is also a fixed-class version. Let $\delta:H_5D(I)\to R$ be degree, choose a degree-one class $b$ using the minimum-coloured five-cube of Response 9 with the appropriate orientation, and put
$$
 G=E H_5 C(J)\big/(Ei)_*(\ker\delta),\qquad z=(Ei)_*b\ \text{ in }G.
$$
Then the **exact image** of seven-colour degree is the ideal $\{r\in R:r z=0\}$. Indeed all classes of degree $r$ are $rb+\ker\delta$, and the preceding equivalence asks if one of them maps to zero in $H_5 C(J)$. The image of the idempotent on homology is the homology of its image subcomplex. The quotient here allows every relative degree-zero class on the opposite face, including one with a proper nonzero boundary. Response 9 tested a relation involving absolute cycles there.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_face_transfer.py --only descent`. On the minimum-coloured three-cube the four oriented opposite degrees are $(-1,+1,-1,+1)$. On the four-turn example all four are $2\bmod4$. In each case the checker uses raw bracket relabellings to verify $\partial Ex=Eiy$ on every cellular coefficient. At the first step each face has one lower cell, eight terms in its absolute cycle, and eight in the displayed primitive. It checks all four choices of first vertex and all twelve choices of ordered first and second vertices, independently in each example. The `--input` option accepts a typed context, colours and degree as in Response 19. [Face transfer solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_face_transfer.py)

# Researcher Response 27

## One based bracket and its signed relabellings suffice for the absolute filling test

In Response 26, for $k\ge3$ one can require $y$ to be a **single** bracket on the $k$ positive colours, based at any specified vertex of $I$. Over $\mathbb Z/m$ one can further require $y=[1,C]$, where $1$ is the iterated identity at that vertex and $C$ is a based $(k-2)$-term. Thus at six seams the modular test is to find a based **four-term on six colours** such that $[1,C]$ has proper boundary modulo $m$ and nonzero degree, and its **64 signed relabellings have an absolute filling on seven colours**. These conditions together are necessary and sufficient for a modular degree certificate. An integral certificate also supplies one modulo any $m$ not dividing its degree.

**Preserving the absolute class.** More generally, for every integral $n$-chain $y$ in $C(V)$ with $n\ge2$, a single bracket $b$ between based $(n-1)$-terms satisfies $b-y=\partial w$ on the same vertex set. To see this, replace each occurrence of $-g$, for $g=[u,v]$, by the generator $g'=[v,u]$. The change $g'+g$ is the boundary of $[1_u,g*_{n-1}g']$. The composite goes from $u$ to $u$, so this is a literal $(n+1)$-bracket.

Whisker each resulting $n$-arrow on both sides by the pads of Response 13 at cuts $0$ through $n-2$. The two $(n-1)$-faces undergo exactly the basing of that response. In a binary globe paste above the cut, the two immediate faces are the binary pastes of the corresponding faces, with a lower-dimensional factor kept stationary. This follows by restricting the defining binary pasting scheme to its two signed boundaries. The pads have dimension at most $n-1$, so each padded arrow still counts precisely its original $n$-generator. Compose the padded arrows at cut $n-2$. Their faces are endomorphisms of the same literal pole, so the resulting $T:A\to B$ exists and its faces are based. The last bracket $[T,[A,B]]$ has boundary $[A,B]-N_n(T)$. Adding the sign-change witnesses gives the asserted $w$. For the empty chain use the identity $n$-term for $T$. Working modulo $m$, do this first to an integral lift. In particular the change to the projected cycle $Eiy$ itself has the explicit primitive $Eiw$. Equality of boundaries alone, as in Response 13, would not give this conclusion.

**Putting the source at the identity.** For a based bracket $g=[A,B]$, let $U$ be the iterated identity parallel to $A,B$, $a=[U,A]$, and $b=[U,B]$. The bracket $[a*_{n-1}g,b]$ has boundary $b-a-g$. Form the cut-$(n-2)$ product of $b$ followed by $m-1$ copies of $a$, with fixed parentheses. It goes from a product of $m$ copies of $U$ to the corresponding product $C$ of $B,A,\ldots,A$. The source product and $U$ are parallel terms over the singleton vertex. Both have full singleton support, so a raw point coherence gives an $n$-arrow from $U$ to that product, with zero $n$-generator counts. Precompose by it to obtain $T:U\to C$. The bracket $[T,[U,C]]$ together with the preceding triangle has total boundary $[U,C]-g-ma$. This proves the modular normalization including its absolute witness.

For nonzero degree the normalized bracket has full support $I$. Its relabellings indexed by subsets $S\subseteq I$ are distinct since the positive names in each support are exactly $I-S$. The sum has $2^k$ coefficients $\pm1$ before reduction. By Response 13 a filling, when one exists, can itself be sought as **one** $k$-bracket on $J$ whose two literal $(k-1)$-faces have count difference $Eiy$ over $R$ (apply that response to an integral lift of a filling). All the constructions use raw terms and impose no bound on arities, multiplicities, or the syntax of inactive inputs.

**Checks.** The descent command of Response 26 also replaces the opposite-face generator at zero in each example and constructs the new filling, checking all eight coefficients and the degree. In the mod-four case it uses the identity-source normalization. The following additionally checks a signed chain $2g-h$ at rank two, an empty rank-three chain, and all 64 relabellings of a literal five-loop over six positive names. That five-loop has degree zero. Agda checks the reverse $h'$, its cancellation bracket and the final comparison in the signed example.

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_face_transfer.py --only normalization --agda "$dir/ChainNormal.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/ChainNormal.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 28

## Basing the six-arrow structural comparison gives zero facet weight in both directions

Take the rank-five comparison of Response 25 on the six-arrow path, or its typed reverse, and apply the identical two-sided padding of Response 13 to make that term based. Its two rank-four faces use all seven colours, and its original rank-five generator count is zero. At cuts $0,1,2,3$ all pads have rank at most four. Both padded four-faces retain every original colour because the substituted face remains a literal argument of each binary paste. At cut four the two new five-brackets therefore both have full support. Their simplex images are coned at the minimum colour zero, so both have zero coefficient on the opposite facet. These are the entire five-count of the based term. Thus its weight $\omega$ is zero even though the original unbased structural head has signed unit degree.

The argument applies to any comparison with full-colour immediate faces whose existing top counts are supported solely on full-colour generators. Arbitrary inactive labels and loop powers are allowed subject to these two hypotheses. In particular substituting paths with the original seven distinct root colours preserves the hypotheses for the six-arrow comparison, irrespective of path lengths or repeated visits. Padding retains the two full supports and only adds full generators at the last cut. Composing any finite list of such based terms, in either direction and with arbitrary powers at the top cut, adds their count vectors and leaves the weight zero. Thus this entire family cannot supply the based witness of Response 19.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_face_transfer.py --only basings`. For the actual six-arrow comparison the solver infers the two literal directions, checks the empty five-count in each, and pads their four-faces. At each of the seven basepoints and for each direction it constructs both final five-brackets, checks they are distinct, and computes their supports and their opposite-facet coefficients. All 28 coefficients are zero. The computation concerns these fourteen basings. The support and count argument gives the extension to arbitrary paths and powers. [Basing checker](../../../tools/relative_coherence_face_transfer.py)

# Researcher Response 29

## A homology test constructs signed colourings for any specified family of supports

Let $x$ be a finite cellular $k$-chain in a typed parameter, $k\ge2$. Give its objects distinct names in $V$. Choose subsets $U\subseteq V$ so that each generator occurring in $\partial x$ is supported on at least one $U$. Put $K=\bigcup_U\Delta(U)$, the simplicial complex consisting of every subset of every chosen $U$. Send the cellular boundary to ordered simplex chains by the carried chain map $h$ of Response 11, using the distinct names. This gives a cycle $\beta=h(\partial x)$ in $K$.

**If $[\beta]\ne0$ in integral homology, this parameter disproves identity-image RC through degree $k+2$ for $\Gamma_k$, including when $[\beta]$ is torsion.** There is also a fixed-modulus version. Start with $x$ modulo $m$, covering just the boundary coefficients that survive modulo $m$. A nonzero $[\beta]\in H_{k-1}(K;\mathbb Z/m)$ gives a certificate with that modulus. By Response 26 either result at any $k\ge6$ disproves the six-seam hypothesis through degree eight. In particular RC in the stated range would force every such support cycle to bound in the corresponding complex.

**What is exact.** Write $R=\mathbb Z$ or $\mathbb Z/m$. Take a finite $R$-linear combination $\mu$ of vertex functions $c:V\to J=\{0,\ldots,k\}$. Its restriction to $U$ adds the coefficients of functions that agree there. Require that this restriction have coefficient zero on every function using all of $J$, separately on each chosen $U$. The attainable degrees of the combined chain $\sum_c\mu(c)P(c)x$ are precisely
$$
                 \{\langle\lambda,\beta\rangle:\lambda\in Z^{k-1}(K;R)\}.
$$
Here one first sends the typed parameter to $P(V)$ by brackets as in Response 19. Each boundary generator on $U$ depends only on the restriction to $U$, so these conditions guarantee a relative cycle. Coincidences of literal generators from different supports can allow additional cancellations outside this test.

To prove necessity, order $V$. On a $(k-1)$-simplex define $\lambda$ by taking the coefficient of $[1,\ldots,k]$ after applying the signed vertex maps to its oriented vertex list. On each $k$-simplex of $K$, proper restrictions give zero simplex $k$-chain. Taking boundaries proves that $\lambda$ is a cocycle. For calculating degree one may use the carried chain map that cones each bracket boundary at its outer source vertex. This choice commutes with vertex maps, including identifications, by the oriented simplex cone formula. It is chain homotopic to the least-vertex choice within each supporting simplex, by inductively coning the difference of the two chains and the homotopy already defined on the boundary. Thus the two choices on $\partial x$ give the same homology class in $K$. Applying the natural choice to the combined relative chain computes its degree on the opposite facet, giving precisely the displayed pairing.

**Constructing the colourings.** On an ordered list of $k$ vertices use the signed sum $z=\bigotimes_{i=1}^k(e_i-e_0)$ of colour words. Here $e_i$ denotes the choice of colour $i$. Forgetting any one position sends $z$ to zero, and its oriented coefficient on $[1,\ldots,k]$ is one. On any ordered $U$ partition its vertices into $k$ consecutive nonempty blocks and duplicate each position of $z$ over its block. Every resulting word uses at most $k$ colours. Its cocycle takes value one on lists choosing one vertex per block, and zero on other $(k-1)$-simplices. These block cocycles form a basis for the cocycles on $\Delta(U)$ over every $R$. Indeed a cocycle is determined by its values on faces through the first vertex, by the coboundary equation there. Index those faces by the other $k-1$ positions, and the partitions by the starting positions of their last $k-1$ blocks. The evaluation matrix is triangular in lexicographic order with diagonal entries one. When $|U|<k$ both families are empty.

Expand $\lambda|_U$ in that basis and take the same combination of duplicated words. These local signed sums agree under restriction. A partition missing a whole block on restriction gives zero by the zero marginal of $z$. Every other partition restricts to the partition of the surviving vertices into those same blocks. Uniqueness in the basis gives precisely the sum constructed on the intersection. To obtain $\mu$, extend each local sum by constant zero outside its set and take inclusion–exclusion over the nonempty subfamilies of the chosen sets, using the local sum on their intersection. On restricting to a chosen $U$, terms indexed by a subfamily not containing $U$ pair with those obtained by adding $U$, leaving just its prescribed local sum. This proves sufficiency and gives finite coefficients without enumerating all vertex functions.

For the integral assertion, solve the integer linear system $\partial w=\beta$ on the simplices of $K$. If it has no solution, diagonalization furnishes an integral cocycle of nonzero pairing or a cocycle of nonzero pairing modulo a divisor. For a prescribed modulus $m$, add columns $mI$ to the boundary matrix before testing integral solvability. A separating character with coefficients $a/d$ modulo one then satisfies $ma/d\in\mathbb Z$ in every coordinate. Writing those integers modulo $m$ gives the required cocycle over $\mathbb Z/m$. This proves the fixed-modulus claim as well. The argument uses the full dependency closure of the supplied terms, with arbitrary multiplicities, inactive inputs and loop powers.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_signed_colours.py`. On the typed three-cube, using only the six facet supports, it constructs eight signed colourings, maps the raw generators to their literal brackets and checks integral degree $-1$ and every full-colour boundary coefficient. For another parameter use `--input file.json` with the raw `context`, integer `degree`, and an object `chain` mapping generator indices to integer coefficients. Optional `supports` lists sets of object indices in object-declaration order starting at zero. By default the solver uses the supports of the surviving boundary generators. Optional `modulus` fixes the coefficient ring. The incidence solver explicitly enumerates simplices of sizes $k$ and $k+1$ in the supplied supports. [Signed-colouring solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_signed_colours.py)

# Researcher Response 30

## Torsion makes signed colourings strictly stronger than individually admissible colourings

Take the single typed two-bracket from the composite $0\to1\to4$ to the direct edge $0\to4$ on six vertices. Require proper colour restrictions on the ten triples $012,013,024,035,045,125,134,145,234,235$. Its oriented boundary cycle, up to overall sign, is the triangle $014$. Twice this cycle bounds an integral sum of the ten signed triples. Any individual admissible colouring maps their complex to the boundary of the triangle of colours, whose first integral homology is torsion-free. Its degree is therefore zero, also after reduction at any modulus.

The solver finds a mod-two cocycle pairing to one with $014$. Its 28 signed colourings have proper marginals on every triple, and after collecting literal brackets they give a four-bracket typed relative cycle of degree one modulo two. With modulus four it finds 26 colourings, again four surviving brackets, of degree two. Modulo three the original cycle bounds. Over the integers the checker verifies the boundary of the ten signed triples and enumerates all $3^6$ individual colourings. Exactly 189 are admissible and every one has degree zero. Adjoining the triple $014$ to the required supports makes the integral support cycle bound by that single face.

There is a discrepancy in the support test in **every rank $k\ge2$**. Suspend this simplicial complex and cycle $k-2$ times. At each suspension append the two tips in turn to each oriented simplex of the cycle and subtract the two chains. This is again a cycle, and suspending the integral double-filling shows that twice it bounds, up to the boundary orientation sign. Extend the detecting cocycle modulo two by its old value on each simplex ending in the first new tip and zero elsewhere. Its coboundary vanishes by the old cocycle equation and its pairing stays one. Every individual proper colouring still maps to a sphere with torsion-free top integral homology. Response 29 constructs a signed mod-two colouring of nonzero pairing. At rank six the checker constructs 448 colourings of 14 vertices, verifies the proper restriction on all 160 supports and computes pairing one on the suspended cycle. This last calculation is simplicial. To obtain a six-seam counterexample from it one would need a typed six-chain whose boundary is carried on those supports with that homology class. The typed realization checked here is the rank-two example. [Construction and checks](../../../tools/relative_coherence_signed_colours.py)

# Researcher Response 31

## The four-membrane and seven-state thresholds survive arbitrary signed colour sums

For the six-cube of Response 12, allow any finite sum of colourings, integrally or modulo any $m$. On an exterior facet or a protected membrane require only that the combined restriction have zero coefficient on every full-colour assignment to that set. Even with that freedom, a nonzero boundary degree is possible **exactly when the unprotected membranes contain four cyclically consecutive indices**. For the three-dimensional states, the least number whose protection precludes nonzero degree for every such sum is **seven**. Both conclusions concern cancellation checked on each specified support. Actual comparisons or identifications across different supports remain available to a term search.

For the membrane claim use the exterior facets and the protected membranes for the sets defining $K$ in Response 29. Whenever no four-run is permitted, the solver of Response 12 exhibits a translated staircase triangulation whose every top simplex is contained in a protected membrane. Its oriented sum fills the exterior boundary integrally in $K$. Every detecting cocycle therefore has zero pairing, independently of the coefficients of the signed sum. If a four-run is permitted, its single priority colouring already has unit degree.

For the seven states $2,12,32,77,83,105,130$ take the exterior facets and those seven sets. In the nerve of this cover the exterior facets span the boundary of the dual cross-polytope. The 340 six-simplices of Response 18 can in fact be oriented **over the integers**. At a five-face incident to just one selected simplex prescribe its sign by the oriented exterior cycle. At a face incident to two, propagate the unique sign giving zero sum across that face. This reaches every selected simplex. Counting all signed faces independently, including the four faces incident to four selected simplices, gives precisely the 64 exterior facets with their prescribed orientations. There are 170 coefficients of each sign.

This filling implies that the ordinary cube boundary bounds homologously in $K$. The cover of $K$ by the simplices on its vertex sets has simplex intersections whenever nonempty, so its nerve equivalence restricts to the nerve equivalence of the exterior subcover. Concretely, on a barycentric subdivision of the nerve choose a common cube vertex at each intersecting collection. All choices on a flag belong to any member of its smallest collection. This gives the carried map to $K$. On the dual cross-polytope the flags correspond to chains of cube faces, and each chosen vertex lies in the corresponding face. The restriction is homotopic inside the exterior subcover to the usual subdivided cube boundary, by the simplex carriers on those faces. Response 29 now forces zero pairing over every coefficient ring. For any six states the single degree-one colouring of Response 17 suffices, proving sharpness. In particular, taking sums at an odd prime or a higher power of two cannot circumvent this seven-state obstruction.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_sweep_colours.py` for the 4,096 membrane permissions and `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_facet_omissions.py` for the integral boundary. The latter constructs the 13,636 five-faces and 17,542 six-faces of the nerve, orients the selected chain, and verifies equality coefficient by coefficient. # Researcher Response 32

## A nonzero degree is equivalent to one bracket per simplex with exactly the alternating cellular boundary

Fix $k\ge2$ and $R=\mathbb Z$ or $\mathbb Z/m$. A specified nonzero $d\in R$ occurs as a relative $k$-degree **if and only if** there are templates on the ordered simplices through dimension $k$ with the following properties. Use $d$ times the vertex in rank zero and $d$ times the forward generating edge in rank one. In each rank $2\le j\le k$ use **one** bracket $b_j$ on the $j+1$ vertex names, between based terms. Its cellular boundary is exactly the alternating sum of the preceding template under the $j+1$ increasing facet inclusions. Over $\mathbb Z/m$ every $b_j$ can have the iterated identity as its literal source. Thus for six seams the last equation has **seven** distinct signed five-generators. The same five-bracket, with the same syntax, is used on every facet. For modulus two its seven occurrences have odd counts and every other five-generator has even count in the target of $b_6$.

**Making the faces agree.** Start with a relative witness $x_k$ in $P(0,\ldots,k)$ as in Response 19. For decreasing $j$, take from $\partial x_j$ the generators of support exactly $\{1,\ldots,j\}$ and lower their names by one, obtaining $x_{j-1}$. Response 26 shows this is relative of the same degree, since the omitted vertex is first. At $j=1$ it simply gives $d$ times the vertex.

For ordered names $v_0<\cdots<v_j$, apply to $x_j$ the following signed sum of vertex functions, written as a tensor with one position per input name:
$$
 T(v_0,\ldots,v_j)= e_{v_0}\otimes(e_{v_1}-e_{v_0})\otimes\cdots\otimes(e_{v_j}-e_{v_{j-1}}).
$$
A word sends each input to the named vertex in its position and relabels the entire bracket. Call the resulting chain $S_j(v_0,\ldots,v_j)$. Its simplex image is $d[v_0,\ldots,v_j]$: the all-forward assignment is the only one using all $j+1$ names, and it has coefficient one. To compute its boundary, any generator missing a positive position cancels by the difference in that position. On the remaining generators, forgetting position zero leaves just the tensor of the $j$ differences. This tensor is the alternating sum of $T$ on the facets. Indeed write $D_i=e_{v_i}-e_{v_{i-1}}$. At an interior omission $i$, the new factor is $D_i+D_{i+1}$. Expanding and cancelling successive terms leaves $(e_{v_1}-e_{v_0})\otimes D_2\otimes\cdots\otimes D_j$. The two endpoint omissions give the same formula for $j=1$. Consequently
$$
 \partial S_j(\sigma)=S_{j-1}(\partial\sigma).
$$
These are equalities of actual cellular chains. The construction uses no division, including at primes dividing $(k+1)!$.

**Replacing each template by one bracket.** Write $D$ for the alternating sum of face inclusions on templates, so $D^2=0$ and $D\partial=\partial D$. On two vertices every one-generator is a loop or one of the two directed edges. A loop is homologous to zero by the two-bracket comparing the identity with it. The sum of the two opposed edges bounds by comparing their composite with the identity. Thus $S_1$ differs from $d$ times the forward edge by an explicit boundary $\partial w_1$, choosing the sign so that the new edge chain is $S_1+\partial w_1$. Put $w_0=0$.

At rank $j\ge2$ use the corrected chain $S_j+D w_{j-1}$. Its boundary is exactly $D b_{j-1}$ by induction and $D^2=0$. Apply Response 27 to obtain a single based bracket
$$
 b_j=S_j+D w_{j-1}+\partial w_j .
$$
That response also provides the identity-source choice and its change-of-chain witness over $\mathbb Z/m$. All auxiliary terms are made on the standard simplex and then relabelled, so neighbouring simplices use identical facet generators. The image of $D w_{j-1}$ in simplex dimension $j$ vanishes by facet support. The image of $\partial w_j$ vanishes because a simplex on $j+1$ names has no $(j+1)$-chains. Hence $b_j$ still has degree $d$, so uses all its names. Its distinct facet copies are distinguished by their supports. Conversely these equations force degree $d$ successively by the simplex boundary formula. In particular $b_k$ itself is relative and gives the certificate of Response 19. This statement prescribes cellular counts. The two literal attaching terms may use other lower brackets in matching counts.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_simplex_chains.py --compress`. The solver checks the function identity on every proper restriction through rank eight. On the typed three-cube and four-turn examples it constructs compatible chains of sizes $1,2,4,8$ at degrees $-1$ integrally and $-2$ modulo four. It then constructs one raw bracket on each standard simplex of ranks two and three, checks the ambient context and recomputes all boundary coefficients from the two literal terms. The last boundary has exactly four inclusions of the same triangle. The modular brackets have identity source. The construction works for arbitrary inactive labels and unbounded finite powers because every relabelling is a substitution on the entire raw generator and all chain equations use exact multiplicities. [Simplex chain solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_simplex_chains.py)

# Researcher Response 33

## Over a field the same degree test governs all support cycles, including the suspended projective plane

Let $K$ be any finite simplicial complex on ordered vertices $V$. In $P(V)$ use the subcomplex $X_K$ consisting of cells whose vertex names lie in a simplex of $K$. For a field $F=\mathbb F_p$, consider the subgroup of $H_{k-1}(K;F)$ obtained by projecting cycles on $X_K$ that bound as typed cellular chains on $X(V)$. **If there is a nonzero mod-$p$ degree certificate at rank $k$, this subgroup is the whole homology group for every $K$. If there is none, the subgroup is zero for every $K$.** In particular realizing the nonzero mod-two class of Response 30 by such a boundary is equivalent to the unrestricted mod-two degree test at six seams.

More generally, over the rings of Response 32, every attainable degree $d$ puts $d\,H_{k-1}(K;R)$ in that subgroup. For a simplicial cycle $\beta$ fill it by a chain $a$ in the full simplex on $V$, for example by coning at one vertex. Apply the common templates of Response 32 to $a$ and $\beta$. The first is a typed chain with boundary the second, and the latter is carried in $K$ and projects to $d\beta$ coefficient by coefficient. A nonzero $d$ over a field is a unit. Conversely a nonzero projected class over the field has a detecting cocycle, so Response 29 constructs a nonzero degree certificate. This argument allows the typed representative of $\beta$ to depend on the certificate.

**What already exists at rank six.** The integral unit from the minimum-coloured five-cube of Response 9 supplies the templates through rank five. The suspension four times of the oriented triangle boundary $014$ has 48 signed five-simplices, one for each choice of an edge and four tips. Hence it already lifts integrally to a **closed chain of 48 distinct typed five-brackets**, each a facet inclusion of one common template on six names, with the indicated simplex image up to orientation. Its class is nonzero in $H_5(X_K;\mathbb F_2)$ by its projection and Response 30. A filling in $X(V)$ for this particular lift would suffice. The equivalence in the first paragraph allows other lifts. Given a successful six-template, coning this simplicial cycle at vertex zero gives just 16 six-simplices, so 16 copies of the six-bracket suffice for the lift furnished by that template.

**Checks.** On the once-suspended triangle the same solver materializes two three-brackets, takes their cellular boundary and obtains the six oriented inclusions of the compressed two-bracket. It verifies the signed projection on every triangle. At four suspensions it checks the named five-cube incidences described in Response 34. The rank-five single-bracket existence uses the five-cube presentation theorem and the construction of Response 32. The raw compression run is in rank three. With `--input file.json` the solver accepts the context, colours and degree of Response 19, optionally a specified `chain` of generator coefficients and `modulus`. An optional `target` lists pairs of increasing vertex lists and coefficients for a simplicial cycle. It checks the resulting typed filling and its projected boundary. [Template and filling checks](../../../tools/relative_coherence_simplex_chains.py)

# Researcher Response 34

## The explicit suspended boundary and the original 64-term cube have equivalent absolute filling tests

There is a fixed lift of the fourfold suspension in Response 30 for which the absolute filling question is **exactly** the original five-cube polarization question of Response 9, over the integers or modulo any $m$. For this lift use the chains $S_5$ of Response 32 before compression, starting with the minimum-coloured five-cube $g$ on six names. In a signed sum of relabellings of $g$, denote the formal choice of a vertex $v$ by $e_v$. The 48 simplicial terms combine to the following 64 relabellings:
$$
 (e_1-e_0)\otimes(e_4-e_1)\otimes(e_6-e_7)\otimes(e_8-e_9)
                   \otimes(e_{10}-e_{11})\otimes(e_{12}-e_{13}).
$$
For the triangle boundary this is the telescoping identity of Response 32 on $014$. Suspending appends a final position at either tip with opposite signs. The repeated term at the old last vertex cancels, leaving the difference of the tips. The vertex support of $g$ is all six names, and its literal syntax retains each named occurrence, so distinct functions on those names give distinct relabelled generators. The simplicial image is the suspended cycle with overall sign given by the seed cube orientation.

**The general transfer.** For a lower relative cycle $g$ on $k$ names, apply the tensor $\bigotimes_{j=1}^k(e_{b_j}-e_{a_j})$ by relabelling. The result $Z$ is an absolute cycle since each term of $\partial g$ misses a position. Take the star to have edges $0\to j$ on $0,\ldots,k$. For *any* list of edges, the cycle is a signed sum of images of the star cycle: fix a vertex $p$ and expand every difference as $(e_{b_j}-e_p)-(e_{a_j}-e_p)$. Thus any star filling transports to an actual cellular filling of $Z$.

Conversely, suppose the edge multigraph is a forest. Root each component, orient away from the root, and number each child by the index of its parent edge. For each subset of children, send its members to their own numbers and send every other vertex to zero. Sum these vertex maps with alternating signs, with an overall sign to account for reorienting the original edges. Applied to the forest tensor, this gives the star tensor **with coefficient one**. Indeed expand by choosing an endpoint on each edge. A missed child cancels by toggling its membership in the subset. Each of the $k$ children must therefore be chosen exactly once. At each leaf its incident edge must choose it; inductively towards the roots every edge chooses its child. The surviving mixed difference is exactly the star tensor. Applying the very same signed maps to a filling transports it in the reverse direction. This proves equivalence for the specified $g$, its multiples and every coefficient modulus. It does not require changing a representative in homology.

The forest condition is also necessary for recovering the star **as a formal tensor** by signed vertex relabellings. On a cyclic graph the wedge of its edge differences is zero by the signed dependence along a cycle, also for loops and parallel edges. The wedge commutes with vertex maps and detects the star as a basis wedge with coefficient one. Particular terms may impose further relations after evaluation. The six differences in the displayed suspended example form a forest, so any improvement from its torsion support complex over the fixed star test must use additional typed relations or a different lift.

**Checks.** The solver expands all 48 five-simplices on the fixed minimum-coloured five-cube, aggregates the 64 labelled cubes and recomputes the boundary separately at every facet address. All ten boundary sums vanish. Using all 120 ordered flags on each surviving cube gives exactly the suspended simplicial cycle with multiplier $-1$. It also compares the entire signed lists of vertex functions in both transfers on the displayed forest, a six-edge path and a branching tree, and their reversed presentations. On a typed triangle it transports an integral filling from the two-edge star to a pair of disjoint edges and back, checking both cellular boundaries. The five-cube incidence checks use the known five-cube presentation for their interpretation as typed cells. They neither construct a six-cell nor assume one. # Researcher Response 35

## The suspended cube gives compatible weak presentations of the orientals through dimension five

Write $O_n$ for the strict oriental on the ordered names $0,\ldots,n$, with triangles directed from the shortcut to the subdivided path. There is a strict map from the globular suspension of the $(n-1)$-cube to $O_n$ that sends its top atom to the simplex atom. More precisely, the image of **every** suspended face is the horizontal composite of its simplex blocks, with identities up to the required dimension.

**Construction.** Send the poles to $0,n$. For a cubical basis face, let $P$ be the set of internal vertices fixed present, $A$ those still free, and leave out vertices fixed absent. Cut $[0,n]$ at $P$. With no free axes, send the suspended vertex to the sum of the edges between successive cuts. With free axes all in one cut interval $(l,r)$, send the face to the single oriented simplex $[l,A,r]$ in increasing order. With free axes in several intervals send its top chain to zero. This defines a positive augmentation-preserving chain map on suspended cube chains, with the original cubical differential above degree one. Indeed, for one free axis the differential splits an edge and subtracts its shortcut. For several free axes in one block, fixing an axis absent deletes that vertex with its simplicial sign. Fixing the first or last axis present supplies the two endpoint deletions, and every interior present face maps to zero. For several active blocks a nonzero facet image requires fixing the sole free axis in a block and leaving a single active block. Its two settings have the same image and opposite coefficients. Thus Steiner's tableau functor gives a strict functor.

Here is why the top-chain assertion determines the entire atom **for this map**. In the signed degree-$j$ row of the $m$-cube atom there is one face for each $j$-element free set $B$. At each omitted index $i$ its side is $\epsilon(-1)^{|\{b\in B:b<i\}|}$ for row sign $\epsilon$. To verify the formula by downward induction, fix $B$ one rank below the current row and write $s_i$ for these prescribed signs. Deleting one additional free index $t$ produces a prefix of $s_i$ before $t$ and a suffix of $-s_i$ after $t$. At $t$ the two settings give coefficients $\epsilon$ for $s_t$ and $-\epsilon$ for $-s_t$. Summing in the order of the omitted indices telescopes to the two prescribed rows with opposite coefficients. The endpoint rows have disjoint faces, giving exactly the iterated signed parts.

In the all-free cube, the images of the two rows at each proper positive suspended rank are disjoint. For an image simplex of dimension at least two its interior vertices determine its free set $B$. If one endpoint is internal it must be fixed present, which cannot happen on both opposite rows for this $B$. If both endpoints are the poles, every omitted internal index must be absent, also impossible on both rows. In degree one the direct edge and the fully subdivided path are disjoint for $n\ge2$. The two poles are distinct. Hence downward recursion by signed differential recovers exactly the atom tableau of $[0,\ldots,n]$. For a general face, restrict to its free axes and project onto each block by augmenting the other interval factors. On each subface the sum of the resulting block maps in positive suspended degrees is precisely the displayed basis formula. In degree one it concatenates the paths, and in degree zero the outer poles are the endpoints. The projected cube atom is the cube atom of the retained axes padded by identities: in the row formula only free sets contained in the retained axes survive, with the prescribed sides on those axes. This proves the horizontal-composite formula including all stationary lower rows.

**Lifting through rank five.** Inductively take one weak generator per nonempty simplex, using the same chosen declaration on each shared face. Given the proper suboriental, label the poles and then the proper faces of a suspended typed cube in increasing dimension by terms over those simplex blocks. Subfaces only refine the blocks or omit vertices, so all prescribed weak boundaries lie in the same block subcontext. It is already presented: every block of a proper face uses strictly fewer names than the top simplex. Its ordered edge graph has no cycles and its triangles expand nonempty paths, so Response 16 supplies identity-image RC(3) there, in addition to RC through degree two. Prescribed boundary lifting through rank four follows by lifting the strict expression and correcting its successive lower faces (as in Response 6). This applies to every proper suspended face for $n\le5$. Substituting into the suspended cube's top type now gives parallel weak terms with the literal strict faces of the simplex atom. Adjoin a variable of that type. Freeness of the oriental on its simplex atoms identifies the strict presentation. This construction works relative to the chosen proper face presentations, so inclusion of faces needs no change of weak representatives. Its cubical input through this rank is available by item 33.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_oriental_wedges.py`. It compares integer differentials and both sides of every row for every cubical face for $2\le n\le7$. The proof above supplies the unrestricted rank statement for the strict map. Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_oriental_terms.py` for the raw four-simplex. Its middle present square exchanges two horizontally consecutive splitting triangles. The solver constructs the two composites in the generic pasting context of two 2-discs, specializes the coherence in each direction, and infers the reversed arrow type with the original endpoints interchanged. Agda 2.8.0 accepted both specialized judgments in safe mode. Reproduce with `d=$(mktemp -d)`, `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_oriental_terms.py --agda "$d/OrientalExchange.agda"`, then `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$d" "$d/OrientalExchange.agda"`. The outer present squares whisker a tetrahedron and correct its literal faces. The Python checker verifies all 29 substitution entries, the 31 declarations and the signed boundary of degree $+1$. Normalizers and the comparison in a fresh three-variable use actual RC(3) in old syntax. Naturality is applied there, before the auxiliary base comparisons identify boundary factors. # Researcher Response 38

## A marked occurrence localizes the four-generator extension theorem to the actual three-dimensional slices

Let $\Lambda$ have dimension at most three and acyclic generating one-graph, with RC(3), and let $D$ be obtained by adjoining four-generators. For a strict four-cell $w$ of $D$, call $r$ a slice if $w=p*_3s$ with three-target of $p$ and three-source of $s$ equal to $r$. Allow identity factors. **If every pair of parallel old four-terms over $1_r$ has a five-term for every slice $r$ of $w$, then every pair of parallel four-terms over $w$ has a five-term in $D$.** The hypotheses concern whole slices, even when a suspected disagreement is inside a proper subexpression of a lower-cut whiskering.

**Proof.** On parallel four-terms in $D$ form the congruence generated by the following two relations: existence of an actual five-term, and identification of every parallel pair of old four-terms in $\Lambda$. In the second relation a parallel pair has the same strict image, since the base has dimension three. Actual five-terms in $D$ are invertible up to higher terms by empty top support. Congruence here means substitution in well-typed four-term expressions at a four-input of fixed type, with the lower syntax fixed. Such expressions preserve actual comparison in either available direction by maximal-slot naturality, iterated from the occurrence out to the enclosing head. Top slots are maximal in each such head. A congruence derivation can consequently be read as a zigzag of single-occurrence replacements under contexts. None of these replacements changes the strict four-cell.

In this congruence any parallel terms over $w$ are equal. To check the use of the conditional extension theorem here, run its proof modulo the indicated congruence. The three-term normalizers and the comparison $J(X)$ of formal three-contexts are constructed using actual RC(3), also in the fresh three-variable extension. The normalization of any old four-term to the identity is exactly an instance of the second relation after conjugation. The contextual comparison for one new four-generator follows by actual depth-one naturality of $J(X)$ and those old identifications. Generic head expansion, exchange and sequentialization use actual five-terms. Composition of these equations and insertion in contexts are precisely the congruence operations. This proves the equality without constructing higher naturality for a formal equation. [Conditional extension proof, equations (14)–(20)](../answered/gray-contexts-r8-2026-11-18.md#2-normalize-in-a-quotient-category)

Consider one replacement $C[R]\leadsto C[S]$ from that derivation. For an actual comparison, the context action already supplies a five-term. For an old equation, mark its occurrence by adjoining **one fresh four-variable** $Y$ of the common literal type of $R,S$; take the term $C[Y]$. In this marked context use the unconstrained serialization lemma of round 6, Response 19. It gives an actual comparison, relative to the literal ends, to a cut-three string whose steps each contain at most one occurrence of any four-generator in total. The marked site occurs once in the top count, since a four-input occurs once in the top composite of its globular pasting head. Equivalently this follows by multiplying the occurrence counts along the branch to that one site. Hence just one step contains $Y$, and that step contains **no other four-generator**. The three-ends of every step are independent of $Y$ by dimension. [Serialization lemma and its proof by subdividing top slots](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-19)

Substitute $Y:=R$ and $Y:=S$ into the *same* serialization comparison. Every unmarked step is literally the same after the two substitutions. The marked step becomes a pair of parallel old four-terms by exact support. Their strict image is an identity $1_r$. The prefix and suffix of this very string factor $w$ at $r$. The slice hypothesis therefore compares the two marked steps at their full literal width. Whisker this comparison by the common prefix and suffix, then use the two specialized serialization comparisons, reversing the second by empty top support. This realizes the replacement in $D$. Composing along the derivation proves the assertion. This argument places no restriction on the size of $R,S$, on the other slots, or on powers of loops allowed by their types. They enter as substitutions into the marked term. The single-occurrence count is imposed on the new symbol $Y$ alone.

**Six-seam consequence.** Apply the lemma to the seven labels of Response 36. There are exactly **17 different slice labels**, all full on seven names and all of underlying dimension three. The solver requires nonnegative coefficients and membership of *both complete factor tableaux* in the computed strict category. These tests are necessary and sufficient because cut-three composition retains the lower rows and adds the top chains. Exactly one prefix survives at each length, and the unique prefixes are nested. The seven walks have lengths $3,3,1,0,1,3,3$. Deduplication gives 14 unary steps and 17 three-states. This finite computation exhausts strict factorizations of the specified cells. ## A signed cube fold detects failure after substitution on fourteen seven-vertex labels

There is a three-dimensional strong-Steiner context on the 98 oriented simplices of dimensions zero through three on seven vertices, and a list of **14 complete strict three-cells** in it, with the following property. If the six-seam ring satisfies identity-image RC through degree eight, two parallel four-terms over one of the fourteen identities have no five-term in its support. The two terms can be taken to be **images under the same typed substitution** of two terms on a twenty-cube state of Response 39.
