These are additional possible witnesses of different weights from both the oriental and cubical alternatives.

**The fold.** At a vertex of the six-cube, compare its bits with those of seed 16, numbering axes from zero. Its image is zero if they agree, otherwise one plus the greatest axis on which they differ. For a cubical face take the set $S$ of attainable vertex images. Send its basis chain to the atom on $S$ if $|S|$ is one more than the face dimension, and to zero otherwise. Orient each target simplex so this map has positive coefficients. In this example its sign relative to increasing vertex order is negative precisely when 5 is a nonleast element of $S$. In particular the oriented six-chain has degree $-1$.

This construction works on chains for any full binary decision tree whose internal nodes test distinct axes, one axis per node, with either sign. Name its leaves by distinct vertices. Begin with mass one at the root. At a node with coordinate $u$ distribute the incoming mass in proportions $(1-u,u)$ to its children, exchanging them when the sign is reversed. The leaf masses are barycentric coordinates of a continuous map of cellular pairs from cube to simplex. In the relative interior of a face omit unreachable nodes and suppress deterministic branches. Each remaining free node splits a positive mass, so the surviving free coordinates are recovered individually as child-to-parent mass ratios. The map in these coordinates is a homeomorphism onto the relative interior of the simplex on the reachable leaves. An unreachable free axis lowers image dimension. For a given leaf set the surviving nodes are exactly the branch vertices of its spanned subtree. Their cube order and signed mass-splitting functions are independent of the preimage face. This orients each basis simplex consistently, making the displayed positive basis map the cellular chain map.

In the seed-16 example the solver checks all 729 faces: their differential coefficients, both signs of every tableau row, and equality with the atom of their reachable set padded by identities. It checks augmentation and topologically sorts the oriented incidence relation on all 127 basis simplices. Thus the unital strong-Steiner complex presents the free strict category on these atoms, and applying the tableau functor gives the specified strict fold. This finite check of freeness and of the complete atom images concerns this particular signed target.

**The typed substitution.** Choose a common weak three-skeleton of the signed simplex. Its edges form a linear order after reordering the vertices, and each triangle has nonempty input and output. The solver realizes each tetrahedral front by firing its indicated triangles in path contexts and aligning parentheses, checks the two literal types, and recomputes the signed boundary of each generator. To present the four-skeleton, choose weak representatives of the two strict three-faces of each four-atom with common prescribed lower faces in its proper simplex. The boundary correction of Response 7 uses only identity-image RC through degree two here. Adjoin a four-variable of that type. This gives the signed simplex four-skeleton by freeness. Now send each cubical declaration through rank four to a lift of its folded tableau at the already prescribed boundary, working in its simplex support. This time the correction uses RC through degree three, supplied by Response 16. Every cubical five-facet maps into a proper simplex. For each adjoin a five-variable of its actual substituted type there, evaluating it strictly to its folded five-cell, including when that cell is an identity.

Use the marked proof of Response 38 **in the cube before substitution**. Every old-equation replacement at a three-slice has an actual serialization in the marked cubical context. Specialize its two sides, then apply the four-skeleton substitution. The differing stationary terms now lie in the support of the folded three-label, by exact support. For a proper label supply an auxiliary five-generator there; its simplex chain vanishes, since its ends live in the three-skeleton. For a full label it suffices to supply a comparison of those two substituted terms. Generic comparisons and common prefix and suffix terms can all be substituted. Consequently, if every requested full pair can be compared, all twelve membrane comparisons of Response 12 exist in the target enlarged only by proper five-generators. This reasoning requires no assertion that strict factorizations in the target lift back through the fold.

The top boundary assembly of Responses 12 and 14 then yields a relative six-generator. Its simplex degree is $-1$. On a five-facet this computes the signed folded five-chain by its boundary, since it is carried on at most six names and the five-boundary there is injective. The auxiliary contributions are zero. Each original five-facet occurs once with its incidence sign, and the corrections contribute only proper auxiliaries. **The count.** In the negative five-face fire the facets omitting $2,1$, in that order. In the positive face fire those omitting $3,6,5,0,4$. The checker verifies each input row, the remaining nonnegative output, and the common initial and final four-fronts. The two paths visit seven distinct fronts. For each, every top four-atom occurs once. For every subset taken as a prefix, add its differential to the initial three-row. A nonnegative row gives the two factor tableaux: the prefix has that target, the complementary suffix has that source, the common lower rows come from the front, and the complementary differential gives exactly its final row. Conversely every cut-three factorization partitions the four-atoms in this way. This yields 25 distinct complete three-tableaux, of which 12 contain all seven vertices.

Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_simplex_sweeps.py --slices` for the two paths, the twelve tableaux and the 98-declaration raw three-skeleton. The solver verifies the signed cellular boundaries of all declarations, including both literal faces of the 35 tetrahedra. The number twelve is a sufficient list for this signed basis and these paths. Deciding all weak comparisons on that list is open. [Flag, sweeps and slice enumeration](../../../tools/relative_coherence_simplex_sweeps.py)

# Researcher Response 42

## All paths of scheduled exchanges on the twelve labels have five-dimensional comparisons

For each label of Response 41 one can choose weak representatives of its tetrahedron firings and of their adjacent exchanges such that **any two exchange paths with the same endpoints admit a five-term in each direction** in the label's three-dimensional support. The endpoints are bracketed three-terms representing complete firing schedules. There are 341 schedules in total. Only two local diagrams require the braid relation, and the same typed substitution handles both.

**Which paths this covers.** For a fixed label, use one two-term per prefix ideal and one three-term for adding a ready tetrahedron there. For each pair of ready tetrahedra use a four-term comparing their two cut-two composites and an inverse for the opposite orientation. Whisker this local term by the unchanged prefix and suffix of the word using coherent reassociation to a fixed bracketing. In particular, two occurrences of the same local square use the same choice. All choices away from the triple may be made freely subject to these rules.

At a triple make its eight states, twelve edges and six squares simultaneously by substituting in the cut-one string of three three-discs. Its generic opposite square heads have cancellation five-terms by full support on the face arity. There is at most one such prescription per label. Use the common one-poles of this cube throughout that label. At those poles lift the other two-states by prescribed-boundary lifting, and then lift the remaining three-edges between the chosen two-states by the same lemma through RC(2). With no triple, choose common one-poles first. Response 16 supplies an oriented four-comparison between the two three-composites of each remaining square since their strict tableaux agree. It is weakly invertible by its empty four-generator support. All lifts can be made in the support of the label. This constructs the choices used in the statement.

**Finite computation.** In the order of Response 41 the counts of linear extensions are 25, 48, 12, 30, 24, 33, 32, 21, 78, 31, 2 and 5.

Every triangle has at most one tetrahedron producing it and at most one consuming it. No initially present triangle is produced and no finally present triangle is consumed. A legal firing therefore respects the producer-before-consumer relation. Conversely the solver visits every prefix ideal and checks that its minimally available events are exactly the firings with available inputs and fresh outputs. A top-cut serialization must use each indicated tetrahedron once by its strict count. The triangle state at each prefix is forced by its differential.

There are 86 choices of a ready pair at a prefix and two choices of a ready triple. For every such choice the solver replaces its disjoint input fronts by markers, orders the resulting triangle and marker layers between the outer paths, and expands every corner to check its triangle counts. It checks the typed substitutions for the 32 distinct placements. The triples are on labels 8 and 10. Reading down their common cut-one string gives tetrahedron 0136, tetrahedron 1456 prefixed by edge 01, and tetrahedron 0125 suffixed by edge 56. The consecutive paths are (03|36, 01|16, 01|15|56, 05|56). Path comparisons in the substitution align the literal parentheses. On this actual placement the forward and reverse five-terms compare the routes of swaps `010` and `101`. Their four-dimensional boundaries include the whiskered face terms and reassociations. The Agda certificate checks both judgments by substitution and the solver checks weakening from the dependency closure back to the 98-declaration context.

**Why arbitrary paths are covered.** Fix one linear extension and sort towards it by exchanging neighbouring events inverted relative to it. Every unsorted word has an adjacent inversion. Such events are incomparable and every step decreases the inversion count. Two disjoint inverted pairs give a commuting square by the generic interchange of two four-arrows at cut two. The prefix ideal before the later pair is the same on both sides, so its actual local face agrees. Rebracketing and inserting intervening unchanged terms is legitimate by generic pasting coherence, or equivalently by bicategory coherence in the twice-iterated hom modulo five-comparison. Two overlapping inverted pairs form a descending triple. Its three events must be pairwise incomparable since the fixed order and the present word order all three oppositely. All three can therefore fire at the first position of the triple. The six words on those positions form the checked hexagon. Both directions of its relation and cancellation of opposite edges give the two routes around it from any starting order.

Induction on inversions now compares any two sorting paths by joining their first steps with the relevant square or hexagon and applying induction to the shorter tails. Append fixed sorting paths to the two ends of any one exchange and cancel to see that it equals the corresponding difference of sorting paths modulo five-comparison. Composing gives the result for every finite zigzag. The proof also works for any finite event poset with compatible typed lifts of its triple diagrams and the same local exchange choices. Generic interchange, cancellation and braid witnesses survive every well-typed substitution, including arbitrary inactive labels. The comparison applies to every finite power of a loop, with no bound on the length of an exchange path or on the terms supplied to its inactive slots.

**Implication for relative coherence.** A failure forced by the six-seam hypothesis in Response 41 must distinguish four-terms beyond these chosen exchange paths. The remaining question includes whether an independently given raw four-term is five-comparable to such a path. The sorting proof makes no normalization assumption on those raw terms.

python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_simplex_exchanges.py --agda "$dir/SliceBraid.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/SliceBraid.agda"

# Researcher Response 43

## The six-seam hypothesis forces an infinite-order based four-loop invisible to cellular counts

Write $J=\{0,\ldots,6\}$. Let $A$ be the free weak category on all brackets of $P(J)$ supported on at most **four** names, in all dimensions. Define a larger subcomputad of $P(J)$ in declaration order and call its free weak category $B$. Include all brackets on at most **five** names. On six names also include five-brackets whose two four-ends have identical four-generator count vectors, and all brackets in dimensions at least six, provided their boundaries have already been included. Both constructions commute with vertex relabelling: relabelling pushes count vectors forward, including when brackets become equal.

Suppose the six-seam ring has identity-image RC through degree eight. Then there is a class in the algebraic group $\pi_4(A,0)$ with **infinite-order image in $\pi_4(B,0)$**. It is the difference of two literal four-endomorphisms of $1^3_0$ with *identical four-generator counts in $A$*. In particular its image in the integral cellular $H_4$ of the groupoidified realization is zero. Identifying any two names by a pointed vertex map kills its image in $B$. One may furthermore choose an infinite-order class in the source kernel of cellular counts modulo boundaries killed already in $A$ by each of the six individual maps $i\mapsto0$. The unconditioned conclusion is a test: torsion of the indicated based differences after inclusion in $B$ would disprove the six-seam hypothesis.

**Transport to the basepoint.** In a bracket subcomputad containing all brackets on a set of names, a generator $g=[s,t]$ has reverse $[t,s]$. The brackets from each of the two composites to the corresponding identity and back give both cancellation laws. Iterating gives coinductive invertibility on the same names. For an added six-name five-generator of $B$, the swapped five-bracket satisfies the same count test and its cancellation laws start in degree six. Thus all generating cells of $A,B$ are invertible. Closure under weak operations makes both free algebras groupoidal. Here $\pi_4$ uses four-loops at $1^3_0$ modulo five-comparison; composition at cut three gives its group law, abelian by interchange with the next lower composition and the unit constraints. The count of a four-loop is a cellular cycle, and a five-comparison changes its count by a cellular boundary. These facts define the map to cellular homology used above. [Closure of invertible cells, Theorem 3.3.7](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.14907)

Follow the marked assembly of Response 41, taking actual five-brackets $h=[u,v]$ in $P(J)$ for the missing local comparisons. Their ends come from the old three-skeleton and have zero four-counts. If $h$ has proper support it belongs to $B$. For a full label, regard the old three-skeleton as mapped into $A$ by sending each simplex generator to the bracket on its substituted ends. The common two-dimensional poles of $u,v$, with all lower faces, are carried on $\{0,3,5,6\}$ by exact support and the common lower rows of the twelve labels. Whisker $u,v$ together at cuts zero, one and two as in Response 13, using brackets on these four names to put their two-poles literally at $1^2_0$. All pads have dimension at most three. Write $a,b$ for the resulting three-poles. They are now three-loops at $1^2_0$. Whisker once more at cut two by a weak inverse of $a$ in $A$. Its new source $a'$ is equivalent to $1^3_0$ by cancellation; its new target $b'$ is equivalent to $a'$ by the transported $u$. Choose a four-pad from $1^3_0$ to $a'$ by cancellation and a four-pad from $b'$ to $1^3_0$ using the inverse of the transported $u$ and cancellation. These are terms of $A$ even when $a,b$ use every name. Apply both pads to both terms at cut three to obtain based terms $L_h,R_h$. Their four-count vectors agree exactly, since all earlier pads were lower dimensional and both last pads are identical on the two sides.

Perform the same whiskerings on $h$ itself in $P(J)$. This gives $\widehat h:L_h\to R_h$ with top count **one copy of $h$**. All choices of inverse and cancellation data above live in the free weak algebra and so are actual typed terms. Define $\alpha_h=[R_h]-[L_h]$. After a pointed identification of two names the two relabelled four-ends use at most six names and have equal counts. Their primitive five-bracket in $B$ directly witnesses vanishing.

**Why the order must be infinite.** If the image of $\alpha_h$ is annihilated by $m>0$, there is an actual five-term $g$ in $B$ from $L_h^m$ to $R_h^m$, using the same bracketing to take both powers. Compose $m$ copies of $\widehat h$ at cut three. Its ends are those same powers by the globular face formula. In $P(J)$ take the six-bracket from $g$ to that composite. Its cellular boundary is $m h-N_5(g)$. Every summand of $N_5(g)$ has proper support and zero simplex five-chain. On five names the dimension forces this; for an allowed six-name generator the cellular boundary vanishes by its count test, hence its cone chain vanishes.

The original finite assembly with the formal $h$'s gives a six-bracket of signed simplex degree $\pm1$. Its proper original facets contribute the signed simplex boundary; each formal $h$ has zero simplex chain since its ends have zero four-counts. If every full $h$ used had torsion image, take a common positive multiple $M$ of their annihilators. Take $M$ copies of the assembly column, then subtract the signed replacement columns at multiplicity $M$ for every occurrence of a full $h$. The result is a finite integral six-chain whose boundary has only proper support and whose simplex degree is $\pm M$. Each replacement column has degree zero by its displayed boundary and the cone formula. Response 11 excludes **every nonzero integral degree** under the ring hypothesis. This proves infinite order for at least one of these specific based differences. The proof permits arbitrary inactive labels in the marked terms. It uses their strict counts and common poles, and the power construction is an induction under binary composition for every positive integer.

Finally write $e_i$ for the pointed collapse $i\mapsto0$. The operators on $\pi_4$ commute. Apply $\prod_{i=1}^6(1-e_i)$ in $A$ to an infinite-order witness. Its image in $B$ is unchanged because each nonempty product of collapses kills the original image. Its cellular homology class remains zero by functoriality, and each $e_i$ kills it already in $A$ by idempotence. This gives a rational obstruction in a single based homotopy group. Its existence without the six-seam hypothesis remains open.

**Reproduction and scope.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_simplex_transport.py --paths`. The solver checks the shared exterior tableau of 0356 and computes the entire component under signed elementary four-simplex flips. It equals the 25 fronts of Response 41. The shortest **combinatorial** distances from 0356 to the twelve full labels are 4, 5, 4, 3, 5, 4, 5, 4, 4, 3, 4 and 3. For groupoidal transport it verifies the literal reverse and both orientations of the two cancellation laws for each of the 91 positive-dimensional generators of the signed three-skeleton, including the actual compound fronts of all 35 tetrahedra. Every new declaration stays on the names of the original atom. On a four-term pair at 0356 it checks the powered replacement column, its literal ends and all its top counts at multiplicities 1, 2, 3 and 5. The finite checks verify the input to the degree argument, which gives the infinite-order conclusion. [Transport and powered-column solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_simplex_transport.py)

# Researcher Response 44

## A global integral degree potential splits off the based obstruction

Under the hypothesis of Response 43, the image of the cellular-count kernel from $\pi_4(A,0)$ in $\pi_4(B,0)$ contains a **split integral summand** $\mathbb Z$. A signed combination of the actual comparisons in the assembly has value one under a homomorphism $\pi_4(B,0)\to\mathbb Z$. More generally the one-bridge hypothesis through degree $k+2$ gives a single integer potential on **all** full-colour $(k-1)$-brackets on $k+1$ names, simultaneously solving the equations of Response 19.

**The integral cochain on the infinite parameter.** Use the $k$-skeleton $X$ of $X(J)$ and its proper-colour union $Y$, with $|J|=k+1$. Form $(T,T_\partial)$ and the two maps $t,p$ of Response 11 for this entire parameter. This construction extends over any number of cells in each dimension. The choices on a boundary precede the choice on its generator, and the CW weak topology gives continuity. On $T_\partial$ the maps are homotopic in $\partial I^k$ by the same contractible carriers. Extend to a homotopy of pairs on $T$: prescribe its two ends and the given boundary homotopy and extend over each remaining cell times the interval in the contractible cube. Since $p(T)\subseteq\partial I^k$, the pullback under $t$ of the integral relative orientation class is zero.

There is a **chain map** $S:C_*(X,Y)\to C_*(T,T_\partial)$ given by the signed sum of flag inclusions. Indeed a generator missing an interior rank cancels in pairs under the corresponding adjacent transposition. A generator missing an exterior rank lands in $T_\partial$. This works in every chain degree. Use the staircase triangulation of the cube. Its integral relative orientation cochain can be represented by the cochain equal to one on the increasing flag simplex and zero on the other top simplices: it evaluates to one on the oriented fundamental chain. Choose a cellular carried map of $X$ to the ordered simplex and use it on each flag in $t$. Pulling this cochain back along $t$ and $S$ gives the ordered simplex degree cochain. Only the increasing flag can contribute. The carried cellular map and the least-vertex chain cone are chain homotopic in the supporting faces, by induction in the acyclic simplex chains. This homotopy preserves the proper-colour subcomplex. Therefore the specific cone degree row $d$ of Response 19 is an **integral relative coboundary**. There is an integer cochain $\lambda$ on the full-colour $(k-1)$-generators with $\delta\lambda=d$ on every $k$-generator. This constructs its existence directly on the infinite CW complex, independently of finite matrix solvability.

Extend $\lambda$ by zero on proper generators. Write $w(g)$ for the coefficient of the facet $[1,\ldots,k]$ in the simplex chain of a $(k-1)$-generator. The simplicial boundary formula gives $\delta w=d$. Thus $z=w-\lambda$ is an integral cocycle on $P(J)$ with prescribed value $w$ on **every proper generator**. In rank six, $z$ vanishes on every five-generator of $B$ by the two support and count conditions of Response 43.

**Reading the cocycle one dimension lower.** For a based four-loop $T$ in $B$ put $D(T)=[1^4_0,T]$ in the unrestricted parameter and $F(T)=z(D(T))$. For any five-term $G:T\to T'$ in $P(J)$ on such based ends,
$$
 F(T')-F(T)=z(N_5(G)).
$$
Indeed the six-bracket comparing $D(T)*_4 G$ with $D(T')$ has precisely this cocycle equation. Hence $F$ is constant on five-comparisons in $B$. The product $D(T)*_3 D(U)$ has source $1^4_0 *_3 1^4_0$. Precompose with the five-bracket from $1^4_0$ to that source, on the singleton vertex, and compare with $D(T*_3U)$ by a six-bracket. The singleton term has $z$-value zero. This proves additivity, so $F$ defines an integer homomorphism on $\pi_4(B,0)$, including on inverses and all powers.

For the terms of Response 43 this gives $F(\alpha_h)=z(h)$, because the padded $\widehat h$ counts $h$ exactly once. Write $c_h$ for the coefficient of each full auxiliary bracket in the boundary of the signed assembly. The cocycle vanishes on the boundary of its six-bracket. Its evaluation on the proper part is $\pm1$: the seven simplex facets contribute their incidence signs times $w$, and every proper auxiliary contributes zero. Thus $\sum_h c_h F(\alpha_h)=\mp1$. Take the corresponding signed combination with sign chosen to give $+1$, and apply the projector $E=\prod_{i=1}^6(1-e_i)$ in $A$ as in Response 43. Its image in $B$ still has value one, giving the claimed retraction in both groups. This proof uses only the top counts of the marked comparisons and works for every allowed choice of their inactive inputs.

On any pointed set with finite or countably infinite set $M$ of other vertices use the same support rules for $A,B$. There is a split copy of $\bigoplus_{S\subseteq M,\ |S|=6}\mathbb Z$ in each based four-group, carried isomorphically under inclusion and with zero cellular homology image. Embed the projected class on each ordered six-subset and take as coordinates $F$ after the retractions onto those six names and the basepoint. For unequal subsets some positive input collapses to zero, which kills the projected class. For equal subsets the coordinate is one. To obtain direct-sum-valued coordinates on *all* elements in the infinite case, use $F E$ after each retraction. A class is represented by finite syntax, and a coordinate is zero unless all six positive names occur. The projected classes are already fixed by $E$. This supplies the retraction on the entire group.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_flag_potential.py`. It checks every rank subset at each rank from two through seven, grouping flags by their actual surviving cube vertices, and checks coefficient one on the selected simplex. In rank six this uses all 720 flags. It separately checks the two transgression identities on literal six-brackets, including the singleton alignment and every boundary coefficient. The cochain and its value-one class above are conditional on the ring hypothesis. The calculation constructs neither a global potential unconditionally nor a decision on the ring. # Researcher Response 45

## The split subgroup vanishes in the ordinary fourth homotopy group of the realization

Let $A,B$ be the two groupoidal free models in Response 43. If the six-seam ring satisfies identity-image RC through degree eight, the comparison from algebraic to topological fourth homotopy at $0$ has a **split copy of $\mathbb Z$ in its kernel**, for each of $A$ and $B$. On the larger pointed sets of Response 44 the entire displayed free summand lies in the kernel. Equivalently, injectivity of this comparison on the single seven-name model $A$ would disprove the six-seam hypothesis. The comparison here sends a literal based four-cell to its realized sphere. A five-cell gives a homotopy, gluing at cut three gives addition, and relabelling commutes with this map. Equivalently use the realization unit followed by groupoidification.

**Why the space is two-connected.** Let $X$ be the groupoidified cellular realization of the subcomputad of $P(V)$ with generator supports of size at most four, for any nonempty $V$. Its generating arrows are the edges $e_{xy}=[x,y]$. Its triangles
$$
 T_{q;xy}=[e_{qx}*_0 e_{xy},e_{qy}]
$$
give the relations $e_{qx}e_{xy}=e_{qy}$ in the fundamental groupoid. Fix $q$. These relations express every edge as $e_{qx}^{-1}e_{qy}$ and telescope around any edge loop, so $\pi_1(X)=0$.

For a raw one-term $t:x\to y$ construct a two-term $c_q(t):e_{qx}*_0t\to e_{qy}$ with exactly one copy of $T_{q;ab}$ per occurrence of $e_{ab}$ in its strict word. To do so align $e_{qx}*_0t$ with the left-bracketed edge word by a structural two-term in the one-skeleton. RC(1) gives this alignment even for repeated edges and the empty word. Successively apply the indicated triangles to the leftmost pair, whiskering by the remaining suffix. Each step shortens the word once. The construction uses only $q$ and the names of $t$.

For any two-generator $g=[u,v]$ with common one-source object $x$, compare $c_q(u)$ with $(e_{qx}*_0 g)*_1c_q(v)$ by their three-bracket. The two literal ends agree as required, including for empty paths. Writing $C_q(e_{ab})=T_{q;ab}$ linearly on edge counts, its cellular boundary is
$$
                  g+C_q(\partial g).
$$
First choose $q$ separately in the support of each $g$. Each three-bracket is allowed, and this relation converts any cellular two-cycle, modulo a boundary, to a two-cycle of triangles on at most three names each. Now use a *single* $q$ for all of those triangles. Their three-brackets use at most four names even when the original cycle uses many more. The second replacement sum is zero because $C_q$ is linear and the triangle chain is closed. Thus $H_2(X;\mathbb Z)=0$ and the simply connected Hurewicz theorem gives $\pi_2(X)=0$. There is no bound on lengths of the original words or on powers of an edge loop.

**The projector in stable homotopy.** Let $V=\{0,\ldots,6\}$ and use the six commuting collapses $e_i$ of Response 43, now as based cellular maps. Every generator of $X$ misses at least one positive name, and that collapse fixes the entire corresponding characteristic cell. On the quotient of successive skeleta, a wedge of spheres, $E=\prod_i(1-e_{i*})$ consequently acts by zero on reduced stable homotopy. Indeed on each sphere summand the missing-name factor annihilates its inclusion; these commuting factors define a sum of maps in the stable category, and a map out of the wedge is determined on all its summands. The same argument applies in dimension zero relative to the basepoint.

The exact sequences of the skeleta now show that $E$ lowers the skeletal filtration on reduced stable homotopy. Every class in degree four has finite filtration, by cellular approximation after suspension. Iterating $E$ kills it, and $E^2=E$, so already $E=0$. Two-connectivity puts $\pi_4(X,0)$ in the integral stable range by Freudenthal. Naturality therefore gives **zero on the ordinary $\pi_4$ as well**. This topological calculation is unconditional. More generally the filtration argument works in every stable degree whenever each individual cell is fixed by one of the commuting missing-name collapses. [Freudenthal suspension theorem, Chapter 10](https://math.stanford.edu/~ralph/bookR4.pdf)

The class of Response 44 is fixed by $E$ in the algebraic group and has an integral retraction under the ring hypothesis. Its realized class is fixed by the topological $E$, hence zero. The same follows for its images in $B$ and on larger vertex sets by naturality. In particular the kernel statement requires neither rationalization nor passage to cellular homology. Whether the algebraic projector actually vanishes on this free groupoidal model is the remaining comparison problem.

**Reproduction.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_path_cones.py`. It takes a signed two-cycle of three path comparisons with repeated and stationary edges, checks each local three-bracket against its inferred ends, changes the three different cone vertices to one common vertex, and verifies that every cellular coefficient cancels. A separate empty-path versus loop-power comparison checks the unitor case. The 63 raw declarations have at most four names each although the calculation uses five names overall. The argument for all raw paths uses the structural alignment followed by one triangle per occurrence. [Path cone and integral two-cycle checker](../../../tools/relative_coherence_path_cones.py)

# Researcher Response 46

## Point joins give a cellular contraction through degree four and a binary test for degree five

For arbitrary nonempty $V$, the integral groups $H_j X(V)$ of the unrestricted bracket parameter vanish for $1\le j\le4$. With a support bound, the inclusion from brackets on at most $m$ names to brackets on at most $m+1$ names induces zero in the same positive degrees. There is a precise remaining step at rank five. **If RC(4) holds on the three-skeleton of every left point join of a globular pasting arity of height at most four, the six-seam ring fails identity-image RC by degree eight.** Under the contrary ring hypothesis, some such finite three-skeleton fails RC(4). In its two- and three-dimensional declarations the strict source is always one generator and the target contains either one or two generators of the immediately preceding rank.

**The cone of a raw term.** Write $K(P)=a\star P$ with the convention of §1.3. For a variable $x$ use its mixed generator $p_x$, of dimension $\dim x+1$. For an arbitrary object $x$ it has type $a\to x$. For an $n$-term $t$ with $n>0$ define its required cone type inductively. The source is $p_{t_{n-1}}$, where $t_{n-1}$ is the immediate target of $t$. The target is obtained by starting with $t$ and successively whiskering on the left at cuts $0,\ldots,n-1$ by the cones on its respective source faces. These are parallel by the globular face formulas for the binary heads. In the strict point join the distinguished cell of this type is the image of the mixed generator of $a\star D^n$ along the join of the globe selecting the strict value of $t$. Its top count has one mixed copy of each top occurrence of $t$, with multiplicity. In the augmented directed chain model of the join this is the linear map taking each top basis element $b$ to $a\star b$; a promoted identity has zero top chain. [Point joins and the chain model, Ara and Maltsiniotis](https://arxiv.org/abs/1607.00668)

Construct $p_h$ generically for each raw head $h$ on its pasting arity $P$. First choose the cones on its lower-dimensional faces. Lift the indicated strict join cell with exactly those literal faces by prescribed-boundary lifting, using RC through the dimension of $h$ on $K(P)$. The point join is unital strong Steiner. Its strict endomorphisms have identity top cell, by the maximal-basis argument of Response 15, so the RC hypotheses here give identity-image corrections. Through degree three they hold: the one-graph is acyclic, the two-skeleton is bubble-free, and Responses 7 and 16 apply. For an instance of a head substitute its argument terms and their cones into the one chosen generic $p_h$. Judgments for the mixed variables are precisely the required cone types on those arguments. This substitution respects the literal faces by induction on the head dimension for the generic choice and on the labelled syntax for the instance, as in Response 15. Functoriality of the strict point join gives the required strict value when the labelling is itself generic. This constructs the actions through degree three with no bound on the number of variables of $P$.

**The chain identity.** Choose one base vertex $a\in V$. For every generator $g$ of $P(V)$ choose as $p_g$ the primitive bracket of its prescribed cone type, starting with $[a,x]$ on an object. Its dependency colours lie in $\{a\}\cup\operatorname{supp}(g)$ by the construction on smaller terms. Denote this generator by $C(g)$ and extend $C$ linearly. In the degrees with generic actions, every raw term satisfies
$$
                 N_{n+1}(p_t)=C(N_n(t)).
$$
For a variable this is the definition. At a labelled head only its mixed top slots can contribute to dimension $n+1$. Their counts in the generic lift are exactly the strict join counts above. Substituting the actual cones into those slots proves the formula, including repeated slots, identity labels and arbitrary inactive terms.

The extra dimension in the homology conclusion matters. For an $(n+1)$-generator $g:s\to t$, its cone is a primitive bracket; only its two **$n$-term** faces require generic actions. Its source counts $C(N_n(t))$. At the target the last whiskering counts $C(N_n(s))$ and one copy of $g$; earlier pads are lower-dimensional. Thus
$$
          \partial C(g)+C(\partial g)=g,\qquad \partial C(x)=x-a .
$$
Through generator dimension four these are unconditional identities on cellular chains. They contract the inclusion with the stated support increase. They apply over any coefficient ring by extension of scalars and to every finite multiplicity. They also show that generic cone actions through degree four would give $H_5 X(\{0,\ldots,6\})=0$, contradicting the integral class forced by the six-seam hypothesis in Response 7.

**Why three-skeleta suffice.** For a four-dimensional generic head $h$, the needed strict cell in $K(P)$ has dimension five. Present the join through dimension five by the mixed-variable types above. To obtain a five-term at the prescribed ends it suffices to have identity-image RC through degree four in its four-skeleton (Response 7). If its three-skeleton has RC(4), Response 38 supplies RC(4) after adjoining the four-generators: every slice comparison in its hypothesis is then available in the old three-skeleton. The lower RC conditions hold as above. This proves the implication, and also permits witness terms for a failure under the ring hypothesis to be taken over one strict three-label in the three-skeleton. If the label omitted the apex, its terms would lie in the old globular context by exact support; the globular boundary-lifting theorem would compare them there. Thus the support of a failing label contains the apex.

Concretely the old declarations come from the globular generators of the arity. The new edges are $p_x:a\to x$. For $f:x\to y$ the only new two-generator is $p_f:p_y\to p_x*_0 f$. For $\alpha:f\to g$ the only new three-generator is $p_\alpha:p_g\to p_f *_1(p_x *_0 \alpha)$. All higher declarations may be omitted in the test. These contexts are strong Steiner by the point-join construction and truncation. The binary description is a restriction on the generators in the entire possible counterexample; the raw terms being compared can have any size. Establishing RC(4) for this family remains open.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_point_joins.py`. It constructs the literal left point joins on all 186 planar trees of height at most four with at most six tree edges. It typechecks 2,212 mixed declarations in ranks one through five and verifies $\partial C+C\partial$ coefficient by coefficient for every variable. The rank counts are 601, 778, 547, 235 and 51. The existence of the generic head actions through rank three uses prescribed-boundary lifting, independently of this enumeration. [Point-join checker](../../../tools/relative_coherence_point_joins.py)

# Researcher Response 47

## The original 64-cube class detects every integral and modular six-seam degree

Fix the unit-degree five-cube $g$ on the six positive names $I=\{1,\ldots,6\}$ from Response 9, choosing its chain sign for degree $+1$. Let $i:I\hookrightarrow J=\{0\}\cup I$, $E=\prod_{j\in I}(1-e_j)$, and $z=Eig$. For $R=\mathbb Z$ or $\mathbb Z/m$ the following test is **necessary and sufficient** for the relative degree $d\in R$ of Response 11:
$$
         d z+ Ei b=\partial w
$$
for an **absolute integral five-cycle** $b$ on the six positive names and a six-chain $w$ on $J$, with the equation interpreted over $R$. In particular a search over arbitrary relative lifts of the opposite five-simplex gains no new degrees beyond the fixed 64 signed labellings and absolute-cycle corrections. The result concerns exactly the degree obstruction; deciding whether the displayed relation holds at nonzero degree remains open.

**Repairing the opposite boundary.** Perform the following construction on each nonempty face of the ordered simplex. Write $h$ for the support-preserving cellular projection to simplex chains. There is a chain section $s$ through dimension five. Set $s(v)=v$. On an oriented basis simplex $D$ of positive dimension take $s(D)=C_q(s(\partial D))$, using $q=\min D$ and the cone of Response 46. In dimension one use the augmentation-zero identity on the two endpoints. The identity $\partial C+C\partial=1$ gives $\partial s(D)=s(\partial D)$. The image $h s(D)$ equals $D$: they have the same boundary by induction and the top boundary of the simplex on $D$ is injective. All choices use only names of $D$.

There is also a carried chain homotopy $K$ through dimension four satisfying $\partial K+K\partial=1-sh$. In degree zero take $K=0$. For a positive-dimensional generator $u$ of dimension at most four, take
$$
       K(u)=C_{\min\operatorname{supp}(u)}
              \big(u-s h(u)-K(\partial u)\big).
$$
The expression in parentheses is a cycle by induction, so its cone has the required boundary. It lies in the support of $u$. Both formulas are integral; reduce coefficients afterwards.

Response 26 characterizes degree $d$ by an opposite-face relative five-cycle $y$ of that degree with $Eiy$ a boundary. Let $b_0=y-dg$ using absolute cellular lifts over $R$. Its boundary lies in the proper union of $I$, and $h(b_0)=0$ since the simplex on $I$ has just one five-dimensional basis cell. Thus
$$
        r=K(\partial b_0),\qquad b=b_0-r
$$
satisfy $\partial r=\partial b_0$ and $\partial b=0$. Every generator of $r$ misses a positive name, so $Eir=0$ as a chain. This yields the displayed relation. Conversely any absolute $b$ on $I$ projects to zero as a five-chain since the top boundary on that simplex is injective over $R$. Therefore $dg+b$ has the required opposite degree, giving the converse by Response 26. For modular coefficients, every homology class of such $b$ lifts integrally by the universal coefficient theorem and $H_4X(I)=0$ from Response 46. Changing its representative by a boundary changes $w$ by the corresponding polarized chain, so an integral $b$ suffices. All substitutions act on the entire literal term, and the homotopy is linear at every finite multiplicity.

**One integral group for every modulus.** Put
$$
 G= E H_5(X(J);\mathbb Z)\big/Ei_* H_5(X(I);\mathbb Z)
$$
and write $\bar z$ for the fixed class. The attainable integral degrees are exactly the integers $d$ with $d\bar z=0$ in $G$. Modulo $m$, they are exactly the residues of integers $n$ with $n\bar z\in mG$. Indeed $H_4$ vanishes on both vertex sets, so both fifth homology groups reduce by tensoring. The image of $E$ is a direct summand and tensor product preserves the cokernel in the definition of $G$. Under degree-eight RC the class in this quotient even has an integral retraction by Response 7 and its polarization in Response 9. The spaces $X(V)$ for $V\ne\varnothing$ are four-connected: the triangle relations of Response 45 give simple connectivity, and Response 46 and Hurewicz give the successive vanishings. Thus the same integral group $G$ can equivalently be computed from the ordinary fifth homotopy groups, naturally in the vertex maps.

**Checks.** Run `python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_opposite_cycle.py`. In the three-dimensional opposite-face analogue the solver compares a typed cube to its relabelling by a three-cycle. Their equal-degree difference has ten nonzero boundary coordinates. The carried homotopy constructs a repair with fourteen proper coordinates and leaves an absolute cycle with sixteen coordinates. Polarization agrees coefficient by coefficient over the integers and modulo 2, 4 and 5. The same command checks the actual seven-name cubical incidence: 42 parameter cubes project to the 64 signed labellings of one cube, with ten separate facet cancellations. In degree five the existence of the repair on arbitrary input uses the all-arity argument of Response 46. # Researcher Response 48

## One prescribed five-arrow on the cone over a simplex boundary would settle the six-seam test

Use the five-cube of Response 9 with positive vertex names $I=\{1,\ldots,6\}$. Let $D$ consist of all brackets whose support is a proper subset of $I$. Let $S$ consist of all brackets on $\{0\}\cup I$ whose support **misses at least one positive name**. These are the subcomputads on the boundary of the five-simplex and on its simplicial cone with apex $0$. Write $g=[u,v]$ for the top bracket of the minimum-coloured five-cube, interchanging the orientation if necessary to give positive facet degree. Then $u,v$ are terms of $D$. **A five-term $G:u\to v$ in $S$ would give a single six-bracket of integral relative degree one.** This asks for one actual filling of a fixed geometric boundary and works for every compatible choice of the weak cube presentation.

Indeed a vertex in this cube has the minimum of 6 and the indices of the axes already turned on. A face fixing axis $j$ at zero misses $j$, and a face fixing it at one misses 6. All proper generators and their attaching terms are therefore in $D$. In $P(\{0\}\cup I)$ take $[G,g]$. Its boundary contains $g$ once and otherwise only five-generators missing a positive name, since they come from $G$. All are proper on seven names. The coefficient of the facet $I$ is one: only $g$ can contribute there. Applying $E$ to this same six-bracket gives an absolute filling of the 64-term cycle of Response 47 with $b=0$. This is a sufficient test. Response 47 permits nonzero absolute-cycle corrections and arbitrary signed multiples.

**What the contrary hypothesis forces on this particular pair.** Both $D$ and $S$ give groupoidal free models: every generating bracket has its opposite and both cancellation laws within its own supporting simplex. Base $u,v$ identically at vertex 6 inside $D$. Here one can use invertible pads without requiring the whole boundary to lie in one simplex. At cut zero prepend and append paths to put both outer vertices at 6. Once the $(j-1)$-poles are both $1^{j-1}_6$, the two $j$-poles are parallel loops there. Whisker at cut $j-1$ on the right with an inverse of the first $j$-pole. The new first pole is equivalent to $1^j_6$ by cancellation. The second is equivalent to the first by the transported common $(j+1)$-source, using the transported $u$ itself when $j=3$. Invertible $(j+1)$-pads at cut $j$ now put both poles literally at $1^j_6$. Perform this for $j=1,2,3$. Call the resulting based four-terms $L,R$. Applying these same whiskerings to $g$ gives a five-term in the unrestricted parameter whose only five-count is $g$ once.

If the ring satisfies identity-image RC through degree eight, the cocycle $z$ of Response 44 vanishes on every five-generator of $S$, since its support is proper and misses the facet coordinate $I$. It takes value $+1$ on $g$. The very same based homomorphism of that response, now using basepoint 6, therefore gives $[R]-[L]$ value **one in $\pi_4(S,6)$**. This particular based difference generates a split integral summand both before and after adjoining the cone vertex and the allowed brackets. The ordinary geometric image in $D$ is also primitive: its four-chain is the difference of the original count vectors, since identical pads are added on both sides. Projecting gives the fundamental cycle $\partial[I]$ with its chosen sign, so degree on the boundary sphere retracts this class. In the ordinary realization of $S$ its entire fourth homotopy group vanishes. To see this, cover by the six maximal pieces $X((\{0\}\cup I)-\{j\})$. All intersections are nonempty, are four-connected by Response 47, and are CW subcomplexes. Their union is four-connected: induct on the number of pieces, also applying the induction to the cover of the intersection of the next piece with the preceding union. Van Kampen and Mayer–Vietoris give simple connectivity and vanishing homology through four; Hurewicz concludes. This proves the conditional algebraic survival and the unconditional geometric vanishing for the same pair.

**Checks.** The solver enumerates the 242 proper faces of the five-cube, separately checking the 32 vertices, 80 edges, 80 squares, 40 cubes and ten four-faces for a missing positive name. In the two-cube analogue, the two coloured paths have vertex words 321 and 311. The solver constructs the actual star comparison by factoring through vertex 0, with all triangle labels missing a positive name. It constructs the opposite comparison as a typed derivation as well. Both resulting three-brackets have absolute degree one and their polarized boundaries are exactly the polarized seed in their respective orientations. Agda checked both paths at their literal types in safe mode. In rank five the check concerns the carriers; the star filling itself remains open. # Researcher Response 49

## An integral presentation detects actual five-fillings in the simplex star

Fix the star $S$ of Response 48 and base it at its apex 0. In its free raw algebra let $B$ have the cells of the free **point** on 0 through rank three, and in higher ranks take all cells whose two globular three-faces are in that point. Purity here tests the actual declarations occurring in a term and its type. Even the bracket $[0,0]$ is outside the free point.

For a cell in $B$, read its top-dimensional count $N^B$ as follows. An ambient variable gives one basis element. A labelled head with some argument outside $B$ also gives one basis element, indexed by the **whole literal term**. At any other head, add the counts of its arguments of the output dimension. Let $A$ be free abelian on just the resulting four-generators whose ambient dependencies use all six positive names. Write $p$ for projection onto those coordinates. Then
$$
 \pi_4(S,0)\cong
 A/\langle p(N^B(t)-N^B(s))\mid q:s\to t
       \text{ a five-generator of } B \text{ using all six names}\rangle .
$$
In particular, base the cubical pair $u,v$ of Response 48 identically at 0, giving $L,R$. Its prescribed filling in $S$ exists **if and only if** $p(N^B(R)-N^B(L))$ is a finite integral combination of these columns. A positive membership certificate would therefore give the unit-degree obstruction to degree-eight ring RC. Under degree-eight RC the displayed quotient instead has an integral retraction taking this designated vector to one. All rows and columns retained here are labelled coherence heads, since each ambient generating bracket of $S$ misses a positive name.

**Restriction to the point.** The specified cells form a subalgebra by substitution and the globular face equations. It is itself free on the indicated generators in every dimension. Introduce its generators in order of dimension, with boundaries decoded one dimension earlier. Decode each of its terms by stopping at an ambient variable or at the first head with an argument outside. Otherwise decode the substitution recursively. Decoding respects typing by substitution, since the subalgebra is closed under all heads with internal labels. Expanding a new generator to the whole original term and decoding are inverse by induction on syntax. In particular its only generator below dimension four is 0. Every cell of positive dimension is weakly invertible there. Through dimension three use the free point. For a higher cell, choose the inverse and cancellation witnesses in the groupoidal algebra of $S$; their three-faces stay pure (for a four-cell these include the original poles in reverse order). All subsequent higher witnesses stay inside for the same reason.

Every parallel pair of $n$-terms of $S$, $1\le n\le3$, admits an $(n+1)$-term there. Apply the generic cone actions of Response 46 with apex 0. Each required cone on an ambient generator is a bracket using only the original support and 0, so belongs to $S$. The cones on two parallel terms start at the same cell and end at the two terms whiskered by identical invertible source-face cones. Invert the first cone, compose with the second, and reflect comparability along the common whiskerings by weak invertibility (the whiskering lemma used in Response 48). For objects the brackets to or from 0 suffice. Thus the inclusion of $B$ is essentially surjective on cells in every dimension in the weak-equivalence sense: for a cell through rank three between point terms, take any pure point cell with those ends and apply the preceding comparison; above rank three the inclusion is full on parallel cells. In particular the based four-loops and *all* five-comparisons between them already lie in $B$.

**Why the integer equation suffices.** First retain every four-generator. Any four-generator can be padded to a based four-loop with exactly that one $B$-count. At cuts zero through three choose the left and right pads in the free point, where parallel terms always admit a head over the point. Starting at vertex 0, this sets successive poles literally to its iterated identities as in Response 48. The four-dimensional pads have zero count. The same choices base a pair of parallel four-terms and carry any five-arrow on the pair to a five-arrow on the based pair.

In the four-skeleton of $B$, the strict four-cells form the free commutative monoid on the four-generators. All lower positive strict cells are identities; interchange identifies the compositions and makes them commutative. Conversely the count evaluates into that commutative monoid, so imposes no further equality. Parallel four-terms with equal counts consequently admit an actual five-comparison by Response 38: the old three-skeleton is the point, its one-graph is acyclic, and each required old comparison is a head over the point. For infinitely many generators the two terms use a finite subcontext. Thus sending each basis element to its padded loop expresses every based loop as the sum of its generator classes in the abelian group $\pi_4$. Each five-generator boundary gives a relation there, by basing the five-generator itself. Conversely taking counts of a based loop modulo these relations is well-defined: the boundary of any five-term is the sum of the boundaries of its top occurrences, by the cellular chain identity in the free computad $B$. The two maps are inverse, since padded generators have singleton counts. This also identifies the group with $H_4(|B|;\mathbb Z)$, as there are no three-generators. Weak invertibility of the pads reflects a comparison of $L,R$ back to one on $u,v$.

A four-generator missing a positive name represents zero: its point pads and its entire loop lie in one cone facet, where the primitive bracket to the identity exists. Its basis element is therefore already a relation in the unprojected presentation. After killing these rows, every five-generator confined to a facet has zero projected boundary by dependency closure. This proves the formula. In particular degree-eight ring RC would make the weak equivalence $B\to S$ kill a split integral summand on ordinary fourth homology, since $|S|$ is four-connected. The formula applies to arbitrary finite raw terms, regardless of inactive slots or loop powers. It gives a recursively enumerable integral membership test by enumeration of finite declarations, heads and integer combinations. Deciding nonmembership from a finite truncation remains unjustified.

**Reproduction.** The solver implements the restriction and count recursion. With $U=1^3_0$, $X=1^2_0$, $a=[X,X]$, $l=[U,a]$, $r=[a,U]$, it distinguishes $l *_3 r$ and $(l *_3 1_a) *_3 r$ as different reduced basis elements with identical original four-counts. A unitor on the four-disc whiskered by $r$ gives a five-column with their exact signed difference. The solver constructs the opposite column too, and Agda checks both literal types. This example tests the change of basis and its relations; the degree-five cubical membership instance is still open. # Researcher Response 50

## A four-head with pure three-faces and facetwise four-inputs contributes zero

Take a rank-four composition head on a globular pasting arity of height four in the star \(S\) of Response 48. Suppose its two instantiated three-faces lie in the free point at 0, and **each individual four-slot** is labelled by a term missing at least one positive name. The missing name may depend on the slot. Then the instantiated head admits a five-comparison in each direction with a pure four-term of its own literal type. Consequently all such rows can be discarded from the integral presentation of Response 49. In particular merely combining all six names inside one indecomposable based four-head does not suffice for the obstruction.

**Compress the vertical strings.** In the planar tree of the arity \(P\), replace every nonempty string of consecutive four-cells over the same two-dimensional boundary, composable at cut three, by one four-cell with the first and last three-poles. More explicitly, at every node of depth three replace its nonempty list of children by one child. Call the resulting pasting arity \(Q\). Both signed three-boundaries are canonically unchanged, including all their lower variables. The two generic ends of the original composition head, with their types, use precisely the respective signed boundary contexts. Rename them along these identifications to define the new composition head on \(Q\). Label its merged four-slots over \(P\) by the cut-three strings, keeping their exterior variables. Its type recovers the original pair **literally**, since the substitution is the identity on both boundaries. This expanded term and the original generic head each use all of \(P\): the exterior labels account for both boundaries, and every four-slot and internal three-pole occurs in a string. Thus a full-support five-head over \(P\) compares the two terms in either order. Substitute the actual labels to obtain the two comparisons in \(S\).

Every variable of \(Q\) of rank at most three lies on one of those two boundaries. Its original label is pure, since it occurs in the typed support of a literal pure end and raw substitution preserves occurrences. In particular the exterior three-poles \(s_0,s_k\) of each merged four-slot are pure. For its label write the constituent arrows as \(b_i:s_{i-1}\to s_i\), \(1\le i\le k\). All three-states in this string have the same literal type. Choose \(c_i:s_i\to s_k\) by primitive bracket for internal states, take \(c_k=1_{s_k}\), and take \(c_0\) to be any pure four-term on the specified ends. Each pair \(b_i *_3 c_i,\ c_{i-1}\) lies in a single allowed facet: the endpoint states are dependencies of \(b_i\), and \(s_k\) and the pure choices add at most 0. Both directions of their five-bracket are therefore present. Whiskering these comparisons successively, starting at the last segment, contracts the right-associated string to \(c_0\). The last comparison may use \(b_k\) directly in place of \(b_k *_3 1_{s_k}\). Other bracketings compare to this one by a full-support head over the string. Reversing each comparison and the order gives the opposite five-arrow. Use maximal-slot naturality to make these replacements in the head on \(Q\). Its resulting labels are all pure, giving the asserted pure four-term. The proof applies to every tree shape, arbitrarily many slots, arbitrary well-typed inactive labels with the stated boundary and support conditions, and arbitrary powers occurring inside a proper slot. String length and raw term size are unrestricted. No common omitted name across a string is required.

**Computation.** Over seven actual vertex names the solver builds six strings, each with an impure middle three-pole. It checks two different full-support based generators: a row-first composite and the single composition head on the entire chart. For each it computes opposite five-columns with exactly seven coordinates, the full generator against six generators supported on \(\{0,i\}\). Projection retains just the unit coefficient of the full generator. A separate seven-segment string passes successively through the six impure poles, with each transition using at most two positive names. The solver contracts that whole string to the literal identity and constructs the reverse expansion. Agda 2.8.0 checked the six resulting five-arrows at their claimed types. The generic compression and the facetwise argument give the family conclusion. The specific cubical difference of Response 49 may involve four-slots which themselves use all six positive names. # Researcher Response 51

## Five split inclusions of contractible free algebras test the six-seam ring

There are five explicit pushout squares, up to renaming vertices, in which one leg is a split cellular inclusion between groupoidal free CaTT algebras that both have globular boundary lifting to the terminal algebra. **Surjectivity on algebraic \(\pi_4\) of its pushout in all five squares disproves degree-eight identity-image RC for the six-seam ring.** If the ring satisfies that RC, at one of the five stages *every* labelled square has a split copy of \(\mathbb Z\) in the cokernel on \(\pi_4\). This asks only for surjectivity in one degree, at specified objects. In particular any left semi-model structure with the computadic cofibrations and FHM weak equivalences would decide the ring negatively: the original inclusion would be a trivial cofibration between cofibrant objects and so would its cobase change here. The semi-model structure for weak \(\omega\)-categories is a proposed application of FHM's weak equivalences. [Fujii, Hoshino and Maehara](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.aim.2025.110490)

**The squares.** In \(J=\{0,\ldots,6\}\) write \(A_j\) for the full bracket algebra on \(J-\{j\}\). For a nonempty set \(U\) of positive names write \(T_U\) for the free algebra on the union of the facet computads indexed by \(U\). Its generators miss at least one member of \(U\); a composite can use all of \(U\). Fix \(j\in U\), \(2\le |U|\le6\). Put \(O=T_{U-\{j\}}\), \(F=A_j\), and let \(D\) be their common subcomputad, viewed also as a free algebra. Then \(T_U=O\amalg_D F\). Indeed a compatible pair of maps assigns images to exactly the generators of the union, respecting their literal attaching types. All these unions and intersections have cone vertex 0. The term cones of Responses 46 and 49 make their algebras three-connected.

Inside \(F\) enlarge \(D\) to \(D'\) by keeping its four-skeleton and, in each successive rank starting at five, taking every bracket of \(F\) on parallel terms already present. This retains all of \(D\). The map \(D'\to1\) has globular boundary lifting: through rank four use the just-proved connectivity of \(D\), and thereafter use the prescribed bracket. The same lifting holds for \(F\to1\). In particular \(D'\hookrightarrow F\) is an FHM weak equivalence by two-out-of-three, and a computadic cofibration by dimension-ordered attachment. It admits a strict retraction: fix \(D'\), and assign the other generators successively to fillers in \(D'\). These statements apply to terms of every finite size. Put \(O'=O\amalg_D D'\). Its computad inside the union intersects \(F\) exactly in \(D'\), so the pushout of \(D'\hookrightarrow F\) along \(D'\hookrightarrow O'\) is \(O'\hookrightarrow T_U\), again split. Every generator of \(O'\) belongs to one of the two groupoidal subalgebras, and composites of weakly invertible inputs are weakly invertible. The four-skeleta of \(O\) and \(O'\) are identical. Consequently their images in \(\pi_4(T_U,0)\) are identical: every based four-loop of \(O'\) is already a term of \(O\). Extra five-arrows may identify representatives, but cannot supply a new four-loop. Taking \(U=\{1,\ldots,r\}\), \(j=r\), \(2\le r\le6\), specifies the five tests without choices of retractions.

**The integral obstruction.** Assume the ring hypothesis and let \(\Phi:\pi_4(T_{\{1,\ldots,6\}},0)\to\mathbb Z\) be the detector of Response 48 based at 0. Write \(\alpha=[R]-[L]\) for the identically based cubical pair, oriented to degree one. For any permutation \(\sigma\) of the six positive vertices, \(\Phi(\sigma_*\alpha)=\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\). Indeed the relabelled based comparison in the unrestricted algebra has the relabelled cube bracket once as its only five-count. Its support misses 0, so the prescribed proper values of the cocycle give its relative degree on the positive simplex. Relabelling acts on that degree by the sign. This holds also for a projection constructed using a chosen order of vertices: the relabelled projection and the permuted one are carried by the same simplex faces and are homotopic as maps of pairs by extension within these convex faces.

Now put \(f=\sum_\sigma\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\Phi\sigma_*\). Then \(f(\alpha)=720\), and \(f\) transforms by the sign character. Its image on the loops coming from \(T_U\) is therefore a subgroup of \(\mathbb Z\) depending only on \(|U|\). These subgroups are nested. The first is zero, since a single facet is contractible; the last is nonzero. At the first nonzero stage \(r\), for *every* \(U,j\) of that size the restriction to \(T_U\) has image \(d\mathbb Z\) with \(d>0\), and vanishes on \(O'\) by the four-skeleton argument. Thus the cokernel of the pushout inclusion surjects onto \(d\mathbb Z\) and the surjection splits integrally. There are respectively \(30,60,60,30,6\) labelled squares at the five possible stages. The finite calculation does not determine which stage occurs under the hypothesis. The argument uses the full group of based terms and its additive homomorphism, so it applies to arbitrary inactive inputs and every loop multiplicity.

**Reproduction.** The solver verifies all 127 nonempty vertex supports in every union/intersection square. It enumerates the 120 staircase simplices of the five-cube, checks every positive-name permutation on its degree and obtains the alternating sum 720. At stages three through six it constructs a new five-bracket and its reverse in \(D'\) on terms traversing all the older distinguished colours. It checks that a full-colour four-bracket and a five-bracket depending on that excluded four-bracket both stay outside \(D'\), while the six-loop on the admitted five-bracket lies inside. At stage two the intersection is already a single full simplex. Agda checked all eight claimed forward and reverse five-types. These tests exercise the completion at the four-skeleton cutoff; its lifting property in unbounded rank follows from its recursive definition. [Star pushout and alternating-degree solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_star_pushouts.py)

dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_star_pushouts.py --agda "$dir/StarPushouts.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/StarPushouts.agda"

# Researcher Response 52

## Any failure of star gluing is born at one vertex, edge, or two-generator

The five pushout tests of Response 51 can be filtered by the rank of the newly admitted brackets. **Introducing generators first in rank three or four, together with their higher brackets, always induces a surjection on based \(\pi_4\) in these pushouts.** Under the six-seam degree-eight RC hypothesis there must instead be a step admitting just one new generator of rank at most two plus the higher brackets it enables. Its pushout has a split infinite cyclic summand in the cokernel on \(\pi_4\). For the fourth, fifth and sixth facet stages the new generator must be a two-generator between already available paths.

**The filtration.** With \(D,F,O'\) as in Response 51, let \(D_k\subset F\) agree with \(D\) through rank \(k\), then take all brackets on available parallel ends in each higher dimension. Thus \(D_4=D'\), \(D_{-1}=F\) (at rank zero take all vertices), and
\[
 D_4\subset D_3\subset D_2\subset D_1\subset D_0\subset F .
\]
To pass from \(D_k\) to \(D_{k-1}\), choose an enumeration of the missing rank-\(k\) generators with ends in the common lower skeleton. Include them one at a time, after each admission closing under brackets in all ranks strictly above \(k\). Use the same construction for missing vertices. Each intermediate algebra has globular boundary lifting to the point: through rank \(k\) its old skeleton supplies the fillers on the unchanged lower terms, above rank \(k\) it is complete by construction. Countable unions give the claimed endpoints because terms and attaching types have finite syntax. Push each inclusion out under \(O'\); all the resulting models embed as free subcomputads of \(T_U\), and remain groupoidal by the generator criterion in Response 51.

**Stationary boundaries.** Write \(A\subset C\) for one of the slice inclusions of contractible algebras, \(Y\) for the algebra already assembled, and \(Z=Y\amalg_A C\). Choose a strict retraction \(C\to A\) by globular lifting, and hence \(\rho:Z\to Y\). Suppose that the new variables start in rank \(k\ge3\). For a raw based four-loop \(t\) of \(Z\), take its finite context of dependencies and mark precisely the variables from \(C-A\). This is up-closed: an old variable has only old dependencies. In this context all marks have dimension three or four, so the depth in \(t\) is at most one. Apply depth-one CaTT naturality. Interpret the minus and plus labels by inclusion and \(\rho\), respectively, fixing the unmarked variables. For each marked variable the required mixed label has parallel ends in \(C\): its old dependencies lie in \(A\), and all preceding mixed labels for these dependencies have been chosen in \(C\). Use its globular boundary lifting to choose the mixed term. The literal poles of the based loop lie in the point and contain no marks, so the specialized naturality term has *exactly* type \(t\to\rho(t)\), without endpoint whiskers. Interchanging the copies and choosing the mixed labels in the opposite assignment gives the reverse. This proves surjectivity for all such four-loops, irrespective of the number of marks, the size of unmarked inputs or their loop powers. [Depth-one naturality, Theorem 3.1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11620)

Choose a square detected by the integer homomorphism of Response 51, zero on the old side and nonzero on the final one. Restrict it at every step of the filtration. A limit stage cannot be the first to have nonzero image: a representative four-loop has finite support. The first nonzero image therefore occurs at a single successor, and the stationary-boundary proof excludes ranks four and three. The image in \(\mathbb Z\) at that successor gives the claimed split summand.

**Which low cells can occur.** At stage \(r\) let \(M\) be the \(r-1\) earlier distinguished names. An extra rank-\(k\) variable must use every name in \(M\). For \(r=2\) the overlap is a full simplex missing one vertex, so only the vertex admission changes it. For \(r=3\) the vertices already agree. The only missing edges are the two directions between the two distinguished names. For \(r\ge4\) all vertices and edges agree. A missing two-bracket has parallel path ends whose joint vertex support contains \(M\). At the two-generator stage each constituent edge still belongs to the overlap. The minimum total strict path length of the two ends at stages \(3,4,5,6\) is respectively \(4,3,4,5\). Traverse one path forward and the other backward to get a closed walk in the symmetric old edge graph. A positive-length walk of length \(L\) visits at most \(L\) distinct vertices. With two marked names the direct edge between them is absent, so a closed visit needs at least four steps. In that case go through 0 in both directions. With at least three names, a cycle through them attains the bound. Use the closed path and the empty path as the two ends. Longer paths of arbitrary multiplicity are covered by the filtration.

**Checks.** The solver enumerates old directed edges and closed walks through the first attainable length at each stage, including repeated vertices. In a separate seven-name typed test a new three-generator on a path through \(1,2,3\) is traversed by two four-cells. Its replacement by the old identity is composed at cut zero with an external four-loop on \(4,5,6\). The two five-comparisons use only allowed generators. Agda checks their literal types with external powers \(0,1,3,7\). These examples check the endpoint framing; the all-powers claim follows from the depth argument. [Rank filtration and stationary replacement checks](../../../tools/relative_coherence_star_attachment_ranks.py)

dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_star_attachment_ranks.py --agda "$dir/StarRanks.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/StarRanks.agda"

# Researcher Response 53

## Three fixed relative cylinder inclusions suffice, independently of the attaching paths

For each \(k\ge0\) take two \(k\)-globes with identified boundary. Fold them to the first globe \(D^k\), and factor this fold by freely filling all globular boundary-lifting problems in ranks \(>k\). Write the result as
\[
 D^k\amalg_{\partial D^k}D^k\ \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow W_k
        \xrightarrow{p_k} D^k ,
 \qquad j_k:D^k\hookrightarrow W_k
\]
where \(j_k\) selects the first copy. Use one generator for each pair of parallel \((n-1)\)-terms and **each specified \(n\)-term of the globe** on their projected ends, in increasing rank \(n>k\). The projection is a globular trivial fibration: below \(k\) it is the identity, in rank \(k\) the first copy supplies the lift, and above \(k\) use the specified generator. Thus each \(j_k\) is a split computadic cofibration and an FHM weak equivalence. Its source and target are fixed, even when an attaching map sends the top cell of the first globe to a large composite.

**If cobase changes of \(j_0,j_1,j_2\) at three-connected groupoidal free algebras are surjective on algebraic \(\pi_4\) at the old basepoint, the six-seam ring fails identity-image RC through degree eight.** Conversely, if the ring has that RC, one pushout of one of these three fixed maps at such an algebra has a split infinite cyclic summand in the cokernel on \(\pi_4\). This removes the dependence of the *attached inclusion* in Response 52 on the changing path endpoints, overlapping facets and higher brackets of its source.

**What these attachments preserve unconditionally.** For any groupoidal free algebra \(Y\) and any attaching cell \(D^k\to Y\), put \(Y^+=Y\amalg_{D^k}W_k\). Then \(Y\to Y^+\) induces an isomorphism at each old basepoint on
\[
                         \pi_d,\qquad 1\le d\le\max(3,k+1).
\]
It also gives a bijection on components. The retraction is induced by \(j_kp_k\) on the template. In \(W_k\) any parallel pair of \(m\)-terms with \(m\ge k\) admits a comparison: in the globe their images have the same strict value, since the strict globe is thin in rank \(k\) and has only identities higher up. Pasting-context RC supplies a globe term on their images, then lift it with \(p_k\). This yields coinductive inverses for every new generator above rank \(k\). The duplicate \(k\)-cell, if \(k>0\), is joined by an invertible \((k+1)\)-cell to the chosen old cell, hence becomes weakly invertible in the pushout. For \(k=0\) the new vertex is connected to the old one. Thus the pushout remains groupoidal.

For a loop mark all the new generators in its finite context, including dependencies. All have rank at least \(k\). Interpret its naturality cylinder with minus copy the identity and plus copy the retraction followed by the inclusion. On the template itself, recursively choose the mixed label for each marked variable in \(W_k\): its prescribed parallel ends have dimension at least \(k\), so the preceding lifting argument applies. These choices specialize along the attaching map, with the old globe unmarked. More explicitly, the presentation of \(Y^+\) freely adjoins the complementary template variables with the old globe substituted in their types. Naturality respects substitution of unmarked terms; the old labels may therefore be arbitrary cells of \(Y\). In dimension at most three use arbitrary-depth naturality. In dimension \(d\le k+1\) use depth at most one; when there are no marks the term is already old. The loop has literal point poles, so its cylinder is a comparison with its retracted loop. This proves surjectivity, and the strict retraction proves injectivity. No comparison of different choices of naturality data is used. [Arbitrary-depth naturality through dimension three, §7](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md#7-arbitrary-arities-and-conjecture-n-through-dimension-3) · [Depth-one theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.11620)

**Lifting the four-skeleton using only the fixed templates.** Take any square \(D'\subset F,\ O'\subset T\) of Response 51. Starting at \(P=D'\), construct a free algebra over \(F\) by processing the generators of \(F-D'\) through rank four in order of dimension, using a countable enumeration within each rank. Maintain a literal lift of the part processed so far, equal to the inclusion on the old four-skeleton. For a new \(k\)-generator \(g\), lift its boundary using those earlier choices. Choose an old cell \(z\) on this lifted boundary in the current \(P\); for \(k=0\) choose the old apex. Such a cell exists because \(P\) remains three-connected and groupoidal. Indeed the preceding isomorphisms preserve three-connectivity at each step and at unions every term occurs at a prior stage. In a connected groupoidal algebra with all based groups through degree three zero, parallel \(m\)-terms for \(m\le3\) can be based identically as in Response 48, compared there, and the comparison reflected along the invertible whiskerings.

Attach \(W_k\) at \(z\), and use its duplicate as the lift of \(g\). Map this extension to \(F\) by sending the first cell to the image of \(z\), the second to \(g\), and the higher variables to fillers in the globularly contractible \(F\). The two cells have the same literal boundary in \(F\). This produces a section on the entire four-skeleton of \(F\), compatible there with \(D'\). After pushout under \(O'\) the resulting algebra maps to \(T\) surjectively on based four-loops: substitute the chosen lifts on \(F\) and the original terms on \(O'\), which agree on the common four-skeleton. Throughout this second tower the base is likewise three-connected and groupoidal. Its initial object \(O'\) has these properties since \(O\) does and the additional generators start in rank five.

In a square detected by Response 51, pull the detecting homomorphism back along the entire tower under \(O'\). Its image starts at zero and ends nonzero by the literal lifting. A first nonzero image occurs at a successor because every four-loop has finite support. That successor attaches \(W_k\) for \(k\le2\): the unconditional range above gives surjectivity in degree four at \(k=3,4\). The image at that step gives the split integral summand in the cokernel. The same argument proves the sufficient implication by composition and finite support. This is a test for the original ring; surjectivity outside the proved range for \(j_0,j_1,j_2\) remains open.

**Reproduction.** The solver implements the prescribed-image generators, constructs their projection to the first globe, and checks opposite cells before and after projection for \(k=0,\ldots,4\). Two different projection labels on one literal boundary give distinct generators. The solver constructs a comparison between those generators and its reverse. For \(k=2\) it also specializes the template at a composite two-arrow on a three-vertex path and checks both specialized mixed four-types. Agda checks all 62 stated types. The solver checks these finite fragments; the arbitrary-rank result uses the specified-image lifting construction and the two naturality ranges.
