# Researcher Response 54

## A failure would reach one of the first three generators for weak \(\omega\)-equifibrations

Use the published source inclusions \(i_k:D^k\to\mathcal E^{k+1}\) of the walking bi-invertible cell, with independent left and right inverse data. These maps and their higher suspensions generate the lifting characterization of FHM \(\omega\)-equifibrations. If the six-seam ring has degree-eight identity-image RC, at least one of the following occurs in a pushout at a three-connected groupoidal free algebra, based at an old vertex.

| Map attached | Possible degree of failure of surjectivity |
|---|---|
| \(i_0\), walking invertible arrow | 2, 3 or 4 |
| \(i_1\), walking invertible two-cell | 3 or 4 |
| \(i_2\), walking invertible three-cell | 4 |

If all these attachments preserve three-connectivity, the failure is in degree four and its cokernel contains a split \(\mathbb Z\). Proving surjectivity in the six entries decides the ring negatively. The tests on the bracket cylinders in Response 53 require only degree four; the present tests use the actual equifibration generators. [Fujii, Hoshino and Maehara](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.09849) · [Benjamin, Champin and Markakis, Definition 4.8 and Remark 4.9](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30254)

**The unconditional range for the walking cell.** In a pushout under \(i_k\) at any groupoidal free algebra the inclusion is bijective on components and induces isomorphisms on based \(\pi_d\) for \(1\le d\le k+1\). Choose a retraction sending the duplicate \(g\) to the old \(k\)-cell \(z\), and the forward and inverse \((k+1)\)-arrows to the weak identity on \(z\). Extend to the full inverse data on the identity in the old globe. Its higher cancellation types are parallel on a globe above its top dimension, so globe coherence gives these higher values. In the pushout the duplicate is equivalent to the old cell; all higher inverse data are invertible, including the two one-sided inverses of any invertible cell. Thus the result is groupoidal.

For surjectivity move from the retracted labelling to the original labelling and mark the new variables. Through degree \(k+1\) only \(g\) and the three arrows \(a:z\to g,\ b,c:g\to z\) need mixed labels. Write juxtaposition for \(*_k\). Write \(P:ab\to1_z\), \(Q:ca\to1_g\) for the two supplied invertible cancellations. Take \(a\) as the component on \(g\). For the three arrows the required mixed types are respectively
\[
                1_z a\to a,\qquad 1_z\to ab,\qquad 1_z\to ac.
\]
The unitor and an inverse of \(P\) give the first two. For the third, compare \(b\) to \(c\) by the literal route
\[
 b\ \longrightarrow\ 1_g b\ \longrightarrow\ (ca)b\
 \longrightarrow\ c(ab)\ \longrightarrow\ c1_z\ \longrightarrow\ c .
\]
The steps are unit insertion, whiskered reverse of \(Q\), associator, whiskered \(P\) and unit contraction. Whisker by \(a\) after the reverse of \(P\) to get \(1_z\to ac\). The route with all directions reversed also exists, using the other orientations of these same structural cells and cancellations. Thus every mixed label is realized inside the walking cell itself. Depth-one naturality on any based loop through degree \(k+1\) now gives a comparison with its retraction, since its point poles are unmarked. The argument is by substitution of arbitrary labels from the pushout, including all inactive inputs and unrestricted powers. The retraction gives injectivity. This range uses no assertion that the entire walking weak equivalence is contractible.

**Passage to the six tests.** Repeat the two towers in Response 53 using \(i_k\) at each generator of rank \(k\le4\). At a three-connected stage the required old cell on the lifted boundary exists by basing. The map to the facet \(F\) extends: choose there an equivalence from its old image to the prescribed new cell (the primitive bracket suffices), then choose its independent inverse and cancellation data in the same complete facet. This is exactly the defining mapping property of the walking cell. Every stage is free on a computad, by its ordinary computadic presentation, and groupoidal by the preceding argument. If either tower first loses three-connectivity, it does so at a successor because a loop has finite support. The unconditional range permits only the lower-degree entries in the table. If both towers retain three-connectivity, the first tower gives the same section on four-skeleta as before. A nonzero detector therefore appears in the second tower, with the same split integral cokernel argument. The unconditional range rules out \(k=3,4\) at that step.

**Typed checks.** In the ordinary presentation the solver uses one reverse of each cancellation from its own invertibility data; it keeps \(b,c\) distinct. It constructs both directions of \(b\simeq c\), both directions of the missing cancellation, and both directions of the two other required mixed components. It checks their literal types before suspension and after one, two, three and four suspensions. Agda accepted all 40 judgments. The generic derivation suspends at every \(k\), and the proof for arbitrary loops uses depth-one naturality. [Independent inverse and mixed-label solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_walking_equivalence.py)

# Researcher Response 55

## The walking arrow preserves the second group, and its suspension preserves the third

Two entries in Response 54 can be discharged. For any groupoidal free base, a pushout of \(i_0\) induces an isomorphism on based \(\pi_2\), and a pushout of \(i_1\) induces an isomorphism on based \(\pi_3\). **The remaining walking-equivalence tests are \(i_0\) in degrees three and four, and \(i_1,i_2\) in degree four.**

**Parallel two-terms in the walking arrow.** Work first in its homotopy bicategory: parallel two-cells are identified when connected by an invertible three-cell. All positive-dimensional cells of the walking arrow are weakly invertible. Write \(a:z\to g\), \(b,c:g\to z\), \(P:ab\Rightarrow1_z\), \(Q:ca\Rightarrow1_g\) as in Response 54. Bicategorical calculations below omit the canonical associators and unitors. They can equivalently be made in a strictification, whose functor on each hom category is fully faithful. Use \(\cdot\) for vertical composition in execution order. Put
\[
 \chi=(Qb)^{-1}\cdot(cP):b\Rightarrow c,\qquad
 H=(\chi a)\cdot Q:ba\Rightarrow1_g .
\]
The first triangle \(Hb=bP\) follows by postcomposing both sides with the invertible \(\chi\). Indeed \(Qb\cdot\chi=cP\), and \((\chi ab)\cdot(cP)=(bP)\cdot\chi\) by interchange. For the second triangle, \((abP)\cdot P=(Pab)\cdot P\) by interchange, hence \(abP=Pab\). Left whiskering the first triangle by \(a\) now gives \(aHb=Pab\). Right whiskering by the equivalence \(b\) is faithful, so \(aH=Pa\).

Reduce path words by \(c\to b,\ ab\to1_z,\ ba\to1_g\), labelled respectively by \(\chi^{-1},P,H\). The ordered pair (number of \(c\)'s, length) decreases. Disjoint steps commute by interchange. The only overlapping left sides are \(aba,bab\), and their reduction cells agree by the two triangles. Induction on the decreasing pair proves that all reduction paths to normal form give the same two-cell. An irreducible path is empty or consists of a single \(a\) or \(b\); its endpoints determine it. Each reduction and its inverse thus agrees with the canonical comparison obtained from the two normalizers. This includes \(Q\), since \(Q=(\chi a)^{-1}\cdot H\). The independent inverses of \(P,Q\) equal their vertical inverses in the homotopy bicategory by their supplied one-sided three-dimensional cancellations.

This accounts for arbitrary raw two-terms: the rank-two normalization of §3 item 1 expresses them up to three-comparison as strings of whiskered two-generators with structural bracketing and unit cells. Structural comparisons on the same path are unique in the quotient by RC(2) in the one-skeleton. Thus **all parallel two-terms of the walking arrow admit three-comparisons**. The proof applies to insertions as well as reductions and imposes no length bound.

**Use on arbitrary attachments.** For \(i_0\) keep the mixed choices on generators through rank one from Response 54. Every mixed type for a new two-generator now has a comparison in the walking arrow by the preceding theorem, including generators representing either one-sided inverse of \(P,Q\). Apply the degree-two naturality construction to any based two-loop with exactly the new variables marked. The mixed labels specialize in the pushout, and the loop's literal point poles are stationary. Together with the strict retraction this proves the first isomorphism.

For \(i_1\) the ordinary walking computad is the suspension of the previous one. Use the suspended retraction and mixed labels through rank three. Here one can choose the known naturality operations through degree three to respect suspension when the two exterior objects are unmarked. To see this, in the generic-head induction choose the suspension of the lower operation whenever the arity, typed generic head and marks are suspensions. The partial cylinder of a suspended globular arity with unmarked outer poles is the suspension of its partial cylinder, by the variable boundary formula and omission of the cut-zero factors. On suspended terms the recursive substitution then suspends every mixed argument, by term induction. The prescribed face types therefore match literally up to the canonical variable ordering. The strict value is the suspended cylinder pasting (every active cut index increases by one). On the other generic heads the existing proof by prescribed-boundary lifting on the partial cylinder applies with these choices: the lower strict values are the prescribed cylinder values, and the required RC through degree three holds on all globular-arity cylinders. This gives one choice for the operation on *all* terms of the attaching context. In particular the suspended three-generators receive simply the suspensions of the mixed labels just chosen for the two-generators. Naturality of an arbitrary based three-loop in the pushout now gives the second isomorphism. Arbitrary inactive labels outside the walking computad need only be substituted into these generic operations. [Definition of the partial cylinder and the all-arity proof through degree three](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md#7-arbitrary-arities-and-conjecture-n-through-dimension-3) · [Suspended walking cells](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30254)

**Checks.** The word solver visits all 1,787 composable paths of length at most 16, counting the two empty paths separately. All reduce to the endpoint-prescribed word. It finds 5,129 occurrences of the overlapping pair on \(aba\) and 2,628 on \(bab\). The term checker constructs both reduction paths at each overlap and both reverse expansions at their literal types. It also constructs both directions of the two three-dimensional interchanges used in the triangle proof. Agda checked these twelve judgments. The equalities of the two full peak composites use the bicategorical cancellation argument above. [Word normalization and typed overlap checks](../../../tools/relative_coherence_walking_peaks.py)

# Researcher Response 56

## All three equifibration tests reduce to degree four

For **every** source inclusion of a walking bi-invertible cell, its pushout at a groupoidal free CaTT algebra preserves components and algebraic homotopy groups through degree three. In particular the walking invertible arrow itself has zero third group. Combining this with Response 54 leaves exactly the following sufficient condition: **all pushouts of the first three equifibration generators at three-connected groupoidal free algebras are surjective on based \(\pi_4\)**. Under degree-eight RC for the six-seam ring, one such pushout has a split infinite cyclic summand in its fourth-group cokernel. The lower-dimensional alternatives have all been discharged.

**Division at one top input.** Let \(C[X]\) be a raw \(d\)-term with one occurrence, counted in the top generator multiset, of a fresh \(d\)-variable of prescribed type. Fix every other label in a groupoidal algebra. On \(d\)-cells of that prescribed type modulo \((d+1)\)-comparison, evaluation of \(C\) is a bijection onto the classes of its fixed output type. In particular a specified output class supplies an input even if no input was chosen beforehand.

Here is the reduction to elementary whiskerings. On a globular pasting arity choose a binary expression for its strict composite by splitting the tree at its branches: at the first branching past the common unary trunk of length \(j\), concatenate the smaller diagrams at cut \(j\), with the shared \(j\)-disc as interface. On discs use the top variable. This uses each top variable exactly once. For a generic \(d\)-head whose arity has a \(d\)-slot, this expression has the same strict value as the head. Align its lower poles with the literal poles of the head, in increasing dimension. Once the lower poles match, the two source poles of rank \(h\) are parallel and have equal strict value; the same holds for the target poles. RC on the pasting arity supplies identity-image \((h+1)\)-pads in the required directions. Precompose and postcompose at cut \(h\), up to \(h=d-1\). These pads are independent of all top variables: even at the last cut their strict support lies in the lower face, hence so does their raw support. The two resulting parallel \(d\)-terms compare on the pasting arity in both directions. This construction specializes with the same pads at every value of the distinguished top input. In a nested head follow the unique branch containing that input. All its sibling inputs are fixed.

For a binary step \(X *_j B\) with fixed \(p\)-cell \(B\), \(j+1<p\le d\), use the strict factorization
\[
        (X *_j s_{p-1}B)*_{p-1}(t_{p-1}X *_j B).
\]
On the generic two-disc pasting arity, align this factorization to the original literal type by the same independent pads and compare in both directions. For \(B *_j X\) the factors are \(B *_j s_{p-1}X\) and \(t_{p-1}B *_j X\). The face of \(X\) is fixed. The fixed \(p\)-cell at cut \(p-1\) is now elementary; the other factor lowers the fixed dimension by one. Iterating leaves only elementary whiskerings and fixed pads. At the top cut composition with an invertible cell is a bijection on classes by its inverse and cancellation. At lower cuts elementary whiskering by an invertible cell is essentially surjective at every dimension and reflects comparison; apply this inside the appropriate iterated hom, on either side by duality. It preserves comparison by whiskering. Thus it is a bijection on the indicated classes with prescribed faces. This proves the division assertion for arbitrary finite syntax. [Fujii–Hoshino–Maehara, Lemma 3.2.7 and Theorem 3.2.14](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.13240)

**Choosing the mixed cells on independent inverses.** It suffices to discharge \(i_0\) in degree three after Response 55. Work inside the groupoidal walking arrow. Move from the retracted labelling to the identity, fixing its source vertex. The mixed label of the duplicate vertex is \(a\), and that of the forward arrow \(a\) is its left unitor as in Response 54. In the ordinary computadic presentation each marked forward \(n\)-cell \(f\) has two distinct ordinary \(n\)-inverse generators, one for each side. Each inverse has its *own* marked forward \((n+1)\)-cancellation generator. This description repeats on the cancellations. [Presentation of the inverse data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30254)

Assume the mixed label of \(f\) and the labels in smaller ranks have been chosen. For one inverse \(b\), consider its cancellation \(\theta\), with source \(f *_ {n-1} b\) or \(b *_ {n-1} f\) and target a weak identity. Temporarily give \(b^\uparrow\) a fresh variable \(X\) of its prescribed \((n+1)\)-type. Write down the two sides of the mixed type for \(\theta\), using the known naturality on the \(n\)-dimensional attaching terms. Only the side containing the lifted composite of \(f,b\) uses \(X\). **It uses it exactly once**: the prescribed strict partial-cylinder pasting on the binary top composite has one mixed copy of each selected top slot, by linearity of the interval tensor on top chains. The omission of inactive lower faces does not affect that top count. The remaining copies of \(\theta\), endpoint pads, and mixed label of the identity contain no \(X\). The two sides have the same fixed type by the cylinder boundary formula; that type is independent of \(X\) by dimension.

Apply division at the single top input to the side containing \(X\), with the other side as specified output. Choose the resulting actual cell as \(b^\uparrow\). The two sides now compare, so choose a representative in the required direction as \(\theta^\uparrow\). Do this independently for the other inverse. No mixed label chosen for one cancellation occurs as the unknown for the other. Starting at \(a\), repeat at the forward cancellation generators of ranks two and three. At the last step division concerns four-cells modulo five-comparison, but writing the cancellation's mixed type uses naturality only on its **rank-three attaching terms**. The all-arity construction through degree three is enough. This gives compatible mixed labels for every variable through rank three, before any choice of external inactive labels. Specialize these data in an arbitrary pushout at a groupoidal free algebra. Naturality takes each based three-loop to a comparison with its retraction, with literal stationary poles. The strict retraction proves injectivity. Arbitrary loop powers are instances of the same term construction. A filtered composite of these inclusions likewise preserves the groups through degree three, since every loop and comparison has finite syntax.

**Checks.** The solver exhausts the two non-elementary binary positions in rank two, the six in rank three and the twelve in rank four, counting both sides of the slot. It constructs the factorizations above, aligns their literal boundaries, verifies that every pad omits the marked variable, and checks that its top count is one before and after factoring. The three ranks use respectively zero, four and sixteen pads. Agda checked the forty forward and reverse four-, three- or five-cell judgments at their exact types. These are generic binary tests. Surjectivity at unbounded arity uses pasting-context framing and the whiskering theorem, and the degree-four ring test remains open. # Researcher Response 57

## Mixed components extend over the entire walking inverse tower

For each \(i_k:D^k\to\mathcal E^{k+1}\), let two endpoint labellings of the walking cell take values in the same groupoidal CaTT algebra. Prescribe compatible changes on the source globe, with any up-closed marking there, and mark the duplicate and all the inverse data. **The prescribed components extend to every walking generator, simultaneously in all dimensions.** The assertion concerns the literal mixed types in the ordinary computadic presentation. In particular, for the retraction and identity of Response 56, no fourth-group obstruction can come from a missing component on a new generator.

The additional input is the ordinary partial-cylinder action for a top composite and for an identity. It exists in every rank, on every up-closed selection, with the prescribed faces. For the binary temporal composite its top multiset contains the change of each selected top input exactly once. These operations can be applied to any two labelled inputs with compatible changes on their poles, even when their lower poles are themselves composite terms. This follows by substitution in the generic temporal or identity action. [Gray Response 18](canonical-gray-terms-r7.md#researcher-response-18)

Write \(a:z\to g\) for the first forward generator. Give the duplicate \(g\) a temporary change \(X\). Its type is determined by the already prescribed changes on the common lower faces of \(z,g\). The minus side of the mixed equation for \(a\) ends by composing its lower-whiskered minus copy with \(X\) at cut \(k\). The plus side uses the change on \(z\) if selected, and otherwise just the lower-whiskered plus copy. The common type of the sides is independent of \(X\). There is exactly one occurrence of \(X\), including when \(k=0\). Division from Response 56 supplies a value for \(X\) and a comparison in the prescribed direction. This chooses \(g^\uparrow\) and \(a^\uparrow\) without any restriction on the given endpoint maps.

Now suppose a forward \(n\)-generator \(f\) has its component. Each independent inverse \(b\) has a cancellation \(\theta\) from a top composite \(w\) (one of \(fb,bf\)) to an identity \(e\). Give \(b^\uparrow\) a fresh variable \(X\). The mixed equation for \(\theta\) has on one side its padded minus copy followed by \(e^\uparrow\) if active. Its other side has \(w^\uparrow\) followed by its padded plus copy. The identity and all lower pads are independent of \(X\). The temporal action contains \(X\) once, even when the two outer faces of the binary chart specialize to the same cell. The common output type involves only cells of dimension at most \(n\), whereas \(X\) has dimension \(n+1\). Division supplies \(b^\uparrow\) and then \(\theta^\uparrow\). The second inverse has a distinct cancellation, so it imposes no second equation on \(X\). Repeat on the new forward cancellations. Their boundaries belong to the syntax generated by temporal composites and identities, so the preceding step supplies every required boundary action, including on iterated composites. Each finite stage retains all earlier choices. Their union has a component at every generator.

**Consequence for the ring.** In any of the three pushouts of Response 56, a based loop of any dimension compares to its retraction if every active head in its term and face dependencies is temporal or an identity. One may also admit all heads of rank at most three. Use the known lower-dimensional construction, choosing the explicit temporal and identity actions there. The lower-dimensional construction joins the prescribed writings with their specified strict cylinder images, so it allows these choices. Evaluate on the term recursively and use the stationary based faces. All inactive labels are substituted unchanged and no bound is imposed on their complexity. The fragment is closed under top composition, hence under every positive power; the comparison is an actual term for each finite power. In degree four, an obstruction to surjectivity must therefore involve an active four-head outside this fragment in every representative of the obstructing class. The assertion does not require naturality of arbitrary higher heads. [All-arity construction through rank three](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md#7-arbitrary-arities-and-conjecture-n-through-dimension-3)

**Reproduction.** For the local inverse step the checker gives the forward and inverse \(n\)-cells opposite types, identifies the two outer poles of their composite, and declares independent endpoint copies of the cancellation at their literal types. For both orders and every up-closed selection marking both top cells it constructs the two sides, checks their common type, absence of the unknown from that type and the constant side, and multiplicity one on the other side. Ranks one through four give 8, 14, 20 and 26 equations. Agda checked both sides of all 22 equations in ranks one and two. These checks verify the input to division. Existence of the solution in an arbitrary groupoidal target uses the single-input theorem, and the iteration uses the all-rank temporal action. [Walking component equation solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_walking_components.py)

dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_walking_components.py 4 --agda "$dir/WalkingComponents.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/WalkingComponents.agda"

# Researcher Response 58

## Seven elementary operations suffice for the four-input part of the obstruction

Every raw four-term is connected in both directions, at its **original literal faces** and over the strict identity, to an expression whose four-dimensional constructors are of two kinds:

* A head on a pasting arity of height at most three.
* An unbiased binary \(X*_j B\) or \(B*_j X\), where \(X\) has dimension four and \(B\) has dimension \(j+1\). There are seven distinct heads, since the two orders at \(j=3\) give the same binary temporal composition.

The lower-dimensional subterms may be arbitrary. Applied to \(i_1,i_2\) of Response 56, **only two of the seven elementary heads still need a mixed action**, the left and right whiskers at cut zero. For \(i_2\) the one-cell in each such whisker is always stationary. Heads of the first kind still require investigation; their strict four-dimensional value is an identity. This separates the outstanding coherence-head problem from the one-cell whisker problem.

**Reduction at literal faces.** First work in the arity of a generic four-head \(h\) of height four. Split its tree at the first branch below its unary trunk, into the first child and the remaining children with that same trunk. The operands share their literal interface globe at the trunk cut. Recursion, with the variable on a disc, gives a binary expression for the full strict pasting. Align its faces with those of \(h\) from dimension one to three as in Response 56. The two writings of each signed face live on that signed boundary arity. They are parallel after alignment of the previous faces and have the same full strict face there. An identity-image pad is therefore available on this lower arity. At the last cut the pad can be chosen as the single coherence between those full-support three-terms. Earlier pads have rank at most three. Each added binary node is elementary since its pad has dimension one above its cut. The framed expression and \(h\) are parallel and have full support on the original pasting arity. Its full-support five-coherence in either orientation compares them over the identity.

For any binary node of rank four and cut \(j\) with operands \(A,B\), put \(p=\min(\dim A,\dim B)\). If \(p>j+1\), pass on the generic two-disc arity to
\[
 (A *_j s_{p-1}B)*_{p-1}(t_{p-1}A *_j B).
\]
In the strict category these are the same cell by interchange with identities. The outer product is elementary, including when \(p=4\). Of the inner products, each still having rank four now has smaller minimum operand dimension. Normalize these on their own arities and substitute; their original faces are retained so the outer product is defined. Align this result to the unsplit product using the lower-arity pads just described. This simultaneously handles both four-inputs when \(p=4\).

Specialize a framed head at its actual labels. For nested four-inputs, replace the top labels recursively inside the elementary expression. A comparison of a label with its replacement propagates through a binary node by whiskering the five-cell with the other label; at a top product replace the two inputs successively. The elementary heads have the required literal face formulas at that dimension. A head of height at most three has no four-label to replace. Composing the comparisons gives the two actual five-terms, and substitution, whiskering and composition preserve their strict identity values. This proof does not use groupoidality and is valid for arbitrary repeated or inactive inputs. Term induction applies to every finite power of a loop.

**Use in the walking test.** Choose the generator changes of Response 57. For \(i_1,i_2\) the retraction fixes all objects. Each elementary head at a positive cut is literally the suspension of a binary head of dimension three. Its two exterior object variables are unmarked, so its partial cylinder and both mixed faces are suspensions of the lower ones. Choose the suspended lower action there, as in Response 55. For consistency choose lower actions the same way on suspended heads with stationary ends; temporal actions permit this choice on their common cases. Substitution then handles arbitrary labels from the pushout, including unsuspended terms. For \(i_0\) temporal composition still has its action even with moving objects, by Gray Response 18. In each case the normalized representative has the same based faces. A mixed evaluation on it supplies a comparison with its retraction, and the normalizing comparisons transfer that conclusion back to the original representative. Thus a nonsurjective \(i_1\) or \(i_2\) in the ring test requires either one of the two outer whiskers or an active structural head of height at most three at which this recursion fails. No assertion of nonsurjectivity is needed for the reduction.

**Checks.** The solver enumerated all 173 four-dimensional trees with four through seven edges (1, 7, 33 and 132 at the successive sizes). It built the framed expression, inspected every rank-four constructor, preserved the top multiplicities, and checked both five-arrows at their literal types. It separately tested every distinct non-elementary binary arity, including those beyond the edge bound, and a horizontal three-branch arity of dimensions \(4,1,4\). Agda checked both orientations on these ten arities, twenty judgments in total. The enumeration used the unbiased original heads; the proof on signed boundary arities handles other literal writings. # Researcher Response 59

## For the walking three-cell, only structural four-heads remain

Both outer whiskers left open for \(i_2\) in Response 58 have exact five-dimensional mixed actions. Consequently **surjectivity on \(\pi_4\) for a pushout of \(i_2\) follows from mixed actions for structural four-heads on arities of heights two and three** at the induced labellings. A failure for \(i_2\) would have to involve such a head in every normalized representative of an obstructing class. A head of height at most one has only stationary inputs here. The \(i_0\) and \(i_1\) alternatives in the ring test remain.

Write \(W(u)=u*_0p\) or \(p*_0u\) for a fixed one-cell \(p\). In the generic four-globe assume the objects and one-cells are unmarked, exactly the condition induced by an \(i_2\)-attachment. Use a dot for temporal composition in execution order. For two temporally composable \(d\)-cells with \(d=3,4\), use the distributor
\[
 D(a,b): W(a)\cdot W(b)\longrightarrow W(a\cdot b).
\]
Both terms have exactly the same immediate faces. The ordinary pasting arity of the adjacent \(d\)-globes and the exterior one-globe supplies the comparison and its reverse over the strict identity. Write \(\bar D\) for the latter.

**Lower choices.** For a selected two-pole \(z\), use \(W(H_z)\) as the change of \(W(z)\). For a selected three-pole \(y\), its minus cap is \(A^-_y=y^- *_2 H_{t_2}\) and its plus cap is \(A^+_y=H_{s_2} *_2 y^+\), with each missing rail omitted. Let \(D^-_y\) compare the distributed minus cap to \(W(A^-_y)\), and let \(\bar D^+_y\) compare \(W(A^+_y)\) to the distributed plus cap. Omit the corresponding distributor when that rail is absent. Use
\[
                D^-_y\cdot W(H_y)\cdot\bar D^+_y
\]
as the change of \(W(y)\). These lower choices have the prescribed strict cylinder values, so they can be used in the all-arity construction through degree three. The outer whisker heads are unsuspended and the suspended choices of Response 58 can use these lower actions.

**The four-cell.** Let \(x:s\to t\) be the top variable. Define \(A^\pm_x\) by the same cut-two formulas, and let \(U^\pm_x\) denote the corresponding distributed caps. At cut three there are two further comparisons, when the relevant two-rail is present:
\[
 \begin{split}
 U^-_x\cdot D^-_t &\longrightarrow D^-_s\cdot W(A^-_x),\\
 \bar D^+_s\cdot U^+_x &\longrightarrow W(A^+_x)\cdot\bar D^+_t .
 \end{split}
\]
For the first, work over the ordinary arity consisting of the four-globe, the target rail's three-globe at cut two, and the exterior one-globe. The two terms have identical three-faces by the defining literal faces of the distributors. Each uses the whole chart and their strict values agree. Form its five-coherence in either direction and substitute. The second uses the three-globe on the other side at cut two. This step accounts for the different *writings* of both three-faces of an outer whisker.

In the prescribed minus cap replace the first pair by the first comparison, then collect \(W(A^-_x)\cdot W(H_t)\) by the rank-four distributor if \(t\) is selected. On the plus side use the second comparison and collect \(W(H_s)\cdot W(A^+_x)\) if \(s\) is selected. The resulting two expressions have the same prefix \(D^-_s\) and suffix \(\bar D^+_t\), whenever present. Between them insert \(W(H_x)\) with that prefix and suffix. Traverse the plus-side comparisons in reverse. Reassociation at cut three uses only the ordinary temporal string arity. If a three-pole is unselected, both its lower two-poles are unselected and its omitted component requires no distributor. The sole five-dimensional generator in the resulting term is \(H_x\), occurring once. Together with its prescribed literal faces this gives the strict whiskered cylinder value (on the free strict tableau, all lower rows are determined by the faces).

The generic substitution allows *any* stationary one-cell \(p\) of the required type and arbitrary labels and changes at the selected poles. It may identify inputs or substitute loops of any finite power. Normalization and the mixed evaluation of Response 58 apply to every based four-loop, also after iterated composition. Thus the removal of both whisker tests holds without any bound on the base, inactive labels or loop length.

**Checks.** For each order of \(W\) the solver constructs the five-term for all seven nonempty up-closed selections in the four-globe with no selected cell below dimension two. It verifies the exact type and the top multiset for all fourteen terms. Its local rewrites construct and type-check both directions, including the plus-side reverses used in the filler. Agda checked the assembled five-term for the left whisker with both two-poles, both three-poles and the four-cell selected. # Researcher Response 60

## The six-seam test reduces to two walking inclusions and two facet stages

**Pushouts of the walking three-cell \(i_2\) preserve \(\pi_4\) at every groupoidal free base.** More generally a pushout of \(i_k\) preserves every based group through \(\max(3,k+2)\), as does the bracket cylinder \(j_k\) of Response 53. Thus only the walking arrow and the walking two-cell remain in the sufficient test of Response 56. Under the six-seam degree-eight RC hypothesis at least one of those two maps has a pushout at a three-connected groupoidal free algebra with a split infinite cyclic summand in its fourth-group cokernel.

There is also a restriction on the actual facet squares: the alternating detector of Response 51 already has nonzero image on a union of **two or three facets**. In the first case the step of Response 52 admits one vertex; in the second case it admits one of the two oriented edges between the two earlier distinguished vertices. In particular stages four, five and six cannot be the first nonzero stage.

**Why depth two suffices.** In a presentation with new generators of rank at least \(k\), mark exactly the new generators. Every lower-dimensional term is old. Pulling this marking back along a substitution into a globular arity cannot mark a variable of rank below \(k\), since its label has the same rank and uses only variables up to that rank. This applies again inside each labelled head and on each of its faces. Hence for a \(d\)-term with \(d\le k+2\), every head in the substitution recursion has depth at most two. For these selections the Gray depth-two theorem supplies the mixed term at its required literal type and strict cylinder value, also for adjacent marks. In particular it covers *all* structural four-heads isolated in Response 59. [Gray Response 13, including its first-layer lemma](canonical-gray-terms-r7.md#researcher-response-13)

In the four-dimensional case its crucial hypothesis can be seen directly. The three-skeleton of the generic cylinder has variable faces: a change there can only be the three-arrow between two copies of a two-variable. The new four-arrows have variable two-poles, even for a change between \(y^- *_2 r_t\) and \(r_s *_2 y^+\) with both rails present. The same two-pole condition holds for a fresh formal four-letter parallel to the faces of a top change. This is the hypotheses check needed to apply the first-layer comparison and lifting lemma at exactly the dimension of the prescribed caps. Finite enumeration alone would not give the comparison.

For \(i_k\) use the generator components of Response 57 between retraction and identity, choosing the depth-two operations for general heads. The binary temporal and identity actions required on walking attaching terms may be taken as in Gray Response 18: those heads have variable poles, and their top counts and literal boundaries agree with the depth-two construction. Equivalently use the depth-two choices themselves in the division equations whenever applicable; their specified strict top chain gives exactly one occurrence of the unknown. Only the depth-two range of the loop and its dependencies is needed. For \(j_k\) the component choice instead follows by the specified-image lifting of Response 53. The retractions fix old cells, so the poles of a based loop at an old vertex are stationary. The mixed label of the loop compares it at its literal type to its retraction. This proves surjectivity; the strict retraction proves injectivity. Through degree three use Responses 53 and 56. No marked or inactive label is bounded, and every finite loop power is one term to which the same proof applies.

Apply the same argument to \(A\subset C\) in the facet filtration of Response 52 when the admitted generator has rank two. Here the mixed labels exist by the globular boundary lifting in \(C\), in either orientation. Its pushout therefore induces an isomorphism on \(\pi_4\). Higher admissions already have this property. A first nonzero image in the integer detector cannot occur at a limit by finite support. At stage two only the vertex can remain, at stage three only the two edges, and at later stages no vertex or edge is missing. Alternation gives the restriction for every choice of the named facets at the first nonzero size, as in Response 51.

**Checks.** The solver enumerated all 3,156 nonempty up-closed selections starting at rank two or higher on arities of height at most four with at most six tree edges. It checked every declaration through rank three, the two-poles of 7,401 four-declarations, and the type and two-poles of 1,210 formal four-letters for five-dimensional generators. In 638 selected charts a four-declaration has composite terms at both ends. In a separate seven-name bracket test an admitted two-generator occurs at both interior positions of a three-cell associator; the term uses that structural four-head in both orientations. The solver computes its retracted writing, places an independent four-loop on the other side at cut zero, and constructs both five-arrows at powers 0, 1 and 3. Agda checked all six specialized types. The range for arbitrary attachments uses the depth-two theorem, and the facet conclusion also uses the alternating detector. # Researcher Response 61

## A single finite five-skeleton tests the degree-eight ring hypothesis

There is an explicit groupoidal free algebra \(G\) with a map to the six-facet star \(S=T_{\{1,\ldots,6\}}\) inducing a **surjection on algebraic \(\pi_4\)**. Its five-skeleton has respectively \(7,55,147,294,588,1176\) generators from rank zero through five. Its ordinary CW realization is contractible. If the ring has identity-image RC through degree eight, then **\(\pi_4(G,0)\) has a split infinite cyclic summand**. In particular, showing that this one algebraic group has no nonzero integral character would disprove the ring hypothesis. The five-skeleton determines the group: both a four-loop and a five-comparison use only generators in this skeleton.

**The presentation and map.** Start at the free point \(0\). Attach six copies of \(i_0\) at \(0\), one per positive vertex \(v\). Use the forward arrow for the directed edge \(0v\), and one of its ordinary inverses for \(v0\). For each of the remaining 37 ordered pairs \(u,v\) (seven loops and thirty distinct positive pairs), attach \(i_1\) at \(1_u\) for a loop, or at the path \(u0v\) otherwise. Designate its duplicate one-cell as the edge \(uv\). This defines \(G\), including the independent inverse towers of all 43 attachments. Send each designated edge to the literal bracket \([u,v]\) in \(S\). On a ray send the other ordinary inverse to \([v,0]\); on a newly attached two-cell take the bracket from its anchor to its designated edge. Extend each tower by choosing primitive brackets at its successive required types. All of an attachment's images lie in the full bracket algebra on its endpoints together with \(0\). This gives a map \(q:G\to S\).

**Why every based four-class lifts.** For each set \(B\) of vertices containing \(0\), the attachments whose endpoints lie in \(B\) form the same walking presentation \(G_B\) on those vertices, with its designated edges. It is groupoidal and three-connected by Response 56 applied successively from the free point. Its map takes values in the full bracket algebra \(P(B)\) when \(B\) misses a positive vertex.

Starting from \(G\), process all bracket generators \(g\) of \(S\) in ranks \(k=2,3,4\), in dimension order. Maintain a lift of the old skeleton which sends a generator into the part supported on its names together with \(0\). The designated vertices and edges give this in ranks zero and one. For the next generator put \(B=\{0\}\cup\operatorname{supp}(g)\). Its two lifted faces belong to the current \(B\)-part. This part started as \(G_B\) and has itself been extended only by walking attachments supported in \(B\); all their anchors were chosen on their own supports. It is still groupoidal and three-connected. Hence there is a \(k\)-cell \(z\) on the lifted boundary: for parallel positive-dimensional faces use basing, vanishing of the corresponding group through degree three, and reflection along invertible whiskerings as in Response 53. Attach \(i_k\) at \(z\) and use its duplicate as the lift of \(g\). Extend the map to \(S\) by the bracket \(q(z)\to g\) and its inverse data in \(P(B)\), which is a complete facet subalgebra on this proper support. Assign the whole attachment to \(B\). This proves the support assertion at the next step, also in every larger local part. At limit stages loops and comparisons have finite support.

In the resulting extension the lifts give a literal section on the four-skeleton of \(S\). All the inclusions used after \(G\) induce isomorphisms on \(\pi_4\) by Response 60, also for the filtered union by finite support. A lifted four-loop therefore represents a class from \(G\). This proves the surjection without prescribing the lengths, incidence patterns or multiplicities of any input to a head. Under the ring hypothesis, compose with the integral detector of Response 48, which takes the value one on the based cubical difference. A preimage of this class splits the resulting surjection onto \(\mathbb Z\).

**Ordinary contraction.** In the normalized cellular chains, when an attachment duplicates \(z\) to \(g\) its forward generator \(a\) has boundary \(g-N(z)\), where \(N(z)\) counts the top occurrences. For any forward generator \(f\), its two ordinary inverses \(b,c\) have the negative of its boundary. Its forward cancellations \(P,Q\) have boundaries \(-(f+b)\), \(-(c+f)\), respectively. Extend the chain homotopy \(H\) from the old presentation by
\[
 H(g)=a+H(N(z)),\qquad H(f)=0,\qquad H(b)=-P,\qquad H(c)=-Q.
\]
This repeats on each forward cancellation. On \(g\) the old homotopy identity on \(N(z)\) gives the identity relative to the root. On \(a\) one has \(H\partial a=a\); on a later forward cancellation the same identity follows from the two inverse formulas. On \(b,c\) the extra \(f\) in \(\partial H\) cancels \(H\partial\). This is an integral contraction in every degree. The ordinary fundamental group is trivial: in the fundamental groupoid the two ray cancellations identify both inverse edges with the inverse of their forward ray. Each duplicate edge is identified by its two-cell with the old path or identity. The six forward rays form a tree. The simply connected acyclic CW realization is therefore contractible by Hurewicz and Whitehead. The remaining test concerns the algebraic group of raw based four-terms modulo five-comparison.

**Checks.** The solver constructs the actual five-skeleton by substituting the walking templates at their specified anchors. It checks the literal types of all 49 designated edges, closure of every generator carrier under dependencies, and the complete directed edge set in each of the 64 rooted restrictions. It computes integer boundaries from the raw terms and verifies \(\partial H+H\partial=1-\iota\epsilon\) on all 1,091 generators through rank four. Agda checked the two generic templates' independent inverses and their cancellations with forward cancellation rank at most four (40 declarations). The surjection on algebraic classes uses the support-preserving lifting argument, rather than a search cut off at a chosen term size. # Researcher Response 62

## The walking graph would have fourth-group rank growing at least as the sixth power

Let \(G_M\) denote the presentation of Response 61 on a root and a set \(M\) of positive names. Under the six-seam ring hypothesis there is a split inclusion
\[
      \mathbb Z^{(\binom M6)}\ \hookrightarrow\ \pi_4(G_M,0).
\]
Here the basis is indexed by the six-element subsets of \(M\). For \(m=|M|<\infty\) the presentation has only \(12(m+1)^2\) four-generators and \(24(m+1)^2\) five-generators. Its realization is contractible for every \(M\). Thus an upper bound \(o(m^6)\) on the rational algebraic fourth-group rank of this explicit family would already decide the six-seam test negatively. The argument gives a specific summand even if the ambient group has infinite rank.

**Literal forgetting maps.** For \(A\subseteq M\), retain every walking attachment whose positive endpoints belong to \(A\). On any other attachment send its duplicate to the retracted anchor. Send the rest of its template to the trivial equivalence on that anchor as follows. In the source globe \(D^k\), give the duplicate the original \(k\)-variable as value, then for each higher declaration take the full-support coherence on that globe at its substituted faces. Both faces still use the original variable. Substitute the retracted anchor into this single fixed globe assignment. This constructs a raw retraction \(r_A\) with image in \(G_A\), including for an oriented edge whose anchor uses two distinct rays. For \(A,B\subseteq M\) one has \(r_A r_B=r_{A\cap B}\) literally. An attachment retained twice is unchanged. On an attachment first folded at either step the result is the fixed globe assignment evaluated on the twice-retracted anchor, by substitution; this is also the rule for the intersection. Induction over attachment order proves the identity on every generator. Rooted relabellings by injections commute with the construction on their images.

On \(M=\{1,\ldots,6\}\) put \(E=\prod_i(1-(r_{M-\{i\}})_*)\) on the abelian fourth group. Its image is the intersection of the six kernels. Use Response 61 to choose \(x\) above the cubical class with value one under the detector \(\Phi\) of Response 48. Every nonidentity term in \(Ex-x\) maps into a single complete facet under \(q\); its value there is zero. Thus \(h=Ex\) still has \(\Phi q_*(h)=1\). In particular the split integral class can be chosen to vanish after *each* individual deletion. This argument requires no compatibility of \(q\) with folds in the bracket algebra.

For each six-subset \(T\) of general \(M\), identify it with the six names in a chosen order. Include \(h\) on those names. To read its coordinate, retract to \(G_T\), relabel, and apply \(\Phi q_*\). On a class included from a different six-subset the composite factors through a proper rooted subset, giving zero. The diagonal gives one. For infinite \(M\) all but finitely many coordinates vanish on any single loop because it is a finite term. These coordinate maps supply the claimed retraction. Deleting a positive name \(a\) consequently has a kernel containing a split free group on the six-subsets containing \(a\). This uses the polarization argument of Response 9 on the *algebraic groups of the walking presentations* with the literal retractions just constructed.

**Restriction on a chain calculation.** The integral chain homotopy of Response 61 commutes with every deletion. This is immediate on a retained block by its support. In a folded block each cell strictly above the anchor dimension has zero top chain after folding; on the duplicate the formula for \(H\) reduces exactly to \(H\) of the folded anchor. This also works on vertices relative to the root. In any fixed tensor power of the augmented cellular chains split off the root in each factor. On each summand with a reduced factor, the homotopy in its first reduced factor (with the tensor differential sign) contracts the summand and commutes with the deletions. Hence the image of \(E\) on each tensor power is integrally contractible. Even on the underlying graded groups \(E\) kills tensors of one or two generators, since at least one of the six names is absent. For three generator factors only the ordered perfect matchings of the six names can survive, since each carrier has at most two positive names. This restriction allows arbitrary multiplicities and loop powers. A character built using additional data of the weak terms would require a separate analysis.

**Checks.** The solver constructs all four retractions on the two-positive-name graph through rank four, checks every image against its substituted type on all 203 declarations, and tests literal equality for all sixteen composites. It checks commutation with \(H\) in the range where the next rank is present. On all six names it counts zero full-support ordered carrier patterns for one or two factors and 90 for three factors, all disjoint pairs. Agda checked twelve images on a two-ray edge attachment, including the two single-name folds and their composite. Every walking generator is carried on a restriction with at most two positive names. The proof by substitution handles all ranks; the bound on fourth-group rank is conditional on the ring hypothesis.
