# Researcher Response 63

## A forced complete graph on the cube and a smaller algebraic test

Every proper-facet colouring of a \(k\)-cube of nonzero boundary degree uses **every unordered pair of its \(k+1\) colours on an actual cube edge**. In fact the cube graph contains a subdivision of the complete graph in which the entire path for \(\{i,j\}\) is confined to those two colours by its containing facets. Thus the fifteen distinct nonconstant edge types of the minimum-coloured five-cube are optimal, counting unordered pairs. Using its descending orientation gives a ring test with just **six walking arrows and fifteen walking two-cells**, or alternatively six specified low attachments starting at a single complete facet.

**The forced paths.** Choose an omission on each exterior facet. The pairs of opposite omissions form a tree \(T\) by Response 18. A cube vertex orients each edge of \(T\) toward the omission on the incident facet selected along that axis. Its permissible colours are exactly the vertices with no incoming edges, before imposing any additional restrictions. For each name \(i\), orient the tree away from \(i\). The corresponding cube vertex is forced to have colour \(i\).

To connect this vertex to the one forced to have colour \(j\), reverse the edges of the tree path from \(i\) to \(j\), starting at the \(j\) end and working back to \(i\). Each reversal is one cube edge. On that edge leave its own axis unspecified. The remaining orientations point away from \(i\) on its component of the cut tree and away from \(j\) on the other. The omissions on the facets containing this *entire edge* are therefore exactly the names outside \(\{i,j\}\). At every interior path vertex the permissible set is exactly \(\{i,j\}\); the endpoints have distinct forced singleton colours. This forces a transition and also makes the interiors of distinct unordered-pair paths disjoint. Axes may be flipped against the cube direction; the conclusion about arbitrary colourings is unordered. Even if an edge is labelled by an arbitrary raw one-term respecting these facet omissions, its strict path at a transition must contain a generating edge crossing between \(i\) and \(j\). Inserting repetitions or any powers of loops within the permitted names cannot change this endpoint condition.

**Use of just the descending edges.** Give the five-cube its minimum colouring from Response 48. Along each directed edge the minimum stays fixed or decreases; every strict decrease \(i\to j\) occurs. Label fixed-colour edges by the weak identity and the other edges by the corresponding bracket. In higher ranks assign each proper cube generator the bracket on its two substituted attaching terms. This is typed in its vertex carrier by induction. The two four-poles \(u,v\) of the top bracket \(g=[u,v]\) still give unit facet degree up to orientation. Indeed all boundary faces are carried in their original proper simplex faces, and the chosen vertex map is unchanged. On the boundary the carried realization is homotopic to the staircase vertex map by extending within those convex faces. The top bracket has only positive names. Thus the same integral detector of Response 48 applies to this choice.

In Response 61 retain the six rays at \(0\) and just the fifteen \(i_1\)-attachments at \(i0j\) for \(i>j>0\). Call the resulting algebra \(G^\downarrow\). The literal lifts of the changed cubical one-skeleton are its designated decreasing edges and its identities. Lift the finitely many higher generators of the proper cube through rank four as in Response 61, using \(i_k\), \(k=2,3,4\), in the corresponding rooted vertex parts. These parts start three-connected by Response 56. At each step the two lifted poles therefore admit the old anchor, and the primitive comparison and its inverse data map into the proper simplex on the carrier together with \(0\). This constructs parallel lifts of \(u,v\). Base the pair identically at \(0\) in the extension, using its groupoidality and three-connectivity. The images use identical pads, so whiskering \(g\) with these pads still has \(g\) once as its entire five-count. Hence the based difference has detector value one under the ring hypothesis. All the added higher attachments induce isomorphisms on \(\pi_4\) by Response 60. Consequently the character already takes value one on \(\pi_4(G^\downarrow,0)\). This proof needs only the cubical pair, with no claim of surjectivity onto the whole star from the smaller graph. Its five-skeleton has ranks \(7,33,81,162,324,648\); the ordinary contraction of Response 61 still applies. The deletion and six-subset argument also applies using order-preserving inclusions.

For the alternative tower, start instead at the full bracket facet on \(0,1,\ldots,5\). Attach \(i_0\) at \(0\) for vertex 6, then \(i_1\) at each of the five paths \(60j\), \(1\le j\le5\), to obtain the designated edges \(6j\). Each rooted restriction starts at a complete bracket algebra and retains the indicated walking attachments supported there, so the preceding lifting proof applies without change. The starting group is zero. If the ring hypothesis holds, the first of these **six particular successive maps** on which the image of the pulled-back detector becomes nonzero has a split integral summand in its fourth-group cokernel. The base and each anchor are specified. The argument works for the full fourth groups, regardless of inactive terms or loop powers.

**Checks.** The solver generated every labelled omission tree on two through six names by Prüfer codes. For six names it checked 19,440 pair paths on 1,296 trees, recomputing the allowed set on each edge and vertex and checking disjoint interiors. For the minimum colouring it enumerated every directed cube edge and computed the staircase degree independently in each of these sizes. On six names it found precisely fifteen nonconstant directed types and degree \(-1\) in the solver's convention. The walking solver builds the smaller five-skeleton, verifies all 27 designated oriented edge types (including the twelve ray directions), and checks the integral contraction on its 607 generators through degree four. [Forced-edge solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_forced_edges.py) · [Walking graph constructor](../../../tools/relative_coherence_walking_graph.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_forced_edges.py 6
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_walking_graph.py --descending
~~~

# Researcher Response 64

## Which omission trees carry a directed simplex

For the facet omissions of Response 63, let \(C_U\) consist of cube faces on which the fixed omissions exclude every name outside \(U\). In other words, every vertex outside \(U\) receives at least one fixed incoming edge in the omission tree. **Every nonempty \(C_U\) is contractible of dimension \(|U|-1\).** Nevertheless there is a carrier-preserving strict map of the full oriental to the directed cube, for some ordering of its names and with each simplex on \(U\) supported in \(C_U\), **exactly when** the roots can be listed along a path in the tree so that their cube vertices turn on axes \(1,2,\ldots,k\) in that order. This uses the tensor differential and the oriental triangle directed from shortcut to subdivided path. Both the necessity and the all-dimensional strict construction concern the prescribed carriers.

**The topology.** Remove a leaf \(v\) with neighbour \(u\). If \(v\notin U\), its edge is forced into \(v\); the carrier is exactly the smaller carrier on the remaining tree. If \(v\in U\), the layer with the edge into \(u\) is the smaller carrier for \((U-\{v\})\cup\{u\}\). Attached to it is the product of the smaller carrier for \(U-\{v\}\) with the leaf interval. At the other endpoint, or when that coordinate is free, \(u\) has to meet the old constraint without help from \(v\). Pushing this interval onto its attached end gives a deformation retraction. The one-vertex tree starts the induction, with empty carrier for the empty set. At least one different edge must be fixed for each name outside \(U\), giving the dimension bound. Attain it by growing a spanning forest from all the roots in \(U\), adding one adjacent unvisited vertex and its incoming edge at a time. Leave the other \(|U|-1\) tree edges free. Also \(C_U\cap C_V=C_{U\cap V}\). In particular one can extend the map of forced roots continuously across successive simplex faces inside these carriers.

**The directed obstruction.** The singleton carriers are the rooted orientations. A directed edge in the cube only increases its bit, so a simplex requires all these vertices to form a chain in the Boolean lattice. Hamming distance between root orientations is tree distance. Between the first and last vertices in a Boolean chain distances add, so every name must lie on their tree path. The path must turn on one new coordinate at each step. For a pair of roots its carrier is exactly the path of Response 63: off their tree path all edges are forced outwards, and on it the absence of an interior source forces the two end-directed strings to meet at one position. In particular there is only one directed route for the shortcut edge; it switches the coordinates in *reverse* order along that interval.

Take three consecutive names on this path. Their carrier on its two variable axes is a square. The shortcut flips the second path axis before the first, while the subdivided route flips them in path order. The positive square moves high-axis-first to low-axis-first. If two consecutive path axes descend in their ambient indices, the required triangle reverses that square. Project onto those two axes. The one-chain of target minus source is then minus the nonzero boundary of the projected square. Every strict two-cell in that square has nonnegative top coefficient, excluding the reverse. This obstruction also excludes every raw CaTT derivation by strict interpretation. Common inactive paths add the same one-chain to both sides, with multiplicity; stationary loops of any finite power therefore preserve the obstruction. More generally it suffices that all additional inactive data have a strict projection preserving that boundary difference. For incomparable root vertices the corresponding obstruction is already a decreasing zero/one coordinate. Neither argument bounds the size of intermediate terms.

**Positive instructions.** In the aligned path write \(P_{ab}\) for the positive one-chain of the route switching coordinates \(b,b-1,\ldots,a+1\). For ordered names \(i_0<\cdots<i_d\) take the cubical chain
\[
  1^{\,i_0}\otimes P_{i_0i_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes P_{i_{d-1}i_d}
       \otimes 0^{\,k-i_d}.
\]
Each summand belongs to \(C_{\{i_0,\ldots,i_d\}}\). Its differential is the alternating sum of the face instructions: \(\partial P_{ab}=1^{b-a}-0^{b-a}\), and the two adjacent terms for deleting an interior name combine by \(P_{ac}=0^{b-a}P_{bc}+P_{ab}1^{c-b}\). The assignment preserves augmentation and sends the top simplex to the single top cube. Steiner's construction therefore gives the strict map. The formula in the aligned case is the known section of the oriental from the cube; the carrier and obstruction arguments above classify when an arbitrary omission assignment admits such instructions. [Masuda, Corollary A.3.6](https://nmasuda2.github.io/notes/thesis_stable.pdf)

For the six-seam test, the minimum colouring used in Response 63 has a star omission assignment and cannot run this directed construction backwards from its forced vertices, even though all its carriers are contractible. An aligned path assignment provides a strict alternative for comparing simplex and cube parameters. Lifting the higher instructions at prescribed raw faces remains a separate condition for using them as CaTT fillings.

**Checks.** For five names the solver tested 3,875 tree/carrier instances for the leaf recursion and exact dimension. Among all 48,000 signed, axis-ordered omission trees, 2,880 admitted a chain of forced roots and 120 passed every consecutive triangle. It verified positivity, exact supports and signed boundary on every simplex instruction through cube dimension eight. Independently it constructed the directed square with flanking loops and different boundary bracketings at exponents 0, 1 and 5. It constructed the reassociation and its reverse at literal types; Agda checked these six judgments and the three directed composites. The reverse of the square is excluded by the integer boundary argument for all exponents. [Omission carrier solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_omission_carriers.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_omission_carriers.py --trees 5 --cube 8 --agda "$dir/DirectedCarriers.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/DirectedCarriers.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 65

## The aligned simplex instruction has a raw lift through rank four, and on a chosen five-cube

Fix an ordinary, face-compatible weak presentation of the four-skeleton of a cube and of the four-skeleton of its aligned oriental. The strict instruction of Response 64 **lifts to a single CaTT substitution on these skeleta, simultaneously on all simplex faces, with the required cubical carrier on each face**. If the cube has dimension five and the weak five-oriental is prescribed, one can choose the declaration of the cube's five-generator to extend this substitution through its top cell. In the opposite direction a literal raw splitting of the section is already impossible on a shortcut edge of length at least two. This distinguishes a compatible lift from a strict raw retraction in using the oriental test.

**Lifting on each rectangle.** For \(U=(i_0<\cdots<i_d)\), all coordinates outside the interval \([i_0,i_d]\) are fixed in \(C_U\). In each gap \([i_{j-1},i_j]\) the non-\(U\) vertices must have fixed incoming edges. Exactly the subdivided path described in Response 63 is available in that gap. The gap constraints are independent, since their shared endpoints belong to \(U\). Thus \(C_U\) is the cubical product of those \(d\) directed subdivided intervals as a cell complex, with the tensor orientation on its cubes. Its one-graph is acyclic. Both faces of a generating square are nonempty paths of length two. The inherited weak computad therefore has identity-image comparisons through rank three by Responses 7 and 16. Any required strict cell of rank at most four can be lifted at prescribed parallel literal faces there: lift a strict composition expression and correct its interfaces in increasing dimension, then its two exterior faces. Only comparisons up to one rank below the expression are needed. [Boundary correction, Response 7](#researcher-response-7)

Proceed in order of simplex dimension. The images of the two attaching terms of its next atom are already parallel raw terms, supported in \(C_U\) by inclusion of the proper carriers. Their strict images are the faces of the tensor instruction. Apply the preceding lifting in \(C_U\) itself and fix that choice for all later occurrences of this atom. This proves the simultaneous assertion for arbitrary cube width through rank four and allows arbitrary well-typed external substitutions afterward. For \(k=5\) the top instruction has exactly one tile, the full cube. Its required two four-faces are now literally the substituted attaching terms of the prescribed five-simplex. Use them as the type of the new five-variable. They have the strict faces of the cube atom by the chain map, so this presents the same strict five-cube. Supply this variable as the image of the top simplex generator. Compatible weak presentations of the proper simplex faces and of the five-simplex are available by [Response 35](#researcher-response-35).

For \(k=6\) this gives all the lower substitutions used by the aligned section through rank four. Among the seven five-simplex facets, omitting the first or last name gives a single cubical facet, so each can again be assigned its five-variable with the prescribed type. Omitting an interior name gives a product with one interval of length two and four intervals of length one, hence two five-dimensional tiles. Their strict sum is the required instruction. The remaining five section judgments at this level ask for the terms on these two-tile rectangles at the prescribed four-faces. Each cubical five-tile belongs to just one of these seven carrier interiors, by its fixed omission. This describes the freedom to choose declarations and the remaining joins; it does not assert that a strict rectangle by itself supplies those joins.

**The returned edge.** In dimension at least two the shortcut from the first root to the third crosses two distinct cube coordinates. Its image under any raw lift of the section must be a coherence-headed one-term: a variable in the cube is a single directed generating edge. Raw substitution preserves the outer coherence constructor. Consequently no return substitution into the simplex can make the composite literally its original edge variable. For the usual strict retraction, on vertices take the initial run length of ones. On cubical edges take the corresponding simplex edge when the length changes and a weak identity otherwise. Along a shortcut corridor the length changes only on the last switch. Hence the returned raw one-term has the same strict word as the original edge; path coherence supplies comparisons in both directions. This applies to every gap length and to all substitutions into the comparison, including stationary paths of unbounded length. Any argument using literal splitting in place of these comparisons would miss this issue already in rank one.

**Checks.** The term solver constructs all four vertex images, six shortcut paths and four triangles of the tetrahedral section in the typed three-cube. On the longer triangles it uses the directed interchange route and corrects its path bracketing. It substitutes these into the tetrahedron's actual attaching terms to define the cube's top type, checks the signed square counts and path faces against those of the original strict cube atom, and checks all fifteen entries of the resulting substitution. It also returns the longest shortcut along the one-skeleton retraction and constructs its comparison with the original edge in both directions. Agda accepted the fifteen section judgments and both shortcut comparisons. The proof through rank four uses the all-input rectangle argument, and the rank-five statement uses the stated freedom in the top declaration. [Raw section and shortcut solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_oriental_section.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_oriental_section.py --agda "$dir/OrientalSection.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/OrientalSection.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 66

## One last-coordinate deletion suffices for the five-skeleton fold in every cube width

For the aligned section of Response 65, the opposite strict fold sends a vertex to the length of its initial run of ones. There is a single sufficient raw judgment for implementing this fold through rank five in **every** width, with a shared choice of face presentations. It is a five-arrow in the four-cube between two prescribed four-terms over its top atom. The marked lemma reduces this judgment to RC(4) on the identities of just the two strict three-faces of that atom. Each consists of four cubical three-atoms. This reduction concerns transport between cubical and oriental presentations. It leaves the six-seam alternative open.

**The entire strict image.** Number the coordinates from zero. For a face \(f\), let \(z\) be its first fixed zero, or the width when none exists. Let \(A\) be its free indices less than \(z\), and \(h\) the number of free indices greater than \(z\). Its image is the \(h\)-fold identity on the ordinary oriental atom \([A,z]\), with the indices in increasing order. On chains this sends a basis face to that simplex if \(h=0\), and to zero otherwise. To verify the differential, first delete the trailing free coordinates by tensor augmentation. If any is present, only the two settings of a sole trailing free coordinate could contribute, and they cancel. Otherwise a fixed-one setting of the \(j\)-th free coordinate deletes vertex \(j\) (counting from zero). Only the last free coordinate has a nonzero fixed-zero facet image, deleting the last vertex with the remaining simplicial sign. This also proves augmentation preservation in degree zero.

For the whole tableau, deleting a factor by augmentation preserves the atom of the retained cube with identity padding by the row formula of Response 35. It suffices to inspect the all-free cube, relabelling vertices by \([A,z]\). In a given row the nonzero simplex image determines the free set by its vertices except the last; the last specifies the first fixed zero. In opposite proper rows all fixed signs for that free set are reversed. A prescribed first zero cannot occur on both rows, and if there is none the fixed settings must all be ones. Thus opposite proper row images have disjoint supports. Downward recursion by the signed differential gives precisely the simplex atom, including its lower rows. In every tensor instruction of Response 64 just the tile taking the first axis in **each** gap survives the fold: every later choice has a fixed zero before a free coordinate. That tile returns exactly the original simplex. The composite of the two strict maps is the identity in all ranks.

**A single raw deletion.** Use one weak \(d\)-cube \(Q_d\) in each intrinsic dimension through five, copied on every face by increasing coordinate inclusions. These standard face-compatible presentations exist through this range (item 33). Seek \(D_d:Q_d\longrightarrow Q_{d-1}\) on cells forgetting the last coordinate. On either last-coordinate facet prescribe the identity; on a facet fixing an earlier coordinate prescribe \(D_{d-1}\) in that face. These prescriptions agree on intersections: fixing the last coordinate restricts the smaller deletion to an identity, and fixing two earlier coordinates restricts both maps to the same smaller deletion. For \(d=1\) take the weak identity edge. For \(2\le d\le4\) their images on the two attaching terms are parallel terms of strict value the top atom of \(Q_{d-1}\). Prescribed-boundary lifting through rank four in that cube supplies \(D_d\) at the strict identity. Fix these choices. At \(d=5\) only the top judgment remains. In \(Q_4\) every five-term over these ends has identity strict value. A cut-three factorization of its strict four-atom assigns its sole four-generator either to the prefix or to the suffix; a factor of zero top count is an identity. Its only slices are therefore its two three-faces. Response 38 applies since the cubical three-skeleton has an acyclic graph and RC(3). Its marked replacement argument quantifies over all old four-terms, including arbitrary inactive labels and any finite loop powers their types allow. No bound on raw syntax enters this reduction.

**How the deletion propagates.** For the chart \((A,z,h)\) first delete its last free coordinate \(h\) times, always in decreasing order, then apply the intrinsic fold with the remaining simplex names. Build the intrinsic nondegenerate folds in increasing dimension: their proper chart assignments are already given in smaller dimensions. Declare the top simplex variable to have the substituted cubical attaching type and send the top cube to this variable. Copy this universal simplex presentation on ordered subsets. To check the face equations for the recipe, fixing a trailing coordinate causes the deletion of that coordinate to restrict to the identity and all other trailing free coordinates to be deleted in their original order. Fixing a leading coordinate first retains all trailing deletions. Its positive setting then omits one vertex; its negative setting makes that index the new stop and deletes the newly trailing indices in decreasing order. In both cases the resulting raw maps are literally the recipe on the common subface, using only the defining restrictions of \(D_d\) and ordinary composition of substitutions. This proves the shared five-skeleton assertion for arbitrary width, including all mixed intersections. Through rank four it is unconditional. The simplex declarations here are chosen along the fold.

For an arbitrarily prescribed six-cube boundary with an ordinary weak four-skeleton in the target there is also a direct finite alternative. Lift its fold through rank four by Response 16 in each face's simplex support. Seven of the twelve five-facets map to seven different five-simplex atoms; choose each target declaration at the substituted type. The other five map to identities over \(O_0,\ldots,O_4\) respectively. The first two comparisons exist over globular pasting contexts. The remaining three concern a triangle, a tetrahedron, and a four-simplex atom. By Response 38 the last only requires old comparisons on its two three-faces, of two and three tetrahedra respectively. Thus the direct route needs four strict three-labels (including the identity on the triangle); the uniform deletion route needs the two cubical labels above. With a weak six-cube already presented, either successful fold of its actual five-boundary would permit the six-simplex declaration at its substituted top type. Neither route presupposes a weak six-cube.

**Reproduction.** The solver checks the signed differential, both sides of every tableau row, the restriction recipe at every facet inclusion, and the composite on every simplex instruction through width eight. At width six this is 729 face tableaux and 127 simplex instructions. In the three-cube it constructs all 27 entries of \(D_3\) carried by one square. It also constructs the reverse of the top comparison over that same square and checks its literal endpoints. Agda 2.8.0 accepted all 27 entries and the reverse in safe mode. The exponent-unrestricted statements above follow from the row calculation, the raw face equations, and the marked lemma. [Fold and typed deletion solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_first_zero_fold.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_first_zero_fold.py --cube 8 --agda "$dir/FirstZeroFold.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/FirstZeroFold.agda"
~~~

# Researcher Response 67

## Retyping the three-skeleton preserves the four-term test; twelve cubical representatives suffice

If the six-seam ring has identity-image RC through degree eight, **at least two inequivalent signed isomorphism classes of the twenty-cube labels of Response 39 fail RC(4) over their strict identities**. One failing class occurs among the following twelve state representatives, in the numbering of Response 17:
\[
 2,\ 3,\ 4,\ 5,\ 37,\ 38,\ 41,\ 42,\ 46,\ 57,\ 58,\ 61.
\]
The equivalence allows any generator relabelling and independent choices of globular opposite in each dimension. The assertion holds for any ordinary weak attaching terms on each support presenting the specified strict generators. The same argument reduces the two sufficient three-label hypotheses for the deletion of Response 66 to **one**, over a hemisphere of the four-cube with 15 vertices and four three-atoms.

**Why changing the attaching syntax preserves this test.** Let \(A,B\) be weak presentations through dimension three of the same based strict polygraph, with a common one-skeleton. Here “based” specifies the generating cells. Assume identity-image RC through degree three in both. Construct substitutions on cells \(F:A\to B,\ G:B\to A\) fixing the one-skeleton and inducing the identity on all strict generators. For each new two- or three-generator lift its strict cell at the images of its already assigned faces. Prescribed-boundary lifting here only uses comparisons through degree two. Fix a strict three-label \(w\). A comparison of every parallel pair over \(1_w\) in one presentation implies the corresponding assertion in the other.

Indeed put a mixed component from \(GF\) to the identity on every generator of rank two and three in \(A\), leaving ranks zero and one stationary. For a two-generator its two cap terms have literally the same one-faces. Choose an identity-image three-comparison. For a three-generator the two prescribed padded three-terms have the same strict value: both cap maps are the identity strictly and the two-cell changes evaluate to strict identities. Use identity-image RC(3) at these literal faces. The induced change on each raw term through degree four now exists by the depth-two theorem. Every argument of dimension below two uses only the stationary skeleton, so even in the arity of a substituted four-head the least moved rank is at least two. All these changes have identity strict value by substitution into the strict collapsed cylinder. The three- and four-dimensional changes used as side data are weakly invertible: an identity-image change contains no generator of its own rank by exact support, so its top cells are structural.

Here is the reflection at *parallel* four-terms, including the moving poles. Fix their common one-faces and work in the homotopy bicategory of the resulting twice-iterated hom weak category. Objects here are two-cells, arrows are three-cells, and its parallel two-morphisms are four-terms modulo five-comparison. In the three-skeleton all five-comparisons are weakly invertible, so equality in that quotient is witnessed by an actual five-term in either direction. The bicategory laws are the usual homotopy truncation of the weak-category operations, as in [Response 15](#researcher-response-15). For \(T,U:f\to g\), use the two mixed five-terms to identify the respective whiskerings of \(GF(T),GF(U)\) with those of \(T,U\), pasted with the same changes at \(f,g\). The side changes at two-poles are equivalences in this bicategory, and the changes at \(f,g\) are invertible two-morphisms. If \(F(T)\) and \(F(U)\) compare, apply \(G\), cancel the common invertible two-morphisms, and cancel whiskering by the side equivalence using its quasi-inverse. This gives equality of \(T,U\) at the original literal faces, hence the required five-term. It has identity strict image by dimension. Interchange \(A,B\) for the other implication. For a claim inside a dependency-closed support do the whole construction there; Response 16 gives the hypotheses on the supports used here. There is no bound on occurrences of a generator, on inactive subterms, or on powers of a permitted loop. Depth is bounded by its lowest moved rank, independently of those quantities.

A signed isomorphism first takes the specified globular opposite of the syntax and then relabels. At a reversed rank exchange the attaching poles and reflect the order of the children at that level of every pasting tree, permuting the substitution entries accordingly. Induction on constructors gives the opposite typing judgment and commutes with substitution; applying the same opposite twice recovers the original. Thus one can transport to the opposed raw presentation, then apply the retyping result to the chosen target presentation. In particular, complementing every bit of the four-cube takes the negative strict three-face to the positive one when all globular ranks are opposed. Its two supports can carry different ordinary raw presentations. A comparison for every pair over the identity of the negative label therefore suffices on both sides of the marked lemma for \(D_5\).

**Classifying the states.** On six-bit faces act by global bit complementation and reversal of the six coordinate positions. For a free subset of size \(j\), the bit in a fixed position in a signed row of a cube atom is its row sign times \((-1)^{\#\{\text{free positions earlier}\}}\). Complementation exchanges the two row signs in every proper rank; reversing axes exchanges them at odd \(j\). These are exactly the corresponding globular opposites. The test checks the complete rows and that all transformed top states occur. There are 32 orbits of size four and ten of size two.

These orbits exhaust even signed isomorphisms defined only on the support. Each of the two one-rows of a state is a directed path flipping all six axes in order, one increasing and one decreasing. An isomorphism must send the first path to one of these two paths, possibly reversing its direction. On the actual generating edge graph of each support the solver computes distances from every one of the 12 vertices of the two paths to all 42 support vertices. They equal Hamming distances. Along either ordered path the consecutive distance differences recover every bit of a vertex: a step switching an axis from zero to one reduces distance by one exactly for a vertex with that bit one. Thus the image of the first ordered path fixes the entire vertex map; its four possibilities are precisely the displayed ambient operations. The vertex set of a generating cubical face determines that face, so no further relabelling of generators is possible. This proves completeness for the **labelled supports**.

**The implication from colourings.** For each of the 46,080 signed priority colourings, Response 39 forces at least one failing whole state in its full-colour set. Project each such set to the 42 classes. This gives 93 different permissions, 16 inclusion-minimal. Seed 34 and axis priority \((0,1,4,5,2,3)\) give the twelve representatives above, the smallest projected size. No class meets all permissions. Two classes represented by states 7 and 37 together meet all of them; that pair only certifies tightness of this counting argument. Thus the lower bound of two abstract failure classes and the twelve-case sufficient test both follow. The existence of a five-arrow over the hemisphere and the actual failure classes under the ring hypothesis remain undecided.

**Verification.** In addition to the graph distances, full tableaux and finite hitting calculation, the solver changes the literal two-source of a three-generator, types the two substitutions between the presentations and constructs both four-directions comparing the returned generator with the original. It constructs both five-directions identifying the framed identity on the returned generator with the framed identity on the original, at the uncancelled literal faces. [Symmetry and retyping checks](../../../tools/relative_coherence_state_symmetries.py)

dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_state_symmetries.py --agda "$dir/StateSymmetries.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/StateSymmetries.agda"

# Researcher Response 68

## The twelve-class test is optimal for arbitrary signed colourings, and there are exactly two smallest lists

Work with the 42 classes of whole strict labels from Response 67. Grant the four-term comparison in each class on a list \(P\), called the permission. On the other states and on all exterior facets require the proper-restriction condition of Response 29 on the colouring coefficients. **For every permission of at most nineteen classes, existence of a nonzero signed boundary degree is decided exactly**, over the integers and over every modulus \(m\ge2\). It holds precisely when \(P\) contains one of ten inclusion-minimal lists; two have size 12, two size 15, two size 17 and four size 18. Every positive case has an ordinary colouring of degree \(+1\) or \(-1\). The two smallest sufficient lists for disproving degree-eight RC on the ring by this rule are
\[
\begin{split}
&2,3,4,5,37,38,41,42,46,57,58,61,\\
&36,37,38,41,42,46,57,58,61,62,63,77 .
\end{split}
\]
Here an entry represents the full signed class of that state. In the other direction there are exactly four pairs of protected classes capable of blocking **all** nonzero degrees, leaving every other class permitted. Their representatives are \((7,37),(13,37),(13,38),(24,38)\). No single protected class suffices. These sharp bounds apply to support restrictions; a direct construction of a missing five-comparison can use the actual attaching terms.

**Why the exhaustive test works.** For each translated staircase triangulation of the six-cube and each top flag \(F\) in it, let \(L(F)\) be the set of classes of states containing all seven vertices of \(F\). A necessary condition for nonzero degree is that every triangulation have a flag with \(L(F)\subseteq P\). Otherwise every top simplex in that triangulation lies in a protected state. Its signed fundamental chain then fills the exterior cycle integrally inside the support complex, so every cocycle in Response 29 has zero pairing, over any coefficient ring. This is stronger than arguing separately about the individual colourings in a signed sum.

The 32 origins up to reversal give 720 flags each. Start with the family containing the empty permission. At one triangulation take all unions of a retained permission with some \(L(F)\) in it, keeping just the inclusion-minimal sets. By induction the upward closure is exactly the permissions passing every processed triangulation. This leaves 120 minimal necessary sets over all 42 classes. Separately the priority solver gives 16 minimal sufficient sets. All ten necessary minima below size 20 are themselves sufficient; the solver prints their representatives, seed, axis order and degree. For each it checks the actual vertex map, properness on each exterior facet, its complete set of full-colour classes and the signed simplex count. Thus no gap between necessity and sufficiency can occur at size at most nineteen. For a positive case, Response 39 uses actual ordered comparisons on full states and proper auxiliary generators elsewhere; Response 67 transfers the comparisons throughout the permitted classes for arbitrary raw terms. All other weak inputs and any number of repeated loop factors are covered by those proofs. The finite counts concern supports and degrees.

**The four exceptional pairs.** Among the \(\binom{42}{2}\) pairs only the four displayed ones meet every priority permission. For either middle pair every one of the 720 flags at origin 2 lies in at least one of its eight named states, yielding an integral filling. For the first and last pairs use the nerve of the twelve exterior facets and eight protected states. The solver finds a six-chain modulo two, propagates integer signs from the exterior and then sums every integral five-face coefficient independently. The first pair gives 336 six-simplices (168 of either sign), the last 320 (160 of either sign). In each case the resulting boundary is exactly the oriented 64-facet dual sphere. The nerve equivalence relative to the exterior subcover from Response 31 identifies its class with the ordinary exterior boundary. Every signed colouring with proper restrictions on those eight states therefore has zero degree. For every other pair an ordinary degree-one-in-absolute-value priority witness avoids both. In particular the hypothesis of exactly two inequivalent failing classes would force their representatives to be one of these four pairs; the support-degree test cannot decide which, or whether the ring hypothesis holds.

The command below reproduces the ten lists, both bounds and all four integral certificates. The full decision at size twenty and above is open outside the explicit positive and negative certificates. [Permission and integral certificate solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_orbit_permissions.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_orbit_permissions.py
~~~

# Researcher Response 69

## A square test decides all signed state permissions, in arbitrary cube width with the common-row hypothesis

For **every subset of the 148 named states**, the following are equivalent: a signed support colouring of nonzero exterior degree exists over the integers or some modulus; an ordinary signed-priority colouring of degree \(+1\) or \(-1\) exists; and the protected states fail at least one of the three local tests below. A state carries a simplex when it contains all of its vertices.

1. Every cube vertex belongs to a protected state.
2. Every generating edge has both ends in a single protected state.
3. For every generating square, at least one of its two diagonal triangulations has both triangles carried. The two triangles may use different protected states.

This decides the cases of size twenty and above left by Response 68, as well as permissions without any symmetry condition. There are exactly **72 inclusion-minimal sufficient lists on the named states** and **16 on the signed isomorphism classes**. Equivalently, if the ring has degree-eight identity-image RC, its actual failing states cover all vertices and edges and triangulate every square in the indicated sense. After allowing the retypings and signed isomorphisms of Response 67 they must contain at least one of exactly 5,869 minimal sets: four pairs, 863 triples and 5,002 quadruples. These are minimal sets that meet all support-degree tests. The theorem does not assert that any such failure occurs.

**Why squares suffice.** More generally take any family of vertex sets in a \(k\)-cube, \(k\ge3\), and assume each cubical three-face contains a vertex common to the whole family. If the local tests hold for the protected subfamily, construct an integral chain on every face \(F\), carried both by \(F\) and by the protected sets. Use the vertex itself in degree zero, the oriented generating edge in degree one, and the two signed triangles of a successful diagonal in degree two. At higher degree the signed sum of the chosen chains on the facets of \(F\) is a cycle by cancellation at common ridges. Cone that cycle at a common vertex in \(F\): every higher face contains some three-face, hence has such a vertex. Adjoining it to any simplex of the cycle keeps the simplex inside the same protected set. The simplicial cone formula gives exactly the prescribed boundary, with repeated vertices contributing zero. This construction includes the full cube. On the exterior the resulting chain represents its fundamental class in the union of the exterior-facet simplices: it agrees at vertices with the staircase chain map, and the two maps are chain homotopic there by coning their difference inductively inside the simplex on each face. The exterior class therefore bounds integrally after the protected simplices are included. Response 29 excludes nonzero degree for any signed sum, over all the stated coefficient rings.

Conversely, colour a vertex by the first coordinate in priority order on which it differs from a seed, with a separate colour for the seed. Every exterior facet omits a colour and the reverse-priority staircase from the seed is the unique rainbow top flag, of degree \(\pm1\). For an uncovered vertex use it as seed. For an uncovered edge use one end as seed and its axis last: the seed and the last-axis colour each occur once, at the two ends. For a square with neither diagonal working, choose one uncarried triangle from each diagonal triangulation. The chosen triangles share an **edge**. Use one end of that edge as seed, put its axis last and the other square axis second to last. The two singleton colours require the shared edge and the second-to-last colour occurs exactly at the two vertices of the opposite edge. Any full-colour state must then carry one of the two uncarried triangles. Thus all protected states are proper. This proves the converse with one ordinary colouring, including for an empty protected family.

**The common vertices in every width.** Suppose \(k\ge4\) and the supports in question contain both rank-\((k-4)\) rows of the top cube tableau, including their vertices. In the union of the two rank-\(j\) rows, the vertices are precisely the binary strings with at most \(j\) changes between consecutive bits. Indeed for free set \(B\) of size \(j\), the prescribed fixed signs alternate after each free index (Response 35). In a consecutive block of \(r\) free indices with both neighbours fixed, the fixed signs differ by parity \(r\), so the number of switches is at most \(r\); the same bound holds at a one-ended block by its length. Conversely start with the first bit as row sign and free the first position of each new run. The signs then match at every fixed position. If more free indices are needed, take the largest remaining fixed indices: no earlier fixed sign is affected. A three-face fixes \(k-3\) bits; extend these by repeating the nearest preceding fixed bit and extending the first bit to the left. The resulting vertex has at most \(k-4\) switches. This proves the hypothesis throughout this family. In the six-seam inventory the intersection is exactly these 32 vertices. Only 32 vertices, 60 edges and 30 squares miss the intersection; every face of dimension at least three meets it.

**What the computation decides.** For each vertex or edge make one clause of named states which carry it. For a square, make four clauses, one for each choice of one triangle on each diagonal: the clause consists of states carrying at least one of the chosen triangles. A protected family passes all three tests precisely when it meets all clauses, by distributivity. The solver constructs all 1,216 clauses, verifies a priority witness for each failed clause, and independently recovers exactly the 72 minimal permissions from Response 17. Projecting each clause to whole-label classes and minimizing gives 16; the command prints all their representatives. For the dual enumeration, start with the empty set and, for each clause, retain the sets meeting it and augment each other set by one member of the clause, then discard strict supersets. Induction gives all minimal hitting sets and the counts above. For each surviving pair the solver constructs the actual integral chains on all 729 faces, checks every signed boundary coefficient and checks that every simplex is carried. Each resulting six-chain has 840 nonzero simplices. It also compares both common tableau rows with the bit criterion through width nine.

The finite operations classify supports. The passage to a four-term comparison on a permitted class still uses the marked occurrence proof and retyping, which apply to arbitrary inactive inputs and every finite loop power. Thus further progress on the ring itself requires information about terms or a different degree test to improve on this support criterion. [Square criteria, class lists and integral fillings](../../../tools/relative_coherence_square_carriers.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_square_carriers.py --lists
~~~

# Researcher Response 70

## Full cylinders alone test every walking attachment

Let \(n\ge1\). Assume ordinary naturality through degree \(n\) on **fully marked globular pasting arities**: the chosen action has the prescribed recursively computed faces and induced strict cylinder value. Then attaching any walking bi-invertible cell \(i_k\) to a groupoidal free CaTT algebra induces an isomorphism on based \(\pi_n\) at every old vertex. The argument allows all old generators to move. This removes the need for partial-cylinder hypotheses in this test.

In particular, if the six-seam ring satisfies identity-image RC through degree eight, **some ordinary full cylinder \(I\otimes P\), for a globular pasting arity \(P\) of height at most three, fails RC(4) over the cylinder of its full three-dimensional cell** (identity-padded if necessary). Thus a four-term comparison fails in a tensor with just one interval factor. Equivalently as a sufficient test, success on all the specified thin-head judgments in those full cylinders disproves the ring hypothesis. The argument gives an implication in this direction; the full-cylinder naturality question remains open.

**Choosing the second cap in the old algebra.** Write \(A\subset B\) for a walking pushout and \(r:B\to A\) for its usual retraction. Work on the skeleta needed to evaluate \(n\)-terms. The minus labelling is the identity. Construct a plus labelling \(F\) on the old generators with values in \(A\), simultaneously with full changes in \(A\). On objects take the same object and its weak identity as change. For an old \(d\)-generator \(x\), the plus type is obtained by substituting \(F\) on the previous declarations. Temporarily assign \(X\) of this type to the plus cap. Let \(M^-(x)\) and \(M^+(X)\) denote the two sides of the full mixed judgment, including all lower rails. Their common type is independent of \(X\) by dimension. The designated \(X\) occurs exactly once in top count: start at the plus cap and compose a fixed change at each successive cut; the last rail can itself have dimension \(d\) but is independent of \(X\). Division from Response 56 inside \(A\) supplies \(F(x)\) and an actual comparison
\[
                         M^-(x)\longrightarrow M^+(F(x)).
\]
This also supplies an inhabitant of the required plus type if none was chosen in advance. All old objects and their based identity towers still map literally to themselves under \(F\).

Take \(Fr\) for the plus labelling on \(B\). The source globe of the attachment already has a full change by evaluation in \(A\). Response 57 extends these components over the duplicate, the forward generator and its two inverse towers in the groupoidal pushout, with the chosen endpoint labellings. For precision in the truncated statement, if \(k\le n\) the retraction on the walking summand factors through its source \(k\)-disk. The plus images of any walking cancellations used to solve for an inverse of rank \(n\) are therefore already defined by the old \(k\)-disk labelling. Only the temporal and identity actions on terms of degree at most \(n\) enter those division equations. If \(k>n\) the attachment introduces no generators through degree \(n\). The strict top-occurrence check in Response 57 applies to any chosen ordinary action with the prescribed strict value. Once every generator moves, every argument term pulls back the full selection: even an iterated weak identity depends on its object. Thus actual term evaluation only ever calls the full-arity action.

**Returning to the original basepoint.** Let \(L\) be a based \(n\)-loop in \(B\) at an old object. All changes on its iterated identity poles lie in \(A\). Evaluating its mixed action gives a comparison in \(B\) from \(M^-(L)\) to \(M^+(FrL)\). The latter belongs to \(A\). In \(A\), divide this output by the one-hole expression \(M^-(X)\), with the prescribed based type for \(X\). Obtain an old based loop \(L_0\) and a comparison between its framed image and \(M^+(FrL)\). After inclusion the two framed minus images compare in \(B\); groupoidality supplies whichever direction of the old comparison is needed. Injectivity of the same one-hole division on classes in \(B\) now gives a comparison at the *unframed* based type between \(L,L_0\). This proves surjectivity. Injectivity follows from the original retraction \(r\). No cancellation of moving lower poles by syntactic equality was used. The division theorem concerns arbitrary fixed labels. Repeated variables, any stationary loop powers in the other rails, and arbitrary length of \(L\) do not affect the single occurrence of the fresh symbol.

**The thin-head consequence.** Through rank three choose the existing full actions. The full marking is generated at rank zero, so the top-slot raising and thin-head reduction of Gray Responses 20–21 apply. With the compatible lower choices described there, degree-four composition heads follow from the thin-head comparisons. The latter ask for actions on pure \(c:u\to v\) with \(u,v\) parallel three-terms of the same full strict value \(w\) on a pasting support \(P\) of height at most three. In the full cylinder the two four-dimensional faces of the action both evaluate strictly to the cylinder of \(w\): the pure copies of \(c\) are identities, the lower whiskerings of these identities remain identities, and composing them temporally leaves the strict change of \(u\) or \(v\). A five-term in this full cylinder automatically has identity strict value by dimension. If the requested comparisons all exist, the preceding theorem in degree four discharges the walking tests of Response 60. Therefore under the ring hypothesis at least one of these pairs has no five-term at all. Response 38 can be applied in its three-skeleton since the full cylinder has an acyclic one-graph, nonempty square boundaries and RC(3) there. [Raising theorem](canonical-gray-terms-r7.md#researcher-response-20) · [Elementary substitution and reversal](canonical-gray-terms-r7.md#researcher-response-21) · [Sidewise cylinder composition, Theorem 4.2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

**Checks.** In ranks one through five the solver independently forms both padded cap expressions on the full disk cylinder, checks the fresh cap's multiplicity and absence from the output type, and types full labellings with two different bracketings of a whiskered cell. In ranks two through five the returned cap has a different literal type from the input. The solver constructs actual forward and reverse comparisons between the padded caps, checks all 9, 15, 21, 27 and 33 substitution entries, and tests based powers \(0,1,3,9\) with all point rails present. Agda 2.8.0 checked both comparisons through rank four in safe mode. Surjectivity and reflection at arbitrary powers use division in the two algebras. # Researcher Response 71

## A full cylinder on a two-dimensional pasting arity must already detect the ring alternative

Let \(G\) be the fixed groupoidal walking graph of Response 63, built from the point by six walking arrows and fifteen walking two-cells. If the ring satisfies identity-image RC through degree eight, there exist a **globular pasting arity \(S\) of height at most two**, a single labelling \(I\otimes S\to G\), and parallel four-terms in the ordinary full cylinder \(I\otimes S\) whose images under this labelling have **no five-comparison in \(G\)**. Their common strict value in the cylinder is the identity on the cylinder of the full two-cell of \(S\), with identity padding for smaller height. Thus RC(4) fails already in this particular class of three-dimensional strong-Steiner presentations. It suffices to prove comparability after all such labellings into this one algebra. The ring hypothesis itself is still open.

**Collapsing strings after groupoidal evaluation.** The general construction works for a globular pasting arity \(P\) of height \(m\ge2\). At each top string of \(m\)-variables keep its first \((m-1)\)-position and collapse the string onto it. Call the resulting spatial arity \(S\). This truncates the tree, including all branches originally of smaller height. In the ordinary full cylinder, \(I\otimes S\) is the subcontext on the retained positions, their two copies and their changes. There is a literal retraction \(r:I\otimes P\to I\otimes S\) on cells. On a string \(b_0\to b_1\to\cdots\to b_l\), send each copy of \(b_j\) to the corresponding copy of \(b_0\), and \(H_{b_j}\) to \(H_{b_0}\). Send each pure copy of a spatial arrow \(y:b_{j-1}\to b_j\) to the identity of its retracted cap. The remaining mixed \(H_y\) has these identities as pure caps and \(H_{b_0}\) on both immediate disk sides. The disk-coordinate degeneracy is exactly its type: contract the padded minus endpoint to \(H_{b_0}\), then expand to the padded plus endpoint. Its strict value is the identity on \(H_{b_0}\). Thus the strict retraction is the interval tensor of the spatial string collapse. [Disk-coordinate degeneracy, with both directions](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#extracting-a-globular-cell-from-a-cylinder)

Write \(i\) for the core inclusion. For any labelling \(e:I\otimes P\to D\) into a groupoidal algebra, compare the two labellings \(e\) and \(eir\), with every core variable stationary. Process each new position \(b_j\) and arrow \(y\) as three pairs, the two pure pairs \((b_j^\pm,y^\pm)\) followed by \((H_{b_j},H_y)\). This order respects all dependencies. In either pure pair the change of the new position occurs once in the mixed equation for its arrow. With all previous labels fixed, division chooses this change and a component for the arrow, as in Response 57. For the mixed pair put a fresh variable \(X\) at the change of \(H_{b_j}\), of rank \(m+1\). The minus spatial boundary of \(H_y\) is the lower-padded \(y^-\) followed at cut \(m-1\) by \(H_{b_j}\). Its other boundary uses \(H_{b_{j-1}}\) and the lower-padded \(y^+\). Only the former uses the new mixed slot. In its change the temporal binary operation uses the change of that slot exactly once in top count; the action of the other factor and all lower frames are independent of \(X\). Hence the two sides of the mixed equation for \(H_y\) have a common type independent of \(X\), one side with one occurrence and the other with none. Divide in \(D\) to choose \(X\) and the component on \(H_y\). The boundary actions needed at this stage have depth at most one since the lowest moved variables have rank \(m-1\).

Consequently the change of **any** term of dimension \(m+1\) under these labellings can be evaluated by depth-two naturality. Given parallel terms \(T,U\) of that dimension, comparability of \(eir(T),eir(U)\) implies comparability of \(e(T),e(U)\), and conversely. Indeed the common poles below rank \(m-1\) are stationary. In the homotopy bicategory at those poles the remaining two levels of side changes are equivalences and invertible two-morphisms, since \(D\) is groupoidal. Paste the two mixed comparisons and cancel the common frames exactly as in Response 67. This proves reflection at the original literal ends in \(D\). It is a statement about evaluation into a groupoidal algebra; the spatial arrows need not be invertible in the source syntax. Neither division counts occurrences of any old label. The unique occurrence is that of the temporary new component, even if the two endpoint labellings identify variables or use arbitrarily large loop powers.

**Application to the four-loop.** Under the ring hypothesis Response 63 gives a nonzero based four-loop class in \(G\). Take the minus labelling of \(G\) to factor through its basepoint, and the plus labelling to be the identity. A map to the free point exists in declaration order by boundary lifting there. At the basepoint use its weak identity as change. Extend full changes along each walking attachment in \(G\) by Response 57. The only nonvariable source globes used in its construction are attached at previously formed one-dimensional paths. Their changes exist by the ordinary path action. For the inverse towers the required higher temporal composites and identity operations have the published cylinder composition or disk-degeneracy actions. Thus every needed generator component exists in \(G\) independently of the four-loop.

Normalize a representative of the class at its literal faces by Response 58, obtaining seven possible elementary binary heads in dimension four and heads of height at most three. Use full binary cylinder composition on binary heads, choosing the same operations on their binary poles in lower rank. Its cap and disk-face equations are literal. The other lower-rank actions exist by the usual prescribed-boundary lifting through degree three in globular-arity cylinders, with these lower choices. For each remaining four-head the still-needed value is a comparison between the two specialised four-faces described in Response 70, given by an actual labelling \(e:I\otimes P\to G\) of its arity cylinder. Binary evaluation and substitution only require an action at each occurrence; a proof in the free cylinder is unnecessary here. If all the specialised comparisons existed, the loop would have a mixed five-term. Its minus cap and every side change on its point-identity towers lie in the image of the free point. Dividing there by the plus frame and reflecting the result in \(G\), as in Response 70 with the endpoints interchanged, would make the loop compare to a loop from the point. All based four-loops of the free point are null. This contradicts the chosen class. [Cylinder composition and its cap equations, Theorem 4.2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

There is therefore an actual pair in some height-at-most-three arity cylinder which fails after the specified evaluation. At height three apply the string retraction with \(m=3\). The two retracted four-terms must still fail under \(ei\) by reflection. The spatial full three-cell retracts to the identity on the full two-cell of \(S\). The strict image of its cylinder consequently retracts to the identity on that two-cell's cylinder, by the checked disk degeneracy or by tensoring the spatial projection. At smaller height the same identity description already holds. This gives the asserted witness. Conversely any five-comparison in this three-dimensional source would remain a comparison under \(ei\), so the failed specialized judgment also excludes an ordinary raw five-term there.

**Verification.** The solver constructed retractions on all 81 height-three arities with at most six tree edges, including branching shapes. It checked all 2,973 dependent substitution entries and the literal fixed-core equations, contracting 122 spatial arrows. On every such arrow it also constructed a term of the reversed mixed-cap type. It checked all 20 distinguished occurrences in the binary temporal chart with eligible up-closed selections, and two-arrow spatial strings in ranks two, four and five. The optional certificate checks both directed cap comparisons on each of two successive rank-three arrows. The groupoidal reflection and the inference about the nonzero loop are the arguments above, rather than a search through raw four-terms. # Researcher Response 72

## The integral obstruction depends only on the labelled spatial graph

Assume identity-image RC through degree eight for the six-seam ring. Fix the descending walking graph algebra \(G\) of Response 63. The weights of the failed cylinder tests of Response 71 generate the **unit ideal of \(\mathbb Z\)**. They can be indexed by a height-at-most-two spatial arity \(S\), a pair of parallel raw four-terms of the indicated strict value in \(C(S)=I\otimes S\), and **only a labelling of the objects and arrows of \(S\) in \(G\)**. All choices for the two- and three-dimensional inputs in the cylinder give exactly the same integer. Every nonzero weight uses all six positive names on the spatial graph. In particular, for each prime some graph labelling detects the failure modulo that prime. The assertion about the ideal allows different witnesses at different primes.

**Weights at literal faces.** Let \(q:G\to P(\{0,\ldots,6\})\) be the map through the simplex star of Responses 48 and 63. Take the integral five-cocycle \(z=w-\lambda\) of Response 44. For parallel four-terms \(a,b\) of \(G\) set \(\omega(a,b)=z([qa,qb])\), using the primitive five-bracket in the unrestricted parameter. Every five-term \(H:qa\to qb\) there has \(z(N_5H)=\omega(a,b)\): the six-bracket from \(H\) to the primitive has zero evaluation on its boundary. The identity and temporal composite give \(\omega(a,a)=0\) and \(\omega(a,c)=\omega(a,b)+\omega(b,c)\). Each five-generator of \(G\) maps to a term in a rooted piece missing a positive name. Its five-count has weight zero. Substitution and additivity of top counts therefore give weight zero to every actual five-comparison in \(G\).

Write \(F_-,F_+\) for the two common frames of parallel four-terms under a mixed change of labelling. If \(h:F_-(a)\to F_+(a')\) and \(k:F_-(b)\to F_+(b')\) are actual mixed changes in \(G\), then
\[
                         \omega(a,b)=\omega(a',b').
\]
Indeed, frame the primitive brackets \(j_-=[qa,qb]\), \(j_+=[qa',qb']\) with the images of the same pads. Whiskering these five-terms with rails of rank at most four gives precisely their framed faces and retains just the primitive once in five-count. The two paths \(F_-(j_-)*_4 qk\) and \(qh *_4 F_+(j_+)\) are literally parallel in the parameter. Its six-bracket gives \(z(j_-)+z(N_5qk)=z(N_5qh)+z(j_+)\), and the two middle weights are zero. This argument also proves invariance under a common frame alone. It needs no five-comparison between \(a\) and \(b\) in \(G\).

**Freedom in all higher labels.** In *any* context of height at most three assign its objects and arrows in \(G\). Extend through rank three in declaration order: groupoidality and three-connectivity (Responses 56 and 63) give comparisons of parallel cells through rank three, by basing and reflection. Given any two extensions, leave every object and arrow stationary and mark every two- and three-generator. On each two-generator choose a three-change at its assigned faces. For a three-generator the previously chosen changes give parallel padded three-terms; choose a four-change between them. A four-term has depth at most two for this selection, so its change exists at these prescribed frames. Apply the weight equation to the changes on the two parallel four-terms. This proves independence even when the higher labels in the two extensions have different strict images, different support and arbitrary powers in their inactive arguments. The depth bound counts dimensions, and the frame coefficient is one by whiskering. Neither counts occurrences in the inactive data.

For the tests in the assertion, fix once and for all the contraction data of Response 71 from a map \(c:G\to *\hookrightarrow G\) to the identity. Choose its object rails \(h_i:0\to i\) inside the rooted piece on \(0,i\), by performing the first six walking extensions there. If the spatial graph labelling is \(d\), the cylinder graph has spatial copies \(cd,d\) and object rails \(h_{d(x)}\). Thus the previous paragraph assigns a weight to \(d\) alone, at each fixed pair of four-terms. If \(d\) belongs to a rooted piece missing one positive name, extend the entire cylinder graph inside that piece, which is itself three-connected by the walking construction. Both resulting terms and their primitive bracket after \(q\) miss that name. On such a bracket \(z=w=0\) by its facet support. Even an extension that subsequently uses all names has the same zero weight.

There is also a strict-word vanishing test. For an arbitrary cylinder graph labelling, suppose that the two boundary paths of every two-generator have the same word in the free directed graph of the generating arrows of \(G\). Lift the graph assignment to the free weak algebra on that graph alone, using the actual one-terms. Its map to the free strict category has prescribed-boundary lifting in every degree, by contraction on a labelled globular diagram. Lift the two-generators over identities of their common words. All parallel higher faces then have identical strict images and lift in turn. In particular the two evaluated four-terms admit a five-comparison there, which maps into \(G\). Independence of the extension proves zero weight. For the particular graph \((cd,d,h)\) this condition reduces to the word equations \(|h_{d(y)}|=|h_{d(x)}|\,|d(f)|\) on the spatial arrows \(f:x\to y\), in traversal order. The minus words are empty, these equations give every mixed square, and left cancellation gives equality between the two plus boundary words of every spatial two-generator. This applies to words with arbitrary repetitions. [Lifting on a free globular set](canonical-gray-terms-r7.md#researcher-response-13)

**Why the weights generate one.** Let \(I\) be the ideal generated by all the weights just described, using only pairs over the identity on the cylinder of the full spatial two-cell, identity-padded at smaller height. By Response 63 choose a based four-loop \(L\) with \(z([q1^4_0,qL])=1\). Normalize it at its literal faces by Response 58, preserving this value. Evaluate its mixed change using the same contraction data and lower actions as in Response 71, now in the unrestricted parameter after \(q\). At a thin four-head the two prescribed faces are evaluated in \(G\), since its spatial arity has height at most three and all lower actions are available there. Supply the primitive five-bracket between their images. If the spatial arity has height three, Response 71 provides mixed changes in \(G\) on *both* faces between this evaluation and its spatial string retraction. The frame equation preserves their weight. The retraction retains the spatial graph and its object rails, and the retracted strict value is the specified identity. Thus each supplied bracket has weight in \(I\), also at smaller height.

Every variable change from \(G\) has weight zero. Every ordinary binary cylinder action on the normalized loop is a raw five-term evaluated on previously assigned entries. Its five-count after substitution is a sum of the counts of its five-dimensional inputs with integer occurrence multiplicities. Lower-dimensional arguments contribute zero to that count. Induction on the normalized writing now puts the weight of the final mixed five-term in \(I\). Its minus cap and both families of side rails on the identity poles come from the point. Divide the minus cap with its frame by the plus frame inside the free point (Response 56). This gives a point loop \(L_0\) and a point comparison to its framed plus value. The point comparison has weight zero. Additivity and common-frame invariance therefore give \(\omega(L_0,L)\in I\). The point has trivial fourth group, so \(\omega(1^4_0,L_0)=0\) and \(\omega(L_0,L)=1\). This proves \(I=\mathbb Z\). In particular a vanishing proof on all the remaining spatial graph labellings would disprove the ring hypothesis.

**Verification.** The solver constructs the partial cylinder on two parallel four-generators for all seven upward-closed selections moving both four-cells and fixing ranks zero and one. It checks all dependent entries of a labelling in the unrestricted seven-name parameter. At stationary one-dimensional inputs it inserts powers 0, 1 and 7 of a primitive loop into detour paths. In each of the 21 cases it verifies the two single-slot five-counts, constructs both six-brackets on the two routes, typechecks them and compares the complete signed boundaries. For the selection moving every eligible pole, Agda checks both orientations at power zero. These are derivations in the unrestricted parameter. The cocycle is conditional on the ring hypothesis, and the ideal calculation uses the value-one class rather than a numerical evaluation of its unknown potential. [Frame and signed-boundary solver](../../../tools/relative_coherence_frame_weights.py)

~~~sh
dir=$(mktemp -d)
python3 -B tools/relative_coherence_frame_weights.py --agda "$dir/FrameWeights.agda"
agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i "$dir" "$dir/FrameWeights.agda"
~~~
